# A01 — AUDIT: System orchestration, startup, runtime and shutdown

**Status:** DRAFT — the AUDIT of topic A01 (Document 04, section 4, step 6); passed by ChatGPT's review of 05-Nov-2026 (step 7); in the issue discussion with Rama (step 8)
**Date:** 2026-11-05
**Revision history:** First version, 2026-11-05. Corrected, 2026-11-05: after an independent check of the statements against the code and the sources; and after a second-pass check of those corrections; revised, 2026-11-05: the three operations documents read and the dashboard captures checked (ChatGPT's AUDIT review, section 6); revised, 2026-11-05: the start-limit wording qualified after ChatGPT's review of the first issue-discussion records (note 14).
**Purpose and scope:** The comparison of what the legacy code actually does with what was intended, for every A01 area and flow of the Master System Investigation Index: the fifteen A01 paths and the startup, runtime and shutdown flows they implement, at the fixed baseline. It re-checks the STUDY's 41 concerns, audits the A01 paths beyond them, records each result (MATCH, ISSUE or UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED) with its intent source and code evidence, and reviews the STUDY's 24 questions for Rama; it decides nothing.
**Source:** trading-system-prod-snapshot, branch `main`, commit `970aabf3c78d777d9d34549e021f7c8e756c7bdb` (Source Inventory, SRC-10), read from the local read-only clone on 05-Nov-2026; nothing was run.
**Related documents:** Documents 04, 06, 07, 08 and 10; the Master System Investigation Index; the Decision Register, D-07, D-21 and D-23 to D-29; the A01 STUDY (`A01_STUDY_2026-11-04.md`, at commit `36184f01446f6f156c7092611d6b35d16df44aa2`) and its evidence; `evidence/A01_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-05.md`.

## 1. Purpose, scope, exclusions, baseline and method

### 1.1 Purpose

This is the AUDIT of topic A01 (Document 04, section 4, step 6; Document 07, section 5). For every A01 area and flow it compares the intended behaviour, taken from every admitted source that states or implies it (Document 04, section 5.1), with the actual behaviour of the code at the baseline, and records the result with its evidence. The STUDY's statements were treated as leads to re-check, not as conclusions. The AUDIT decides nothing: every issue is discussed with Rama after ChatGPT's review (Document 04, section 5.5), and the Audit Findings Register and the Open Issues Register are filled with the complete topic package (Document 05, section 11.1), not from this draft. No finding here is a V2 requirement or a repair proposal.

### 1.2 Scope and paths

- Index entry A01: process entry, orchestration, lifecycle, runtime loop, startup checks, shutdown, time authority and top-level wiring.
- The fifteen paths: `main.py` (process entry, CLI, orchestration and wiring only); `utils/startup_checks.py`; `utils/instance_lock.py`; `utils/holiday_guard.py`; `core/time_authority.py`; `core/market_windows.py`; `core/events.py`; `core/ids.py`; `core/exceptions.py`; `core/constants.py`; `core/account_registry.py`; `core/__init__.py`; `utils/__init__.py`; `scripts/check_tz.sh`; `scripts/ist_now.sh`.
- Document 08 coverage (section 3), as limited by ChatGPT's AUDIT direction of 05-Nov-2026: item 1 (system overview and architecture); item 3 (startup, runtime and scheduler), limited to A01's startup and runtime orchestration; item 20 (failure, recovery and operational behaviour), limited to A01's startup and shutdown boundary.

### 1.3 Exclusions

- Other topics' internals (A02 to A20). A01 records the boundary (the call site, what crosses it, the owner) and hands the finding to the owner (section 8); it does not re-audit the owner's code. Inside `main.py`, the failure callbacks are A13's code and the historical-data call sites A05's; A01 audits their wiring only.
- Paper-only paths, except where a shared branch had to be read to establish Live behaviour (Document 08, section 2). They are marked where they branch and not traced.
- Any repair, tuning or execution of the legacy system, and any V2 design, requirement or recommendation.
- The RECONCILED TOPIC RECORD and the register updates, which follow the review.

### 1.4 Baseline

Every code citation is to trading-system-prod-snapshot, branch `main`, commit `970aabf3c78d777d9d34549e021f7c8e756c7bdb` (Decision Register, D-21), the clone's HEAD with a clean working tree. A line reference without a path is to `main.py`. The changes made to A01 paths after the last recorded deployment (`7d4970a`) are listed in the STUDY, section 1.8; whether production ran them is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (M-41). Configured values are the values in the files at the baseline; the values in force at runtime are UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.

### 1.5 Method

- The audit was split into five parts, done in parallel by AI helpers under the same read-only rules: the intent sources (the intent-sources part); `main.py` entry, CLI, time and the lock (the entry part, items B-nn); the startup body and the startup checks (the startup part, items S-nn); the runtime, workers, signals and shutdown (the runtime part, items W-nn); and the other A01 modules (the modules part, items M-nn). Their results were merged in two fragments (entry, time, lock and modules; startup, runtime and shutdown), assembled into this document by Claude and checked independently. Where the fragments treated like cases differently, the rule of section 1.6 and, where needed, a fresh read of the code at the baseline decided; every such change is listed in Appendix A, notes.
- Each item was re-read at the baseline with `git show` and `git grep` (callers, readers, writers, setters, overrides and consumers, not only the obvious path), with `git log -S`, `git blame` and `git show -s` for its history, and, where stated, with Python's `ast` and `csv` modules on text copies, in scripts kept outside the clone and run with `python3 -I`. Tests were read as documents only.
- Intended behaviour was sought in every admitted source: configuration (`config/`; secret values never copied), deployment declarations (`deploy/`; a declaration is not proof of installation), the legacy documents, the A01 code's comments, docstrings, names and log and alert texts, tests (as documents), commit messages, the existing technical records (HISTORICAL; they describe the testing copy) and the statements that legacy documents attribute to Rama (HISTORICAL; Rama has made no statement to this project about A01 behaviour). The inventory is the evidence file `evidence/A01_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-05.md` (section 3). The three `.docx` operations documents in `docs/web_claude/06_live_operations/`, which text search cannot read, were read in full on 05-Nov-2026 after ChatGPT's review of this AUDIT (its section 6), from text extracted read-only with python-docx outside the clone, and the 34 dashboard captures were checked by name and index, three of them opened (evidence file, sections 1.1 and 6.3; Appendix A, note 13). Where sources conflict, both are recorded and none is chosen; where no source states the intent, the record says "intended behaviour not established" and audits the code on its own (Document 04, section 5.3).
- Nothing was run: no legacy code, test, script, service, job or broker call. Nothing in the clone, the legacy records or the project registers was written.

### 1.6 Record fields, results, classifications and the KNOWN DEFECT rule

- Record fields (Document 04, section 5.4): area and flow; intended behaviour and its source (path and lines or key, with its date), or "not established"; the actual code path (path, function or class, lines); the actual behaviour and the effective value; the evidence; the result and its classification; the owner topic; for a STUDY concern, its disposition (CONFIRMED, NARROWED, SPLIT, MERGED or REJECTED, with the reason; section 4); and whether Rama's intent or decision is needed. Issues and UNKNOWN items are written as blocks (sections 6.1 and 6.2); matches as table rows with the same fields (section 6.3). Matches are recorded as fully as issues, so that coverage can be checked.
- Results: MATCH (the code does what the sources intend); ISSUE (a difference from intended behaviour, a conflict between sources that the code resolves one way, or a concern on which intent is not established); UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (the answer needs runtime evidence that does not survive, or the evidence lies outside the admitted repository, for example a third-party library's source). An item with a MATCH part and an ISSUE part is counted as ISSUE. An item is a MATCH with a recorded conflict only where a later-dated source, or a source that names the earlier one, replaces the conflicting source; the row cites it. Otherwise a conflict between sources that the code resolves one way is an ISSUE. A finding inside another topic's code is recorded as a handoff (section 8) and is not counted as an A01 ISSUE; A01 records and counts its own part (the wiring, the call site, the constant, the text in an A01 file). A finding recorded by two items is counted once; the second item is a pointer.
- Classifications (Document 04, section 6): FACT, INTERPRETATION, HISTORICAL, KNOWN DEFECT, OPEN QUESTION and UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED. Semantics of Python, the standard library, the kernel, SQLite and systemd are INTERPRETATION (their installed versions are unknown); FACT is what the code, configuration or document text shows.
- The KNOWN DEFECT rule, applied in the same way to every item of both fragments. An item is a KNOWN DEFECT only when all four conditions hold:
  - (a) the code implements a mechanism for the case: a handler, a check, a comparison, a value or a branch;
  - (b) by the language semantics that mechanism fails for certain whenever the case occurs (for example a name that is never bound, an exception that no handler on the path catches, or a comparison or type that can never give the intended result);
  - (c) the case is one that the code itself or an intent source says it handles, and it can arise under the documented set-up: an IST-configured host is the documented set-up, so a failure that needs a non-IST host clock does not qualify, while a malformed input that a validation step exists to reject does qualify. The documented set-up is an IST-configured host running the documented options, flags and bypasses (for example `--resume`, `--dry-run`, `--status`, `TS_IGNORE_MARKET_WINDOW=1`) with the committed input or a malformed input that a validation step exists to reject; only cases that need a non-IST host clock, an undocumented option, or an input that no validation exists for are excluded;
  - (d) the outcome contradicts every intent source found. For exit codes: where the exit list (the module docstring L11 to L20 and SU15) has both a general entry (for example "1 TradingSystemError" or "2 unexpected exception") and an entry that names the case, the named entry states the intent (INTERPRETATION, applied alike to C2, C9 and S-39), so a general entry that would cover the outcome does not make condition (d) fail.
- A requirement that the code simply does not implement, a case reachable only outside the documented set-up, a comment mismatch, a design concern or a runtime risk is an ISSUE, classified FACT, INTERPRETATION or OPEN QUESTION. Whether a KNOWN DEFECT occurred in production is always UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED. Each KNOWN DEFECT record, and each record whose KNOWN DEFECT was considered and not given, states which conditions hold and which fails; Appendix A, notes, lists every item whose classification changed, with the reason.

### 1.7 Identifiers and conventions

- C1 to C41 are the STUDY's concerns (its section 12.2), with this AUDIT's splits C7a to C7d, C10a to C10g, C21a to C21c, C24a and C24b, C36a to C36e and C38a and C38b; C10f is merged into C35, and C39 is split into items of the modules part and B-33 and B-38. B-nn, S-nn, W-nn and M-nn are this AUDIT's other items. They are not the legacy labels B1, B5, B-1, S1, S4, W0, M-C1, M-C8 or M-K4 of `main.py` comments and legacy documents, and the security-audit item C-2 is not STUDY concern C2. R1 to R24 are the STUDY's questions for Rama (its section 14); "records-check R1-nn" and "records-check R3-nn" are result IDs of the STUDY's records-check evidence file.
- IS-, IC-, CD- and RS-numbers are those of `evidence/A01_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-05.md` (the evidence file): its intent sources by area, its conflicts between sources (IC-01 to IC-76; not the legacy labels IC1 to IC15 of Module 38), its statements that only the code contradicts (CD-01 to CD-26) and its statements attributed to Rama or the operator (RS-01 to RS-16). Conflicts found by the fragments that the evidence file does not list are described where they arise and listed in section 3.4.
- The short names of documents (LOCKED-YAML, SPEC, M33, M34, M41, M41C, M38, M25, RDISC, STEP3, STEP4A, FIX3, FRULES, PRULES, PRELIVE, MEGA, AUDIT-14JUN, AUDIT-04JUL, SECAUDIT-02JUL, SMAP, CTI, CTD, UNIT, TW) are those of the evidence file, section 1.5; "module_33", "module_34" and "module_41" are M33, M34 and M41, and "record 03" is the existing technical record `03_STARTUP_RUNTIME_AND_SCHEDULER.md` (SRC-42). RUNBOOK, PLAYBOOK and PLAN are the commands runbook, the paper-session playbook and the paper-to-live transition plan of 18-Apr-2026 in `docs/web_claude/06_live_operations/` (HISTORICAL), and OPSOVERVIEW is that folder's index; their locations are written "P012" (the 12th body paragraph in document order) or "T5R02" (table 5, row 2, in document order), with the heading the item sits under (evidence file, section 1.5). `docs/RUNBOOK.md` is always written with its path.
- Security: credential values, tokens, account identifiers, IP addresses, e-mail addresses and absolute machine paths are never written; per-account variable names are written `ZERODHA_API_KEY_<account-ID>`, and passages that hold such material are cited by line only (evidence file, section 6.4). Legacy crash-test scenario IDs are written with a hyphen (CT-0nn).
- Line references are written "L123" or "L123 to L130"; dates as 2026-11-05 or 05-Nov-2026.

### 1.8 Totals

- 214 items were audited: 56 concern items and 158 other items (the split parents C7, C10, C21, C24, C36, C38 and C39, and the merged items below, are not counted). Recounted from the records after the follow-up on the operations documents (Appendix A, note 13): no item was added, merged or removed, and no result or classification count changed.
- MATCH 101; ISSUE 107; UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED 4 (B-40, B-41, M-41, W-24); two concern items are counted in none of the three: C21b, REJECTED as not material, and C38b, HANDED OFF to the script's owner (not an A01 path). Other rejections do not change the counts: C26 was REJECTED as an issue and is counted as MATCH, and within C39 the stale line references (M-27), the holiday-guard loop and the `check_tz.sh` point were rejected as issues.
- KNOWN DEFECT, within the ISSUE count: 8 items: C1 (part (a)), C2 (parts (i) and (ii), the live token-file and `accounts.csv` paths), C3, C9, C10g (latent), M-33, S-11 (a configuration change lost by `--dry-run` or a failed boot) and S-39 (the `UnicodeDecodeError` sub-case). C7c and M-38 are ISSUE / FACT under the rule of section 1.6 (Appendix A, notes).
- UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED components of three counted items are recorded in section 6.2: the shutdown durations (C18), the boot durations (C32) and the concurrent use of one broker client (C38a).
- Merged into another item and counted there: C10f into C35; S-09 into B-15; S-20 into C1; S-47 into C10g; W-01 and W-02 into C14; W-05 into C19 and C20; W-06 into C18; W-20 into C34; W-21 into C19; M-10 into C19. Parts counted once, with a pointer in the other item: C29 (f) to M-07; C40's `utils/startup_checks.py` L799 to L806 point to S-45; M-39's fixed path to M-37; C21a's aborting cancels to C17. Handed off and not counted as A01 ISSUEs: the internals behind C22 and C27 (A01's own part of each is a MATCH), C23 (e) and (g) (the texts in the `main.py` failure callbacks, A13), C29 (c), M-11's drift-handler part (A09), M-28's site postures (their owners), M-30's flatten behaviour (A13), C38b.
- R1 to R24 (section 9): ANSWERED BY EVIDENCE 4 (R3, R14, R18, R19); retained as OPEN QUESTION 20, of which 13 narrowed by the evidence to a remaining part (R1, R2, R4, R5, R7, R8, R9, R10, R13, R15, R17, R21, R22) and 7 wholly open (R6, R11, R12, R16, R20, R23, R24); NO LONGER MATERIAL 0.

### 2.3 The STUDY's concerns C1 to C41

| C | Result | Where recorded |

## 3. Intended-behaviour sources and conflicts

The full inventory is the evidence file `evidence/A01_AUDIT_INTENT_SOURCES_2026-11-05.md`: the sources by A01 area with their dates and kinds (its section 2), the origin of the requirement labels (section 3), the conflicts between sources (section 4.1), the statements that only the code contradicts (section 4.2), the statements attributed to Rama or the operator (section 5) and the material not read (section 6). This section summarises it. Every source is HISTORICAL documentary evidence of intent at its date; none proves what was installed, ran or was in force (runtime UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED). Rama has made no statement to this project about A01.

### 3.1 Sources by area and kind

The evidence file has 278 entries (263 before the follow-up on the operations documents, which added 15: IS-1.19, IS-1.20, IS-2.45, IS-3.15, IS-4.26, IS-4.27, IS-5.22, IS-5.23, IS-8.13, IS-9.14, IS-9.15, IS-11.23, IS-11.24, IS-12.15 and IS-12.16); an entry may group several passages (a test entry often covers several test files), an entry with two kinds counts under both, and three areas also have a "no source found" row. INTENT is a statement of intent (a decision, requirement, instruction, fix direction or procedure); DESCRIPTION a description of how the system works or worked; DECLARATION a configuration value, unit directive, docstring, comment, name, log or alert text; TEST a test expectation or plan (documentary only).

| Area | Entries | INTENT | DESCRIPTION | DECLARATION | TEST |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 entry and CLI | 20 | 11 | 4 | 4 | 1 |
| 2 early guards, clock, NTP | 45 | 18 | 9 | 20 | 2 |
| 3 instance lock | 15 | 4 | 5 | 6 | 1 |
| 4 startup phases, checks, scenario, session row | 27 | 12 | 10 | 6 | 2 |
| 5 token, broker, account registry | 23 | 10 | 5 | 8 | 2 |
| 6 configuration boundary | 15 | 6 | 2 | 7 | 1 |
| 7 construction and wiring | 14 | 8 | 1 | 4 | 1 |
| 8 startup reconcile and kill checks | 13 | 6 | 4 | 3 | 1 |
| 9 workers, runtime loop, supervision | 15 | 6 | 8 | 3 | 1 |
| 10 pre-alert, 09:15 re-sync, self-exit | 20 | 5 | 6 | 10 | 4 |
| 11 signals and shutdown | 24 | 8 | 9 | 9 | 2 |
| 12 exit codes and the unit | 16 | 7 | 6 | 4 | 2 |
| 13 events, ids, exceptions, constants, account registry | 25 | 9 | 2 | 18 | 6 |
| 14 the two scripts | 6 | 2 | 0 | 3 | 1 |

Totals of kind mentions: INTENT 112, DESCRIPTION 71, DECLARATION 105, TEST 27 (after the evidence file's correction of two kind labels, IS-6.07 and IS-10.03; the follow-up added INTENT 13 and DESCRIPTION 9). The evidence file records 76 conflicts between sources (IC-01 to IC-76; IC-74 to IC-76 added at the follow-up), 26 statements that only the code contradicts (CD-01 to CD-26) and 16 statements attributed to Rama or the operator (RS-01 to RS-16; the operations documents attribute no A01 statement to Rama or the operator).

### 3.2 The main sources

- `docs/locked_decisions.yaml` (2026-07-22): G4 clock, G5a and G5c scenarios, G9 bus, P1 times, P17 pre-flight, CL, EV, E, ID, RC14. No `main_module` or `startup_module` section.
- Module instructions of 2026-04-17: M34 (MAIN1 to MAIN25), M33 (SC1 to SC15), M41 and M41C (two different SU1 to SU20), M38 (IC5 accounts), M25 (reconciler); SPEC `docs/v2_design_spec.md` section 4.
- The A01 code's own docstrings and comments (FIX-nnn blocks in `main.py`; X5 lock docstring; holiday guard; time authority; startup checks), dated by blame.
- Declarations outside A01: the unit (A17) and the token watcher (A04), with the unit's commit history (316b335, ccde039, 398dbae, ebfe780, 2f67bb8) and STEP3/STEP4A.
- Later decision records: headless guarantee 2026-06-20 (`capital/kill_switch.py` L315 to L328), S4 degrade rule 2026-07-27, kill-ladder decision 11 (2026-07-28), Q4 delivery decision (2026-07-30), EOD gate "Option A" (2026-08-25); operations docs (SMAP, `docs/RUNBOOK.md`, incident response, disaster recovery, CTI, CTD); Records 01 and 03 (testing copy).
- The operations documents of 18-Apr-2026 (HISTORICAL operator procedure, read at the follow-up of 05-Nov-2026): RUNBOOK, PLAYBOOK and PLAN in `docs/web_claude/06_live_operations/` (commit `37f6ba5`), with the folder's index OPSOVERVIEW: the hand-run interactive launch of the first paper days and the restarts that relaunch it; the manual checks before the launch (token dated today, service stopped, port 5000, holiday, kill state, disk); the readiness signals within about 10 s; the clean stop by one Ctrl+C within 30 s; the soft and hard kill procedures; the switch to systemd from the second day (08:45 or 09:00); the restart drill. Several of their expected texts and commands match neither the code of their date nor the baseline (evidence file, section 6.3).

### 3.3 The origin of the labels

- MAIN from M34, SC from M33, SU from M41 and M41C (they differ at SU1, SU6, SU10, SU12, SU15, SU17, SU19, SU20); all say "locked" in register sections that do not exist.
- G, Q1 to Q3, P, E, EV1 to EV6, CL, L (looks like line refs: write "decision L8"), ID, RC, EOD, S from LOCKED-YAML; EV7 to EV9 only in `core/events.py`; AR only in `core/account_registry.py`; DH, CV, A1 to A5, IC1 to IC15 in their own files or M38.
- FIX-nnn has no register (commit subjects; FIX-081 has none); Fnn from AUDIT-14JUN; H-n has two numberings (April PRELIVE; July H-11); BL-n PRELIVE; M-xn AUDIT-04JUL. Not defined: lettered Q4, T, NI, BUILD 1 #12, ledger rows.
- Reused labels: RC13/RC14 (YAML against M25), S1/S4, G5, Q4, E4, B5 (twice in `main.py`), X8, AR, C-n. The AUDIT's C, R, B-, S-, W-, M- and IC- forms resemble legacy labels (evidence file, section 3.2).

Additions to the evidence file's list, found by the fragments:

- Addition 1. The exit code for invalid strategy configurations at boot: SPEC C.1 (`docs/v2_design_spec.md` L645 to L659; 2026-04-17) says alert and exit(1); MAIN7 (M34; 2026-04-17), CT-087 (CTD L1164 to L1174; 2026-06-11) and the code (L2484 to L2502) give exit 3: S-18.

- Addition 4. A second source for CD-14 (the order claims of two check docstrings): commit `c303e04` (FIX-169 F34) wires `check_db_permissions` into the aggregate runner, which runs after the store is opened, while its docstring says it runs before the store is opened: S-51.
- Addition 5 (an intent source, not a conflict). `docs/web_claude/03_audit_responses/deep_system_audit_2026-04-24_closure.md` item 6.5 (L150 to L153; file 2026-04-25) directs the webhook server's `threads=8` and `connection_limit=100`; it is not in the evidence file's inventory and is used for C36e.
- Addition 6 (found at the second-pass check). The exit code for a missing or invalid `accounts.csv` at a live start: the exit list's entry that names configuration, 5 ("invalid args / config", `main.py` L17; SU15, M41 L335, M41C L298), against 3 (the handler written for the file, L2420 to L2423; IC13, which makes the file a blocking required file, `utils/startup_checks.py` L842); the code gives 1 in live: C2 part (ii), R2.

### 3.5 Statements that only the code contradicts, and statements attributed to Rama

The 26 statements that only the code contradicts (CD-01 to CD-26) are listed in the evidence file, section 4.2, and cited in the items they bear on. The statements that legacy documents attribute to Rama or the operator are HISTORICAL documentary evidence; none is a statement to this project:

- RS-01 M41 L5 to L40 (2026-04-17): Rama's locked decisions: manual browser login; paper capital ignored in live; `--interactive` for PC and VM live, headless default; holiday check first, no logs. Bears on: C1, C10, C39, R1.
- RS-02 M41C L14 to L54 (2026-04-17): Rama deferred chaos tests; wants five accounts with paper capital; wants `--interactive` with banner. Bears on: C1, C39, B-17, R1.
- RS-03 RDISC L5 to L27, L210 to L236 (2026-04-17): "Rama's proposed flow" (holiday refusal, no logs, token mismatch blocks); answers not recorded. Bears on: C1, C10, R1, R2.
- RS-04 STEP3 L37 to L40 (2026-04-18): live is "Rama's manual override when ready". Bears on: B-40, R1.
- RS-05 LOCKED-YAML P1 L223, SPEC L89: the owner specified 09:30 to 13:30 and raised P1 to P18 (no hard-coded times; P17). Bears on: C11, C23, C32, R17.
- RS-06 `capital/kill_switch.py` L318 to L325 (lines 2026-06-20): headless guarantee, every prior-day kill cleared at boot. Bears on: C7b, S-05, R19.
- RS-07 decision 11 L3, L7, L21 to L23, L67 to L69 (2026-07-28): leave the ladder as is; keep ₹1,000 and ₹2,500; his SOFT/HARD descriptions accurate. Bears on: C22, C25, R13, R16.
- RS-08 Q4 decision L25 to L31 (2026-07-30) and `core/constants.py` L12 to L16: HARD_KILL flattens only MIS; CNC survives. Bears on: C26, C39, M-27.
- RS-09 `main.py` L1078 to L1086 and `02a2a6b` (2026-08-25): Option A, a delivery carry must not hold the service. Bears on: C26, W-16.
- RS-10 MASTER_PENDING L919 to L937 (rulings R1 to R5, 02-Aug): no A01 behaviour
- RS-11 MASTER_PENDING L24, L28; stop procedures 05-Aug and 06-Aug: stops authorised and run by Rama; 05-Aug reason authored by ChatGPT, adopted by Rama. Bears on: C21, C26.
- RS-12 service-window build record L3 to L5, L170, L266 (2026-07-25): the 17:35 instruction (issuer not named); the error-detail helper "Rama's to reject" (corrected below); check alive at 17:10. Bears on: C24, B-18, B-20, W-16.
- RS-13 deployment evidence 14-Jul L5: "Rama's explicit GO" for the Q4 boot guards. Bears on: S-33, S-49.
- RS-14 `main.py` L1066 to L1072 (lines 2026-07-16): a flatten past the 15 s grace "is the operator's call" (a role assignment in code text, not a statement attributed to Rama or the operator). Bears on: C17, C18, R15.
- RS-15 `main.py` L2291, M34 MAIN6: `--resume` recorded as `resumed_by="operator"` (a role assignment in code text, not a statement attributed to Rama or the operator). Bears on: S-06, R19.
- RS-16 SMAP L895 to L904 (18-Jun entry): the operator cleared the emergency kill with `--resume`; later resumes directed to `resume.sh`. Bears on: C31, R19.
- Excluded (not A01): FRULES L101; SMAP L735 to L736; BOOT_PROOF L7; `2f67bb8` "Rama's call"; MASTER_PENDING L350 to L372 (07-Aug rulings).
- No entry for the operations documents of 18-Apr-2026 (RUNBOOK, PLAYBOOK, PLAN, OPSOVERVIEW): they are procedures written for the operator, and a procedure for the operator is not a statement attributed to him; none attributes an A01 statement to Rama or the operator (evidence file, section 5).

## 6. Issue records, match records and UNKNOWN items

Every record carries the fields of Document 04, section 5.4 (section 1.6); the disposition of each STUDY concern is in section 4. Records are in C order for the concerns and by area (section 5) for the other items. A line reference without a path is to `main.py` at the baseline.

### 6.1 Issue records

#### C1 — The interactive start reads `today`, which `_main_locked` never binds

- **Area and flow:** process entry and CLI; the `--interactive` start path (AUDIT direction flow 1).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** `--interactive` runs an account selector, a login and a mode picker, then starts the system: help text L210 ("Interactive startup: account selector, login, mode picker (SU4)"; `main.py`, 2026-09-10); module docstring L8 to L9; SU4, SU7 (expected output "Today: <date> — Trading day") and SU13 (welcome banner, then the normal phases) in `docs/web_claude/01_module_instructions/module_41_interactive_startup.txt` L72 to L78, L112 to L142, L290 to L318 (2026-04-17); HISTORICAL, recorded in the same file as Rama's locked decisions, L21 to L32 (RS-01): one `main.py` with `--interactive`, used for the PC operator session and, for live on the VM, run by hand over SSH before the system goes headless; `docs/disaster_recovery.md` (2026-08-02) prescribes `main.py --interactive` after a DB re-creation (L113), as token "Option B" (L134 to L136) and as the VM-rebuild start test (L227); `docs/01_system_architecture.md` L14, L18 (2026-06-03) lists the flag. HISTORICAL operator procedure of 18-Apr-2026 (`docs/web_claude/06_live_operations/`, commit `37f6ba5`; IS-1.19): the first paper days were to be launched by hand on the VM with `main.py --interactive` (RUNBOOK T5R02, section 2; PLAYBOOK T2R08 and P024, section 3.1), bypassing systemd "on purpose" so that the pre-flight checks could be watched, with systemd taking over from the next day (PLAYBOOK T4R01); the mid-day restart after a new token (RUNBOOK T18R01, section 6.3) and the full restart (RUNBOOK T19R01, section 6.4) relaunch it the same way. No source says the path should fail.
- **Actual code path:** `main.py` `main()` L2085 to L2190 binds `today = date.today()` at L2100 as a local and calls `_main_locked(args, config_dir)` at L2188 (its only caller); `_main_locked` L2193 to L4278 reads `today` at L2534 (f-string), L2541 (argument to `_interactive_check_or_login`) and L4209 (banner).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT (own AST scan, 05-Nov-2026, of a byte-identical text copy): `_main_locked` has the parameters `args` and `config_dir`, no `global` statement and no binding of `today` (no assignment, walrus, `except ... as` or nested binding); the module binds no `today` and has no star import or `globals()` use. INTERPRETATION (Python name resolution): the compiler treats `today` in `_main_locked` as a global name; the lookup fails and raises `NameError` at L2534, after the startup-check aggregate (L2451) has run and before `_interactive_select_account` (L2536). Nothing in `_main_locked` wraps L2528 to L2542; `main()`'s `try/finally` (L2187 to L2190) releases the lock; the guard's `except Exception` (L4291 to L4293) logs CRITICAL `unexpected_error` and exits 2. So the interactive exits 5, 7 and 8 (L1898, L1901, L1946, L1858) and the banner (L4209) cannot be reached through `main()`. FACT: with `--interactive --dry-run` the dry-run returns first (L2476 to L2482). Part (b), FACT for the order and INTERPRETATION for the effect: in live, the token pre-load, the KiteConnect client and the adapter are built from the pre-interactive `--mode` and the primary account's key and token (L2352 to L2411; `paper_mode=is_paper` at L2390) before the interactive account and mode choice (L2536 to L2542); the EF-4 comment (L2578 to L2582) recomputes `is_paper`, but the adapter keeps the earlier mode. Part (b) cannot run at the baseline because of part (a).
- **Evidence:** read L2085 to L2600 and L4120 to L4215; own AST scan of every binding and read of `today` in `main` and `_main_locked` and of the callers of `_main_locked` (one, L2188); text search for `globals()` and star imports. Tests (documents only): `tests/unit/test_interactive_startup.py` (2026-07-25) drives the helpers (L163 to L250) and the holiday and window paths of `main()` (L70 to L156); no test passes `--interactive` to `main()`. HISTORICAL: commit `ab2c6d2` (05-May-2026) moved the body into `_main_locked` and left `today` in `main()`; at `37f6ba5` (18-Apr-2026, the date of the operations documents) `main()` itself bound `today` (L574 of that commit) and held the interactive branch (L751 to L764), so the April launch procedure could run when it was written; `docs/audit/BOOT_PROOF_75e637c_26-Aug-2026.md` L115 (2026-08-26) records the live service's command line without `--interactive`.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. (a) KNOWN DEFECT. The four conditions of section 1.6 hold: (a) the code has a branch for the case, the interactive path (L2528 to L2542); (b) `_main_locked` never binds `today`, so by Python's name resolution the `NameError` at L2534 is certain whenever the branch runs; (c) `--interactive` is an option the code offers (help L210) and the documents prescribe (RS-01; `docs/disaster_recovery.md` L113, L134 to L136, L227; the April operations documents, RUNBOOK T5R02, T18R01 and T19R01 and PLAYBOOK T2R08), and it runs on the documented set-up; (d) the outcome (exit 2, no interactive start) contradicts every intent source cited, and no source supports it. Whether it occurred in production: UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED. (b) INTERPRETATION (latent).
- **Owner:** A01 (CLI and wiring); the login flow (`run_login_flow`) and the token lifecycle are A04's.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify. R1 is narrowed by the operations documents: the interactive launch was the planned procedure for the first paper days of April 2026, when it could run, and the `today` binding broke on 05-May-2026 at `ab2c6d2`; whether the path was used after May 2026 bears on impact only and can wait.

#### C2 — The live pre-load's exception handling

- **Area and flow:** area 5 (token, broker session and account registry at startup); Phase 0d, the live pre-load before the Kite client is built (A01 wiring; A04 boundary `load_token`; `core/account_registry.py` at boot).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** exit 6, with the "Token file missing" message, for a missing or invalid token at a live start: `main.py` module docstring L18 ("6 token missing or expired (non-interactive live)"; file 2026-09-10); the handler itself (L2364 to L2370: CRITICAL "Token file missing. Run: python scripts/zerodha_login.py ..." and `return 6`); `_invalidate_token` docstring L143 to L147 and the H-11 comment L126 to L133; M41 SU5 (`docs/web_claude/01_module_instructions/module_41_interactive_startup.txt` L80 to L89), SU14 (L320 to L327) and SU15 (L329 to L338); M41C SU14 (`consolidated_module_41_with_context.txt` L288 to L290) and SU15 (L292 to L301: "6 — token missing or expired (NEW)"), all 2026-04-17; RDISC Question F (`docs/web_claude/03_audit_responses/startup_flow_redesign_discussion.txt` L132 to L143; 2026-04-17); `docs/01_system_architecture.md` L172 (2026-06-03); CTI P.2 (`docs/crash_test_info_04jun2026.md` L844 to L846; 2026-06-11: "Manual required: ... exit 6 (token missing in live mode)"); `tests/unit/test_h11_invalidate_token_path.py` L8 to L15 (2026-07-06); HISTORICAL: commit `7fc06ff` (12-Jun-2026, FIX-163: "Falls back to exit code 6 (no restart) on missing token or credential") and existing record 03 L174 and L194. SPEC C.2 (`docs/v2_design_spec.md` L661 to L665; 2026-04-17) gives exit(1) for an expired token (IC-39); SU15, of the same date, marks 6 as new, which INTERPRETATION reads as extending the codes. The loader's contract: M41C SU17 (L310 to L315): `load_token` "Parse JSON or return None on any error". For `accounts.csv`: the full load's handler returns 3 (L2420 to L2423); the pre-read is "only to alias the key" (comment L2355 to L2357); IC13 makes the file a blocking required file (`utils/startup_checks.py` L842; exit 3 through the aggregate); HISTORICAL record claim records-check R3-40 (3); the exit list names configuration in "5 invalid args / config / missing env var" (docstring L17; SU15, M41 L335, M41C L298), which Phase 0b applies to a configuration-load failure (`return 5`, L2198 to L2201); its general entry "1 TradingSystemError" (docstring L13; SU15, M41 L331, M41C L294) would cover the exception classes raised, but under section 1.6's reading of exit-code sources it does not state the intent for a case that a named entry covers; the two named sources conflict with each other (5 against 3; section 3.4, addition 6). No source states exit 2 for a token problem or an `accounts.csv` problem, or a difference between live and paper; only the general entry covers exit 1 for an `accounts.csv` problem. For a missing credential variable the sources conflict (IC-40): 5 in the docstring L17, in M41C SU10 (L190 to L193: "If missing: print error with env var name, exit 5") and in M41C SU15 L298; 6 in the code (L2371 to L2374), commit `7fc06ff` and record 03 L174. HISTORICAL operator procedure of 18-Apr-2026 (A04 boundary; IS-5.22, IS-5.23): the token is made on the PC each morning, copied to the VM and checked by hand to carry today's date before the launch (RUNBOOK T4R03, section 1.2; PLAYBOOK T3R02, Gate A), and a token lost during the day is replaced and the process restarted (RUNBOOK T18R01; PLAYBOOK P089 to P093); these documents state no exit code, so they leave this record's result unchanged.
- **Actual code path:** `main.py` `_main_locked` L2352 to L2375 (the branch `args.mode != "paper"`; `--mode` accepts only `paper` or `live`, L184 to L187): `AccountRegistry.load` (L2359; `core/account_registry.py` L91 to L211), `primary()` (L2360), the API-key alias (L2361), `load_token` (L2362; `scripts/zerodha_login.py` L49 to L57), the subscript (L2363), the handlers for `FileNotFoundError` (L2364 to L2370) and `KeyError` (L2371 to L2374); `_build_kite_client` (L2375, outside the try); `main()`'s `finally` (L2187 to L2190); the entry guard L4285 to L4293.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT (code chain) and INTERPRETATION (Python semantics): (i) a missing or unreadable token file (any `OSError`) or a non-JSON file makes `load_token` return `None` (L53 to L57); L2363 evaluates `None["access_token"]` and raises `TypeError`; a non-UTF-8 file raises `UnicodeDecodeError` out of `load_token` (L56 catches only `OSError` and `JSONDecodeError`); valid JSON that is not an object raises `TypeError` at L2363. Neither handler matches; the exception leaves `_main_locked`, the lock is released (L2187 to L2190), and the guard logs CRITICAL "unexpected_error" and exits 2 (L4291 to L4293). The "Token file missing" line and `store.close()` (L2365 to L2369) are never reached. The pre-load runs for every `--mode live` start, so `--interactive --mode live` with a missing token also ends here with exit 2, before the interactive branch (L2528) where SU9 intended the login flow. (ii) In live, a missing or unreadable `accounts.csv` raises `ConfigMissingError` (`core/account_registry.py` L104 to L108, L173 to L177) and a schema problem `ConfigSchemaError` (L120 to L209); both are `ConfigError` subclasses of `TradingSystemError` by single inheritance (`core/exceptions.py` L30, L56, L64, L69), not `FileNotFoundError`, so they leave the try and the guard exits 1 (L4288 to L4290); a short row leaves `None` in a field and `.strip()` (L135) raises `AttributeError`, outside the `(KeyError, ValueError)` clause (L148): exit 2 (the mechanism is M-33). In paper the same problems reach the full load's `except Exception` (L2420 to L2423): exit 3. In live that handler is reached for a file problem only if the file changes between L2359 and L2414. (iii) A JSON object without `access_token` raises `KeyError` at L2363, caught at L2371: CRITICAL "Missing credential env var for live mode: 'access_token'" (a misleading message), store closed, exit 6; an unset API-key variable raises `KeyError` at L2361: exit 6 with an accurate message. (iv) FACT: the token is put into the environment (L2363) and the client is built from it (L386 to L387) before any validity test; the gate comes later (L2546 to L2552; C8). Effective values: the token file is a runtime file, not in the repository (UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED); `config/accounts.csv` at the baseline (2026-06-12): 10 required columns, 5 data rows, one primary row (enabled), no short rows (the startup part's count; no identifier copied). INTERPRETATION (declared unit, A17): exits 1, 2 and 6 are all outside `RestartPreventExitStatus=3 4 5` (unit L35), so each is restarted after 10 s; each attempt re-runs L2196 to L2363 (configuration load, store open with migration allowed, the kill-switch auto-clears, detection) and, once the day's first attempt has written today's session row (L2308), inserts a `CRASH_DETECTED` event unless a SHUTDOWN event or a COMPLETE EOD row exists for today. Whether this happened: UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED. INTERPRETATION (`deploy/token_watcher.sh` L14, A04; IS-5.20): the token watcher starts the unit only with a fresh token, so the token path arises chiefly from a systemd restart, a host boot, a manual start, or a token file removed or renamed after start.
- **Evidence:** the cited lines; HISTORICAL (`git log -S`): `load_token` has returned `None` on any `OSError` since `bcf03b5` (17-Apr-2026), and the pre-load with its `FileNotFoundError` handler was added later by `7fc06ff` (12-Jun-2026), so the handler was unreachable through `load_token` from the day it was written. Tests, as documents only: `tests/unit/test_main.py` (2026-08-28) replaces `is_token_valid` and `load_token` with valid results (L348 to L351), so no test drives a missing token through `main()`; `tests/unit/test_zerodha_login.py` L106 to L108 (2026-07-08) pins `None` for a missing file; `test_h11_invalidate_token_path.py` checks the loader only (L95 to L104, L126 to L131).
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE; KNOWN DEFECT for parts (i) and (ii). Part (i), the live token-file paths: the four conditions of section 1.6 hold: (a) the code has a mechanism for the case, the `FileNotFoundError` handler written for a missing token file (L2364 to L2370); (b) by Python's semantics it can never act: `load_token` returns `None` (or raises `UnicodeDecodeError`), so L2363 raises an exception that no handler on the path catches; (c) a missing or invalid token file is a case that the code says it handles, and it arises under the documented set-up (a file removed, renamed or mis-edited); (d) the outcome, exit 2, contradicts every intent source: the exit list has an entry that names the case (L18: 6), SPEC C.2 gives 1, and no source gives 2; under section 1.6's reading of exit-code sources the general "2 unexpected exception" (L14) does not state the intent for a case that the list names. Part (ii), the live `accounts.csv` paths (a missing or unreadable file, or a schema problem): the four conditions hold: (a) the code has a mechanism for the case, the handler written for an `accounts.csv` load failure (L2420 to L2423: CRITICAL "Failed to load accounts.csv" and `return 3`); (b) by Python's semantics it can never act in live for a problem present at start: the pre-read at L2359 raises `ConfigMissingError` or `ConfigSchemaError`, neither of which is a `FileNotFoundError` or a `KeyError`, before that handler is reached, and the guard exits 1 (L4288 to L4290); (c) a missing or invalid `accounts.csv` is a case that the code says it handles, and it arises under the documented set-up (a file removed or mis-edited; a malformed input that the registry's validation exists to reject); (d) exit 1 contradicts every intent source that names the case: the exit list's "5 invalid args / config" (L17; SU15, M41 L335, M41C L298) and, through IC13, the handler's 3; only the general entry "1 TradingSystemError" (L13; M41 L331, M41C L294) covers it, and under section 1.6's reading the named entries state the intent. The two named sources conflict with each other (5 against 3; R2, narrowed), which leaves the intended code open but not the contradiction. The short-row case of (ii) is the same finding as M-33 and is counted there. Whether either occurred in production: UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED. FACT for (iii)'s misleading message. OPEN QUESTION for the missing-variable code (IC-40: 5 or 6), which belongs to the exit-code contract (C4).
- **Owner:** A01 (the handlers and the wiring); A04 (`load_token`'s contract, boundary only); the restart treatment of each code is C4's; the short-row mechanism is M-33 (C39).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; R2 is answered by evidence for part (i); for part (ii) it stays open, narrowed, only on which named code was meant (3 or 5), since the named sources conflict (section 9). The 5-or-6 question for a missing variable goes with R4. Treatment is for the post-AUDIT discussion.

#### C3 — The token-invalidation path (FIX-062)

- **Area and flow:** areas 9 and 11; the failure callbacks (A13's code, A01's wiring) to `_shutdown` step 4; the FIX-062 auth-restart-loop guard.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** after a broker authentication failure, invalidate the token at shutdown so that the next live start fails fast and the restart loop stops: `main.py` L118 to L119 (blame 16-May-2026); `_invalidate_token` docstring L139 to L148 (16-May-2026, amended 06-Jul-2026); log text L160 to L163 ("manual token refresh required"); `_mark_auth_failed` docstring L169; the H-11 comment L126 to L133 (06-Jul-2026: pointing at the real path "re-arms the guard"); commit `14a889c` (16-May-2026): "_broker_auth_failed flag set via critical failure callback"; `tests/unit/test_fix062_token_invalidation.py` L88 to L112 (2026-07-06; document only): the critical-failure factory, called with the order monitor's own reason ("BrokerAuthError x3 -- monitor stopping"), sets the flag; CTI L838 (2026-06-11: "BrokerAuthError | SOFT_KILL + token invalidation at shutdown"; the kill type it names is A13's) and L895 (shutdown step 2: "Invalidate token if BrokerAuthError occurred"); `docs/audit/full_system_audit_04july2026.md` L90 to L93 (2026-07-06, H-11); `docs/SYSTEM_MAP.md` L1359 (file 2026-09-02; an entry of 06-Jul-2026 that calls the guard "armed": reason contains "BrokerAuthError" → `_mark_auth_failed` → `_invalidate_token` → the next boot returns 6). No source intends the flag to stay unset. Conflicts: IC-43 (the trigger) and IC-39 and IC-60 (the guard's expected exit). HISTORICAL operator procedure of 18-Apr-2026 (IS-5.23; A04 boundary): on a token expiry during the day the operator soft-kills, makes a new token and restarts (RUNBOOK T18R01, section 6.3; PLAYBOOK P089 to P093, section 7.4), and the drill that deletes the token file at 11:00 expects a soft kill, an alert and no new orders (PLAN T4R02, section 3.1); the soft kill on expiry is the token monitor's (A04; FIX-128 Fix E, IS-7.10); none of these addresses FIX-062's invalidation at shutdown, so they leave this record's result unchanged.
- **Actual code path:** the setter `_mark_auth_failed` L168 to L172, called only at L666 to L667 inside `_on_critical_failure` (`_make_critical_failure_cb` L657 to L685), and only when the reason contains "BrokerAuthError"; the factory is wired only at L2930 to L2932 (the live feed); the live feed calls its callback at three sites with fixed formats: "feed gap %.0fs > 10 min" (`data/live_feed.py` L422 to L424), "KiteTicker max reconnect (%d) exhausted" (L477 to L480; an integer attribute) and "tick-age watchdog: no tick for {age:.0f}s (threshold={...}s)" (L774 to L777; the threshold is an `int`, L116). The only producer of a "BrokerAuthError" reason is the order monitor (`broker/order_monitor.py` L648 to L652), whose `on_critical_failure` is wired at L3006 to `_make_api_failure_hard_kill_cb` (L727 to L752), which calls `hard_kill` and alerts but never calls `_mark_auth_failed`. Consumer: `_shutdown` L1678 to L1679.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: in the wired code `_broker_auth_failed` can never become True, so `_shutdown` step 4 never runs; a non-test `git grep` for `_mark_auth_failed`, `_make_critical_failure_cb`, `_broker_auth_failed` and `_invalidate_token` finds no other setter or caller. HISTORICAL (commits examined: `14a889c`, `0cd1b14`, `970aabf`): at FIX-062's commit the order monitor's `on_critical_failure` was not wired in `main.py` at all; FIX-128 (`0cd1b14`, 30-May-2026) wired it to the hard-kill factory; for the commits examined the guard has had no reachable trigger. FACT (code chain, from C2): even if the guard fired, the next live start would end with exit 2 at L2363 (the renamed file makes `load_token` return `None`), not with exit 6 at the gate; and exit 6 is itself restarted under the declared unit (L35; C4).
- **Evidence:** the cited lines and the grep; the live-feed call sites read at `14a889c` and at the baseline; the `0cd1b14` diff of the `OrderMonitor(...)` arguments; only `scripts/sr_level_export.py` L137 imports `main`, and it imports three unrelated helpers. Tests (documents only): `test_fix062_token_invalidation.py` drives the factory directly and `test_h11_invalidate_token_path.py` the loader and `_shutdown` with the flag set; no test drives the wiring.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE; KNOWN DEFECT. The four conditions of section 1.6 hold: (a) the code has a check for the case, the "BrokerAuthError" test in `_on_critical_failure` (L666 to L667) that arms `_invalidate_token`; (b) by Python's semantics the test can never be satisfied in the wired code: the only callback wired to that factory (L2930 to L2932) receives three fixed reason formats that cannot contain the text, and the only producer of such a reason, the order monitor, is wired to the hard-kill factory (L3006, L727 to L752), which never calls `_mark_auth_failed`; (c) a broker authentication failure is the case that the code and its sources say the guard handles (L118 to L119, L139 to L148, H-11), and it can arise under the documented set-up; (d) the outcome (the flag stays False and the token is never invalidated) contradicts every intent source listed. Whether a broker authentication failure occurred in production: UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (HISTORICAL: `docs/audit/HANDOFF_05-Aug-EVENING.md` L1247 to L1248 records one day without one; not runtime evidence available to this project).
- **Owner:** A01 (the wiring at L2930 and L3006; `_shutdown` step 4); A13 (the callbacks' contents; the kill type, CTI L838 against the hard kill) and A04 (token lifecycle) cross-referenced; B-03 audits the two helpers' mechanics (cross-part).
- **Rama:** not needed; R3 is answered by evidence (section 9). Treatment after the AUDIT.
