| signal | SIGINT before L3958 (`KeyboardInterrupt`, not caught by the guard); SIGTERM before L3958 (default action); SIGKILL after `TimeoutStopSec=30` | not listed | INTERPRETATION: a death by SIGINT or SIGTERM is clean for `on-failure` (no restart); a SIGKILL that ends a requested stop is not restarted | - | - |
| import failure | L22 to L106, outside the guard | not listed | INTERPRETATION: the interpreter exits 1; restarted | - | - |

- **Evidence:** the code above; the entry part's AST inventory of every integer return and `sys.exit`; grep for exit calls; the unit file; `deploy/token_watcher.sh` L125 to L191; HISTORICAL: `docs/audit/integrity_audit_2026.md` L3122 (2026-08-01; IS-12.10) states that the token watcher restarts crash exits 1 and 2 with a backoff ("cap 3/hr"); the watcher's code has that branch (`deploy/token_watcher.sh` L168 to L177, the case `1|2|*` with `crash_count_last_hour`; `MAX_CRASH_PER_HOUR` defaults to 3, L27; header L13: "1/2 = crash -> restart with backoff (max 3/hour)"), but the branch runs only when the unit has failed (L144), and the watcher does nothing while the unit is active or activating (L140 to L142); INTERPRETATION (systemd semantics; whether the declared unit was installed is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED, B-40): under the declared unit (UNIT L33 to L35: `Restart=on-failure`, `RestartSec=10`, `RestartPreventExitStatus=3 4 5`) systemd restarts exits 1 and 2 itself, so the unit does not enter the failed state on them and the branch is not reached; L3122 is inaccurate for that reason, not because the branch is missing; the unit's history read with `git show` (read-only): `316b335` added `StartLimitIntervalSec=300` and `StartLimitBurst=3`; `ccde039` (22-Apr-2026) replaced `Restart=on-failure` by `Restart=always`, removed both start-limit lines and added the comment "token-watcher handles the 'wait for fresh token' gating so an always-restart loop will not hammer with a stale token"; `398dbae` (05-May-2026) returned to `Restart=on-failure` with `RestartPreventExitStatus=3` ("Restart=always caused infinite holiday alerts on weekends") and removed that comment without restoring a start limit; `ebfe780` and `2f67bb8` extended the list. Documents and tests as cited. Start limit (added after ChatGPT's review of the first issue-discussion records, 05-Nov-2026; note 14): no explicit start limit is defined in the unit file (the whole of UNIT: no `StartLimitIntervalSec` or `StartLimitBurst`); whether a manager-level override limited restarts is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED, as no admitted record of the VM's manager configuration survives. INTERPRETATION (systemd semantics): systemd's documented defaults, `DefaultStartLimitIntervalSec=10s` and `DefaultStartLimitBurst=5` (systemd-system.conf(5); the same in systemd 229 and in the current source), apply to a unit that sets no limit, and restarts spaced 10 s apart never reach them. HISTORICAL: the unit's only recorded drop-in adds `Wants=trading-watchman.service` (Record 03 section 3.2, IS-12.13, the testing VM; the read-only VM inspection of 05-Jul-2026, `docs/audit/audit_05jul2026.md` L169 and L235: "[Unit] + Wants="; still present, not read, on 26-Aug-2026, `docs/audit/BOOT_PROOF_75e637c_26-Aug-2026.md` L363); CTI L112 (IS-12.05) and L997 to L998 take the defaults as applying; and the legacy records report long restart loops: the "240+ restart crash-loop" on exit 4 of 18-Jun-2026 (`ebfe780`) and the "infinite holiday alerts on weekends" under `Restart=always` (`398dbae`, 05-May-2026).
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT: the sites, the file contents and the docstring differences (9 missing; 7 called live-only; 0 and 1 wider than described; 5's "missing env var" not produced; "3 4" in code comments and documents dated after 23-Aug). FACT (the list): the declared unit's prevent list omits 6 (UNIT L35); INTERPRETATION (systemd semantics): the unit therefore restarts exit 6, which every source that addresses 6 intends to end the loop (R4's exit-6 part is answered by evidence; IC-60). INTERPRETATION: systemd and Python semantics (restart, start limit, signal deaths); the watcher is a second restart layer that, under the declared unit, sees only 0, 3, 4 and 5. HISTORICAL: the 23-Aug change explains the "3 4" comments; the start limit intended in April (STEP3, STEP4A, `316b335`, CT-089) was removed with a rationale tied to `Restart=always`, which was itself removed on 05-May, and was not restored. OPEN QUESTION: whether exit 9 and the deterministic causes behind 1 and 2 were meant to be restarted (R4, narrowed). Not a KNOWN DEFECT: condition (b) fails: the restart outcome is not certain by the language semantics; it depends on an installed supervisor and on the VM's manager settings, neither of which is established (B-40; the start limit, in Evidence).
- **Owner:** A01 (the process contract); A17 (the unit file); the token watcher is A04's file with its restart contract A13's (STUDY section 11); C2, C8, C9 and C12 and the L3899 path are recorded in their own items (cross-reference, not re-audited here).
- **Rama:** R4, narrowed to exit 9 and the deterministic causes behind 1 and 2 (squatted port, refused migration, malformed holiday file or special session, missing token file); not needed to classify; can wait.

#### C5 — Diagnostic options `--status` and `--dry-run`

- **Area and flow:** CLI diagnostics (`--status`, `--dry-run`) and their side effects.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** MAIN16 (`module_34_main.txt` L320 to L329, 2026-04-17): "Loads config + state_store read-only. Prints: Scenario, Kill switch state, Open positions count, Daily P&L, Last STARTUP/SHUTDOWN events, Subsystem versions. Exits 0. No subsystems instantiated." MAIN17 (L331 to L334): "Phases 0a-0d only. Prints StartupReport. Subsystems NOT constructed. No threads started. Exits 0 if ok, 3 if blocking failures." Help texts L198 ("Print subsystem status and exit 0") and L202 ("Run startup checks only; no trading"). Comment L2155 to L2156: "operator/diagnostic invocations" bypass the START window. AC1 comment L2219 to L2225: this boot path is "the ONLY sanctioned migrator". MED #10 comment L2304 to L2307: the early session row keeps cold-start detection correct after a mid-way crash. G5a (`docs/locked_decisions.yaml` L91 to L100, 2026-07-22): WARM = "clean stop, same day", CRASH = "no clean shutdown marker". SU6: the holiday guard runs "BEFORE anything else". MAIN6: HALT without `--resume` exits 4. Use beside a running service: no source; the lock documents warn against a hand-run `main.py --resume` beside the service (`deploy/resume.sh` L4 to L7; `docs/SYSTEM_MAP.md` L904).
- **Actual code path:** `main()` L2094 to L2190; `_main_locked` L2196 to L2302 (`--status`) and L2304 to L2482 (`--dry-run`); `_print_status` L636 to L650; `detect_startup_scenario` `utils/startup_checks.py` L192 to L382.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: both options run the holiday guard first (on a non-trading day: banner, exit 0 and, if the day's sentinel is absent, the holiday Telegram message, L2133 to L2149), bypass the START window (L2157 to L2160), set up logging in the working directory's `logs/` (L2178) and take the instance lock (L2183); beside the running service they are refused with exit 1. INTERPRETATION: run from the service's working directory, a refused diagnostic first appends an INFO start line (with its own default mode "paper") and a CRITICAL "Instance lock failed" line to the service's daily log files. FACT: then `load_all`; `StateStore(..., allow_migrate=True, market_open=...)` (L2233 to L2237), so INTERPRETATION (A03): a pending schema migration is applied by a diagnostic run while the market is closed; the kill switch is built and `clear_stale_state` and `auto_clear_scheduled_kill` run (L2260, L2265; their comments FIX-127 and FIX-154 say they clear kill state; persistence is A13's); detection runs; a CRASH scenario writes `CRASH_DETECTED` (L2277 to L2282); HALT gives exit 4 (L2290) unless `--resume`, which calls `kill_switch.resume(...)` (L2291) and so clears a HALT from a diagnostic. `--status` prints the scenario, the kill state and reason, the session date and mode, and the version, and returns 0 (field gap against MAIN16 recorded in B-15). `--dry-run` also writes today's session row (L2308 to L2314, mode from `args.mode`, default paper), builds the broker stack (with `--mode live`, the token pre-load and the KiteConnect client), loads the registry and instruments, runs the aggregate (in live a broker quote call; scanner HTTP fetches; NTP), and returns 0 or 3 (L2476 to L2482) without a SHUTDOWN event. FACT chain plus INTERPRETATION: `detect_startup_scenario` (`utils/startup_checks.py` L309 to L382) classifies a later same-day start with today's session row, no SHUTDOWN event and no COMPLETE EOD row as CRASH, so a first-of-day `--dry-run` makes that day's real first boot a CRASH (CRITICAL line, `CRASH_DETECTED` event).
- **Evidence:** code read (L2094 to L2482 re-read at L2283 to L2314 and L2476 to L2482 for this AUDIT); `detect_startup_scenario` read in full by the entry part. Tests (documents only): `tests/unit/test_main.py` L974 to L989 (`--status` exits 0 and builds no `SignalProcessor` or `LiveFeedManager`), L681 to L693, L445 to L457; the harness patches logging, configuration, store, bus, kill switch, time authority, lock and holiday guard (L286 to L293, L343 to L346), so no test exercises these side effects. Operator documents use `scripts/clear_kill_switch.py --dry-run` for status (`docs/RUNBOOK.md` L46; `docs/03_daily_operations_runbook.md` L156; `docs/disaster_recovery.md` L170); none prescribes `main.py --status` or `--dry-run` beside the service.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT: the code chain and its departure from MAIN16 and MAIN17 ("read-only", "Phases 0a-0d only", "No subsystems instantiated"). INTERPRETATION: the migration, the kill-state persistence, the next-boot CRASH and the shared log lines. Not a KNOWN DEFECT: condition (d) fails: the side effects come from shared boot code whose own sources support them (MAIN6 gives exit 4 on HALT before the `--status` branch; AC1 makes this path the sanctioned migrator; G5a's literal rule, same day and no clean shutdown marker, is met after a dry-run), so the outcome does not contradict every intent source. OPEN QUESTION: use beside the running service (R5 part 3).
- **Owner:** A01; A03 (migration on open), A13 (kill-state clears and `resume`), A14 (holiday send).
- **Rama:** R5 part 3 (whether diagnostics were meant to run beside the service); parts 1 and 2 are answered by evidence; not needed to classify; can wait.

#### C6 — Detection and the hash check run twice, on different state

- **Area and flow:** area 4; the Phase 0c detection and CL4 hash check (L2268, L2295), the early session row (L2308), and the Phase 0d aggregate's second detection, second hash check and scanner skip (`utils/startup_checks.py` L1561, L1578, L1589).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** G5a (`docs/locked_decisions.yaml` L91 to L100; file 2026-07-22): detection "via system_events table + kill_switch state + session_date comparison". MAIN6 and MAIN7 (`docs/web_claude/01_module_instructions/module_34_main.txt` L60 to L93; 2026-04-17): detection and CL4 in Phase 0c; the aggregate in Phase 0d (MAIN7 does not mention a second detection). SC12 (`module_33_startup_checks.txt` L212 to L250; 2026-04-17): the aggregate includes the detection with HALT blocking, and `scanners: ... | None  # skipped on COLD` (L231). The runner's docstring (`utils/startup_checks.py` L1531 to L1541): "3. Startup scenario detection (HALT is blocking)", "5. Config hash diff (warning only)", "7. Scanner connectivity (warning only; skipped on COLD scenario)"; comment L1587 ("skipped on COLD (no prior session to compare)"); `tests/unit/test_startup_checks.py` L1205 to L1218 (2026-07-17) pins the COLD skip for the runner alone. MED #10 (`docs/web_claude/02_phase_instructions/phase_g_medium_fixes.txt` L86 to L99; 2026-04-17) and `main.py` L2304 to L2307: write the session row after Phase 0c detection and before Phase 0e. CT-084 (`docs/web_claude/07_crash_test/03_detailed_scenarios.md` L1134 to L1143; 2026-06-11): on a COLD start "All 14+ startup checks run". HISTORICAL: `docs/audit/full_system_audit_04july2026.md` L157 (2026-07-06, M-U1): the scanner preflight "is skipped on COLD startups — i.e. every normal trading morning — so it effectively never runs"; `docs/audit/wave7_nine_investigation_22jul2026.md` L40 and L112 to L120 (2026-07-22): from a boot log, "a normal morning runs the checks with scenario CRASH, not COLD", verdict "not a defect — premise refuted / superseded"; `docs/audit/BOOT_PROOF_75e637c_26-Aug-2026.md` L163 to L189 (2026-08-26): COLD at `main.py`, CRASH inside the aggregate, "Blast radius is zero" (its explanation of the mechanism is labelled "INFERENCE ... not measured"). The sources conflict on the scanner skip (IC-30).
- **Actual code path:** L2255, L2268 to L2296, L2308 to L2314 (`_write_session` L619 to L633), L2451; `utils/startup_checks.py` L1548, L1561 to L1565, L1578 to L1580, L1587 to L1597, L1664 to L1668. `main.py` reads only `report.ok`, `report.blocking_failures` and, under `--dry-run`, `report.warnings` (L2476 to L2502).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT (code order): on the first boot of a day the first detection returns COLD (a row with an earlier date, L290 to L307); L2308 then replaces the row with today's date and the current hashes; the second detection finds a same-day row with no SHUTDOWN event and no COMPLETE EOD row and returns CRASH (L367 to L382), logged as "startup_scenario=CRASH: same day, no SHUTDOWN event found" and "run_all_startup_checks: OK scenario=CRASH" (L1666). The detection code is identical at `75e637c` and at the baseline (the startup part's `git diff`). FACT and INTERPRETATION: the second detection returns COLD only if the IST date changes between L2255 and L1548, which only a start around midnight that bypasses the START window allows, so the scanner skip (L1589) does not fire in `main.py`'s flow. FACT: `file_hashes` is `dict[str, str]` (`core/config_loader.py` L2434) and L2308 has just stored its JSON, so the second hash check cannot report a change and `config_hash_changed` cannot occur in `main.py`'s flow. FACT: `report.scenario` and `report.scenario_details` are never read; the STARTUP event carries the first detection's value (L4171). INTERPRETATION (code order): the second detection is a real backstop in two edge cases, each ending with exit 3: (a) after `--resume`, if `kill_switch.resume` (A13) did not clear the state, the aggregate blocks with `halt_requires_resume`; (b) with no session row and a same-day kill state that the auto-clears leave in place, the first detection returns COLD before reading the kill state (L217 to L228), the row is written, and the aggregate returns HALT; `--resume` has no effect on that attempt, because L2283 to L2291 act only on a first-detection HALT.
- **Evidence:** the cited lines; grep for `report.`; `git log -S`: the early write and the aggregate's second detection both date from `bcf03b5` (17-Apr-2026). Tests (documents only): `test_startup_checks.py` L1142 (HALT blocks) and L1205 to L1218 (COLD skip, runner alone); `tests/unit/test_fix131_config_drift.py` L98; `test_main.py` mocks `run_all_startup_checks` in its wiring tests (L791 to L813), so no test runs the real aggregate after the early write.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT (order and effects); HISTORICAL (two legacy records observed it; one judged the scanner part "not a defect"); OPEN QUESTION (R6). Not a KNOWN DEFECT: condition (b) fails, as no failure follows (the first hash check and the CL4 event and alert do their job; the scanner check runs rather than being skipped), and condition (d) fails, as the sources on the skip conflict (IC-30).
- **Owner:** A01 (`main.py` order; `utils/startup_checks.py`); A03 for the session table.
- **Rama:** R6 (OPEN QUESTION; can wait). The boot latency this causes is C32.

#### C7a — WARM after any same-day SHUTDOWN or a COMPLETE EOD row

- **Area and flow:** area 4; Phase 0c detection step 5 (`utils/startup_checks.py` L310 to L365).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** SC4 step 5 (module_33 L76 to L79) and the docstring L201 to L209: a SHUTDOWN for today gives WARM, otherwise CRASH; SPEC L606 to L611 (`get_last_shutdown_event_today`); the Audit #17 comment L331 to L335 (an operator stop after the square-off "is no crash"); A03 docstring `core/state_store.py` L2482 to L2490; G5a (ambiguous on which run's stop counts); SPEC L884 to L890 (2026-04-17): the Phase X.4 SHUTDOWN event "is what makes the next start a WARM restart", and "Without it, the next restart will be detected as CRASH" (INTERPRETATION: a marker per run). The Audit #17 comment (L331 to L335; `7ed3a3b`, 2026-04-22) concerns the EOD row only; no later source addresses a crash after an earlier same-day clean stop.
- **Actual code path:** `utils/startup_checks.py` L310 to L365; `core/state_store.py` L2491 to L2500 (`ORDER BY event_id DESC LIMIT 1`).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: any SHUTDOWN dated today, or a COMPLETE EOD row, gives WARM; the query does not compare it with a later STARTUP, so a clean stop, a restart and a crash on one day give WARM.
- **Evidence:** tests L332, L350 (single-run cases; documents).
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (changed from MATCH under section 1.6's supersession rule): SC4 step 5, the docstring and SPEC L606 to L611 state the code's any-SHUTDOWN-today rule, but SPEC L890 implies a marker per run and G5a is ambiguous on which run's stop counts, and no later source settles the case. FACT (the rule as coded); INTERPRETATION (the per-run reading of SPEC L890); OPEN QUESTION (R7).
- **Owner:** A01; A03 (the query).
- **Rama:** R7 (OPEN QUESTION, narrowed; can wait).

#### C7b — No session row gives COLD before the kill state is read; when HALT can occur

- **Area and flow:** areas 4 and 8; Phase 0c: the kill-switch auto-clears (L2257 to L2265), then detection steps 1 to 3.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** SC4 steps 1 and 2 (module_33 L61 to L65; 2026-04-17) and the function's docstring (L201 to L204): no session row → COLD first, then HARD_KILL → HALT. SPEC 4.1 (`docs/v2_design_spec.md` L598 to L604; 2026-04-17): "HALT takes precedence — never auto-start from a hard kill", tested before the session-date test (IC-28). G5c (`docs/locked_decisions.yaml` L113 to L122): "hard_kill ALWAYS requires manual --resume flag". HISTORICAL: `capital/kill_switch.py` `clear_stale_state` docstring L316 to L328 (file 2026-09-03): "HEADLESS GUARANTEE (Rama's 2026-06-20 decision): a new trading day ALWAYS starts with a clean slate — EVERY prior-day kill is cleared regardless of type" (a statement attributed to Rama inside a legacy file, not a statement made to this project; IC-27). FIX-127 and FIX-154 comments L2257 to L2265. HISTORICAL, related to IC-27 without settling it (IS-8.13, IS-9.15): the paper-session playbook of 18-Apr-2026 has the operator confirm the kill state ACTIVE before and after the launch (PLAYBOOK T3R04, Gate A; P036, Gate B) and says that a soft kill "Resets next morning" (P080, section 7.1), when no boot-time clear existed yet (`37f6ba5`: no `clear_stale_state` or `auto_clear`); the dashboard's "Full Trading Stop" halts trading "for the rest of the day" (`ops_dashboard/frontend/templates/controls.html` L267, A15, line 2026-08-17), its kill type not stated; neither names a prior-day HARD_KILL.
- **Actual code path:** L2260, L2265 (A13: `capital/kill_switch.py` L315 to L364 and L366 to L400); `utils/startup_checks.py` L214 to L286.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: with no row, L217 to L228 return COLD before `kill_switch.status()` is read (L237). Before detection, L2260 clears every prior-day kill and L2265 clears a scheduled same-day kill when no position is open (never a HARD_KILL). So the HALT branch at L2283 is reached only for a kill that the auto-clears leave in place (`clear_stale_state` keeps a kill with no recorded time or dated today or later, `capital/kill_switch.py` L333 to L337, so a prior-day HARD_KILL with no `triggered_at` also reaches it): a HARD_KILL, or a SOFT_KILL triggered today or with an unreadable time (L263 to L286), with an existing session row. INTERPRETATION: with no row, the aggregate's second detection catches a same-day kill with exit 3 instead of 4 (C6, case (b)).
- **Evidence:** the cited lines; tests (documents only) `test_detect_cold_no_session_row`, `test_detect_halt_hard_kill`, `test_detect_halt_soft_kill_same_day` (`test_startup_checks.py` L238, L270, L291).
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT (order); INTERPRETATION (the edge case); the code follows SC4 against SPEC 4.1 (IC-28), and G5c conflicts with the later HISTORICAL headless guarantee (IC-27). Not a KNOWN DEFECT: condition (d) fails, as the sources conflict (IC-28, IC-27).
- **Owner:** A01 (the detection order); A13 (the clearing semantics and IC-27).
- **Rama:** no R question in this part; IC-27 is A13's to raise; IC-28 goes to the post-AUDIT discussion with this item.

#### C7c — A future session date reaches the same-day branch

- **Area and flow:** area 4; Phase 0c detection step 4 (`utils/startup_checks.py` L289 to L307).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** a session date other than today gives COLD: SC4 step 4 (module_33 L73 to L74; 2026-04-17); the function's docstring L207 ("4. session.session_date != today -> COLD (previous session was another day)"); the inline comment L289 ("Step 4: different day -> COLD"); SPEC 4.1 (`docs/v2_design_spec.md` L602 to L604: `if session_date != today: return Scenario.COLD`). No source treats a later date differently.
- **Actual code path:** `detect_startup_scenario` L290 (`if prev_date is None or prev_date < today_date:`), then step 5 (L309 to L382); `main.py` L2275 to L2282 (the CRASH branch) and L2308 (the row rewrite).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: a later date is not `< today`, so it falls through to step 5, which returns CRASH (or WARM if a SHUTDOWN event or a COMPLETE EOD row is dated today); `main.py` then logs CRITICAL "Startup scenario: CRASH -- crash detected", writes `CRASH_DETECTED` (L2277) and L2308 overwrites the row with today's date. The procedure that follows is the same for every scenario (S-07), so the effect is the false CRASH report and event. INTERPRETATION: a later date needs an earlier boot whose IST clock ran ahead, or a manual edit. HISTORICAL: the `<` and the docstring's `!=` both date from the first commit `bcf03b5` (17-Apr-2026); no later commit explains the difference.
- **Evidence:** the cited lines; `git log -S` for both expressions; no test for a later date (the startup part read the test list).
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor); FACT. On the path "session row dated after today" the comparison `<` (L290) certainly gives CRASH or WARM, against every intent source found (SC4, the docstring, the inline comment, SPEC; CD-15); the docstring is not a stale comment beside otherwise intended code, because the module and design specifications say the same. Not a KNOWN DEFECT under section 1.6: (a), (b) and (d) hold, and condition (c) fails: no documented option, flag or bypass writes a later date, because the only writer of the row, `_write_session` (called at L2308 and L4174), writes today's IST date (L2255, L2266, L2310), so a later date needs an earlier boot whose host clock was set ahead of the true date, or a hand edit of the database, an input for which no validation step exists; neither arises under the documented set-up (an IST host kept in time by NTP; R10). The KNOWN DEFECT (minor) proposed in the startup and runtime fragment is withdrawn, returning to the startup part's ISSUE / FACT (Appendix A, notes). Whether a future-dated row ever occurred in production: UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Owner:** A01.
- **Rama:** not needed (low materiality; post-AUDIT list).

#### C7d — Removed configuration files are not reported

- **Area and flow:** area 4; `check_config_hash` (`utils/startup_checks.py` L581 to L634).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** SC6 (module_33 L111 to L128; 2026-04-17): compare the stored and current hashes and "Identify differing files".
- **Actual code path:** `utils/startup_checks.py` L618 to L622.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: only names of the current set whose hash differs are listed; a new file is listed (its previous hash is `None`), a removed file is not. INTERPRETATION: `load_all` requires a fixed set of files (A02), so only an optional hashed file could disappear without failing the boot first; whether A02's hashed set has such a file is A02's.
- **Evidence:** `tests/unit/test_fix131_config_drift.py` L81 (a new file is detected; document only); no test for a removal.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (minor); FACT. Not a KNOWN DEFECT: condition (c) is not shown to hold: whether the path can occur depends on A02's hashed set (not established).
- **Owner:** A01 (A02 for the hashed set).
- **Rama:** not needed.

#### C8 — Broker access before the token gate

- **Area and flow:** areas 4 and 5; Phase 0d: the client built from the pre-loaded token (L2375), the aggregate's clock check (the boot's first broker call), then the non-interactive live token gate (L2543 to L2554).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** a broker failure in the startup clock check fails the check: SC5 (module_33 L83 to L109; 2026-04-17: "broker_adapter.get_positions() or similar cheap call ... any Kite response carries a Date header") and its test list (L311 to L316: "Broker adapter raises -> passed=False with error"); the adapter's docstring (`broker/zerodha_adapter.py` L1705 to L1707; 2026-09-10): raise "so the caller (check_clock_skew) can return passed=False instead of swallowing the connectivity error"; MAIN7: the clock check is blocking (exit 3); comment L2345 ("Build broker adapter early for clock check"). On the order the sources conflict (IC-17): SU14 (M41 L320 to L327) and SPEC C.2 before C.3 (`docs/v2_design_spec.md` L661 to L672: authentication, then the clock validation's first broker call) put the token check first; SU7 (M41 L112 to L142; interactive) puts the checks before login; the code and L2345 put the clock check first. On what the startup clock check measures and its limits (IC-13, IC-14): G4 (`docs/locked_decisions.yaml` L80 to L89: an NTP check that refuses above 30 s); SC5 (a broker Date header); SPEC C.3 (L667 to L672: above 30 s exit(1); 5 to 30 s "warn, retry once after 5s"). Declared unit comment L29: "Exit 3 = startup check failure (would loop on same failure) -- no restart." HISTORICAL operator procedure of 18-Apr-2026 (IS-5.22): the operator checks by hand that the VM's token file carries today's date before the launch (RUNBOOK T4R03, section 1.2; PLAYBOOK T3R02, Gate A), a manual check before any broker call that states nothing about the code's order, so it leaves R8 open and this record's result unchanged.
- **Actual code path:** L2363 and L2375 (`_build_kite_client`, L381 to L390); L2451 → `utils/startup_checks.py` L1567 to L1575 → `check_clock_skew` L414 to L468 → `broker_adapter.get_server_time()` (`broker/zerodha_adapter.py` L1709 to L1728: a quote-bucket token, then `kite.quote` for one index instrument) → `core/time_authority.py` `assert_clock_at_startup` L263 to L301; the gate L2546 to L2552 (`scripts/zerodha_login.py` `is_token_valid` L60 to L77).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the clock check is the boot's first broker call, made with the token file's token before the gate; any exception from the call is translated and raised (L1721 to L1722); `check_clock_skew` catches every non-skew exception (L459 to L468), logs ERROR "check_clock_skew: broker adapter error: ..." and returns `passed=False` with skew 0.0; the runner adds the blocking entry `clock_skew` (L1574 to L1575) and runs every remaining step; `main.py` logs CRITICAL "Startup checks failed: ['clock_skew']" and exits 3 (L2484 to L2502). No retry. FACT: with no Date header captured, the adapter returns the local IST clock (L1725 to L1728), so the check then confirms connectivity and the token's acceptance only (the header hook is A04's). The limit is `system.clock.startup_max_skew_sec` (file 30.0, `config/system_config.yaml` L187), applied by the time authority (L283 to L295); a passing reading is appended to the runtime skew window (L297 to L299). INTERPRETATION: a token that the broker rejects ends the boot with exit 3 under the name `clock_skew` (the broker's text is in the ERROR line), which the declared unit does not restart; the gate's exit 6 (restarted under the declared unit) is reached only for a token that the broker accepted but whose file fails `is_token_valid` (account id, IST date or empty token). Whether Kite rejects a stale token is broker behaviour: UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Evidence:** the cited lines; tests (documents only): `test_startup_checks.py` L431 (`test_clock_skew_broker_error_fails`) and L1061 (a clock failure blocks); no test orders the gate against the clock check.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT (order and mapping); the mapping of a broker failure to a failed check MATCHes SC5 and the adapter's docstring; the ordering and the label conflict between sources (IC-17, IC-13); OPEN QUESTION (R8).
- **Owner:** A01 (the order; the mapping in `utils/startup_checks.py`); A04 (`get_server_time`, the Date-header hook, the exception types). The NTP side (IC-14) is S-42's and C30's.
- **Rama:** R8 (partly answered by evidence; the rest can wait).

#### C9 — The instrument-cache failure path

- **Area and flow:** area 4; Phase 0d: the instrument-master pre-load (L2432 to L2449), the aggregate (steps 1 and 9) and the assert (L2504 to L2506).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** a present but corrupt file blocks the boot with `instrument_cache_too_small` (exit 3): the BL-20 comment L2432 to L2438 ("file present but corrupt: log + pass None → check blocks with 'instrument_cache_too_small'"); the ERROR text L2446 to L2448 ("startup_checks will surface the root cause"); the assert's own comment L2504 to L2505 ("startup_checks passed, so instrument_cache is non-None ... Fallthrough guard for type-checkers"); the module docstring L15 ("3 startup check failure (blocking)"); the declared unit L29. The runner's side (IC-36): its docstring L1540 to L1541 ("skipped if instrument_cache is None — caller is expected to have handled load failure separately") and `test_startup_checks.py` L1310 to L1323 (the runner skips `None`); L1253 pins the function's `(False, 0)` for `None`. No source states an outcome of exit 2.
- **Actual code path:** L2439 to L2449; `utils/startup_checks.py` L1550 to L1553 (step 1) and L1606 to L1613 (step 9); L2506; the entry guard L4291 to L4293. A05 boundary: `InstrumentCache.load` raises `ConfigMissingError` for an absent or unreadable file and `ConfigSchemaError` for a present but invalid one (`core/instrument_cache.py` L112, L128 to L172, L180 to L181).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: any exception from the load leaves the cache `None`. Step 1 blocks only when `instruments.csv` is absent (L841, L868 to L871). Step 9 is skipped for `None` (L1608). If no other step blocks, `assert instrument_cache is not None` (L2506) raises `AssertionError` and the guard exits 2, without `store.close()`. INTERPRETATION: under `python -O` the assert is removed; the first unguarded use is L3946 (`instrument_cache.token_map()`; the use at L3223 sits inside a try that logs and continues), which raises `AttributeError`: exit 2 again, after several workers have started (C13). INTERPRETATION (declared unit): exit 2 is restarted every 10 s; the intended exit 3 is not. HISTORICAL (`git log -S`): the comment, the runner's skip and the assert were all added by one commit, `1e2d836` (19-Apr-2026, "BL-20 | startup_checks | reject startup if instruments < 1000"), so the composition never worked as its comment describes.
- **Evidence:** the cited lines; `git log -S` for the three parts; grep for every use of `instrument_cache` in `main.py`.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE; KNOWN DEFECT for the path "`instruments.csv` present, the load raises, every other check passes, no `-O`". The four conditions of section 1.6 hold: (a) the code has a mechanism for the case, the BL-20 design that passes `None` so that the size check blocks (comment L2432 to L2438), with the assert (L2506) behind it; (b) by Python's semantics the runner skips the check for `None` (`utils/startup_checks.py` L1608), so no blocking entry is added and the assert raises `AssertionError`, which only the guard catches (exit 2), whenever that path runs; (c) a present but corrupt file is a malformed input that the check exists to reject, and the declared unit starts `main.py` without `-O` (UNIT L18 to L19; no `-O` or `PYTHONOPTIMIZE` was found in `deploy/` or `docs/`); (d) the outcome contradicts every source that states the boot outcome (exit 3 through the blocking check; the exit list's "3 startup check failure (blocking)", L15, names the case, so under section 1.6's reading of exit-code sources the general "2 unexpected exception", L14, does not state the intent), and the assert's own comment says it cannot fire. Whether it occurred in production: UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED. FACT for the composition conflict (IC-36).
- **Owner:** A01 (the `main.py` handling and the runner's skip are both A01 files); A05 (the load's exceptions).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify (the code comment states the intent); treatment post-AUDIT.

#### C10a — The holiday decision uses the host-local date; the rest of the boot uses IST

- **Area and flow:** time and date sources at entry (AUDIT direction flow 11).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** `core/time_authority.py` L5 to L6 ("Single source of time for the entire system. Every module that needs 'now' calls time_authority.now_ist()") and L96 to L97 ("No datetime.now() elsewhere"; 2026-07-14); G4 (`docs/locked_decisions.yaml` L80 to L89: VM clock as runtime source "(all now() calls)"); FRULES 3.8 (`docs/foundation_engineering_rules.md` L221 to L225, 2026-08-06: "IST throughout the project") and PRULES §8 (`docs/trading_system_project_specific_rules.md` L79 to L83, 2026-04-17); SU6 in `module_41_interactive_startup.txt` L94: `today = today_ist_date()`; but SU6 in `consolidated_module_41_with_context.txt` L126: `today = date.today()  # before time_authority exists` (both 2026-04-17); STEP3 `docs/web_claude/05_deployment/step3_systemd_and_firewall.txt` L377 to L379 (2026-04-18: if the holiday guard does not fire, check that the VM's time zone is Asia/Kolkata); `scripts/fetch_daily_candles.py` L249 to L252 (2026-09-09: "a naive now() takes the HOST's timezone ... time_authority is the single clock for exactly this reason"); the H-17 sweep (`tests/unit/test_time_authority_sweep.py` L1 to L17, 2026-08-01) routes former `datetime.now()` sites through `now_ist()` with two documented exemptions, neither of them this site. HISTORICAL: `docs/audit/clock_dependency_class_26jul2026.md` L84 to L98 (2026-07-26) lists the boot guard's `date.today()` among five direct clock reads and says rule 3.8 "has four exceptions, only one of which is acknowledged".
- **Actual code path:** L2100 (`date.today()`, host-local) feeds `is_trading_day`, `next_trading_day`, `get_holiday_name` and the sentinel name; `utils/holiday_guard.py` L77 to L78 (`current_holiday_set` default year from `date.today()`); `core/config_loader.py` L2449 (A02: holiday-file year from `_date.today().year` at import); `utils/startup_checks.py` L859 to L866 (UTC plus 05:30 year) and L1548 (the aggregate's `today_date = time_authority.now_ist().date()`); IST via `now_ist()` at L2161, L2229, L2255, L2332; log-file names from `today_ist()` (`core/logger.py` L386, A15).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: three date derivations meet at boot: host-local (the guard, A02's file name), IST by offset (`check_config_files_present`) and IST (`now_ist`, including the aggregate's date). INTERPRETATION: with a host time zone of IST all agree; with UTC they differ only 00:00 to 05:30 IST, outside the START window, so only bypassed starts are affected; with a zone west of UTC the guard can evaluate the previous calendar day inside the START window (this is also the route that exposes C10g). The consolidated SU6 rationale "before time_authority exists" does not hold at the baseline: `main()` calls `time_authority.now_ist()` 61 lines later (L2161), and the module needs no initialisation (`now_ist` L94 to L99).
- **Evidence:** code read; grep at the baseline (Python files outside `tests/` and `docs/`): 10 `date.today(` lines in 8 files and 35 `datetime.now(` lines in 26, comment and docstring lines and the time authority's own lines included, as the STUDY counts; code lines only, the time authority excluded, 9 in 7 and 30 in 23 (the method and the figures are in B-34).
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT (sources and sites); INTERPRETATION (time-zone effects); sources conflict (the two SU6 texts; STEP3 accepts the host-time-zone dependency by requiring an IST VM, against the single-source rule). The VM's time zone is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (B-41).
- **Owner:** A01 (L2100, `utils/holiday_guard.py`); A02 for `core/config_loader.py` L2449; A15 for the log-file names.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; R10 is answered by documentary evidence as to the intended set-up and stays open, narrowed, for the actual VM state (section 9).

#### C10b — The `FileNotFoundError` handler also covers `next_trading_day`: a weekend can fail open

- **Area and flow:** holiday and weekend guard.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** `utils/holiday_guard.py` docstring L11 ("Returns False for Saturday/Sunday regardless of holiday list"; 2026-06-16); SU6 (both texts: weekend → exit 0); RDISC L9 to L27 (2026-04-17, HISTORICAL, "Rama's proposed flow", RS-03: on a holiday or weekend refuse to start and show the next trading day); comment L2098 ("zero log on non-trading day"); `reports/daily_report.py` L103 to L104 (2026-07-25: "Weekend stays a local check so a missing YAML still blocks weekends") and `reports/daily_trade_review.py` L2851 to L2852 (2026-08-29: the weekend half stays local "so a missing/unreadable YAML still blocks weekends rather than failing open"); the cron-side calendar guard (`tests/unit/test_s1_market_day_guard.py` L23 to L24, 2026-07-17, A16) fails open on a calendar error only by degrading "to a weekday check", so weekends stay blocked; record `01_SYSTEM_OVERVIEW.md` L147 (HISTORICAL: "It did not run unattended across a year boundary without a new holiday file"). Against these: comment L2152 ("missing holiday file: proceed with startup") and the test `test_holiday_guard_missing_yaml_proceeds` (`tests/unit/test_interactive_startup.py` L108 to L119), which covers only `is_trading_day` raising on a weekday.
- **Actual code path:** L2101 to L2152: `is_trading_day` (a weekend gives False without reading a file, `utils/holiday_guard.py` L92 to L93), then `next_trading_day` (L2103; walks forward and loads each weekday's year file, L98 to L111); one handler, `except FileNotFoundError: pass` (L2151 to L2152).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: on a weekend (or listed holiday) whose search for the next trading day reaches a year without a file, `next_trading_day` raises, the handler swallows it, and `main()` goes on to the START window, logging, the lock and `load_all`. INTERPRETATION: if the missing file is the current host-local year's, `load_all` stops the boot with exit 5 (A02); if only the next year's is missing, nothing in A01 stops the boot on that non-trading day (the aggregate's holiday step only warns, and C10g). FACT (file content): only `config/nse_holidays_2026.yaml` exists; the last 2026 weekend (26 to 27 Dec) has its next weekday (28 Dec) in 2026, so the case cannot arise in 2026 with the baseline files.
- **Evidence:** code read; `tests/unit/test_holiday_guard.py` (2026-05-01) covers a missing file only through `is_trading_day` (L72); no test covers `next_trading_day` across a missing year.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT (code path); INTERPRETATION (consequence). Not a KNOWN DEFECT: condition (d) fails: the general comment L2152 supports proceeding when the file is missing, so the outcome does not contradict every intent source (sources conflict).
- **Owner:** A01; A02 for `load_all`'s file requirement.
- **Rama:** not needed; R9(c) is answered by evidence (section 9); treatment later.

#### C10c — Other holiday-file problems escape before logging and before the START guard

- **Area and flow:** holiday-guard failure path and the exit-code contract.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** commit `2f67bb8` (HISTORICAL, 23-Aug-2026): a rejected configuration should "settle in failed instead of restarting every RestartSec=10s forever"; unit comment L29 (exit 3 "would loop on same failure -- no restart"); FIX-189 comment L1968 to L1977 (the service must not run or restart-churn overnight). No source addresses a malformed holiday file at the guard; the module itself raises `ValueError` for an unrecognised entry by design (`utils/holiday_guard.py` L51 to L62).
- **Actual code path:** `utils/holiday_guard.py` L51 to L62 (`yaml.safe_load`; `ValueError` for an unrecognised entry; `date.fromisoformat`); `main.py` L2101 to L2152 catch only `FileNotFoundError`; the guard runs before the START-window guard (L2154 to L2176) and before `setup_logging` (L2178).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT (code chain) and INTERPRETATION (Python semantics): a YAML syntax error, an unrecognised entry, an invalid date, a non-mapping top level (`AttributeError`), a decoding error or a permission error reaches the `__main__` guard and exits 2, with only Python's last-resort stderr output (no log file), on any weekday, and on a weekend through `next_trading_day`. INTERPRETATION (systemd semantics, if the unit was installed): exit 2 is restarted every 10 s, with no explicit start limit in the unit file (a manager-level override is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED; C4), at any hour, because the START guard is never reached; the same file read by `load_all` would give exit 5, which is not restarted. A file whose `holidays` key is missing or empty gives an empty set here (no error); `load_all` then rejects a missing key (HISTORICAL measurement, `docs/audit/register_items_26jul2026.md` L35 to L44, 2026-07-26).
- **Evidence:** code read; `docs/audit/register_items_26jul2026.md` L59 to L62 (HISTORICAL: the same data read twice in one boot has "two OPPOSITE failure policies"; recorded, not decided).
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT (code chain); INTERPRETATION (Python semantics; the restart loop); OPEN QUESTION (R9(a): which failure policy was meant to win). Not a KNOWN DEFECT: condition (a) of section 1.6 fails, as the guard has no handler for these errors (the module raises by design, and no source states the guard's intended handling). Whether it occurred: UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Owner:** A01; A17 for the unit.
- **Rama:** part of R4; can wait.

#### C10e — The direct holiday send bypasses the Telegram master switch and the configured names

- **Area and flow:** holiday notification and the alert contract.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** `alerts/telegram_notifier.py` `from_env` docstring L282 to L286 (TASK-10 Item A, 2026-07-27): the switch "lives in the config file, but cron scripts construct via this factory rather than load_all(). So we read alerts.telegram.enabled here and honor it — the master switch therefore silences ALL alerts (main app AND cron) with no exceptions"; `config/system_config.yaml` L387 (`enabled: true`, "master ON/OFF switch. Set false to suppress ALL Telegram alerts (silent)"; 2026-09-10); configured names `bot_token_env: "TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN"` (L388) and `chat_id_env: "TELEGRAM_CHANNEL_PRIMARY"` (L416), plus secondary channels (L419). The holiday helper's own docstring (L265 to L267: "Best-effort ... Uses direct HTTP POST before logger setup. Silent on failure") is silent on the switch; the anti-loop comment L2130 to L2132 asks for one message a day.
- **Actual code path:** `main.py` `_send_holiday_notification` L264 to L285, called at L2144, before `load_all`; `alerts/telegram_notifier.py` `from_env` L270 to L297 and `_read_telegram_enabled` L81 to L102 (called at L296).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the helper posts directly with urllib to the primary channel named by the hard-coded variables, never reads `alerts.telegram.enabled` or the configured variable names, and never uses `TelegramNotifier.from_env`, whose helper `_read_telegram_enabled` (L81 to L102) reads `system_config.yaml` directly with `yaml.safe_load`, without `load_all`, and fails open to enabled (so the guard's "before config" placement did not prevent honouring the switch). Effective file value: the switch is `true`, so at the baseline files the two paths agree.
- **Evidence:** code read (re-read for this AUDIT: L264 to L286, L2126 to L2153; `from_env` L270 to L311); configuration read.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE, classified FACT (established by evidence; latent at the file value). Changed from the entry part's proposed KNOWN DEFECT, because condition (a) of section 1.6 is not met: nothing on this path fails by the language's semantics; the helper does what it is written to do and omits a check that another module's docstring says covers all alerts. A configured switch or value that a path does not honour is classified ISSUE / FACT throughout this AUDIT (C30's unwired tiers; C36a `smart_tgt.enabled` and C36b `connect_sec`). The "no exceptions" claim in A14's file is a derived statement written for the notifier factory; it is untrue for this channel.
- **Owner:** A01 (the code); A14 owns the switch contract.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; treatment later.

#### C10g — `check_market_holiday_today` compares a string with a set of dates

- **Area and flow:** startup check 6 of the aggregate (the holiday warning); the guard-to-aggregate date handoff.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** docstring `utils/startup_checks.py` L884 to L889 (2026-07-17): "Return True if today is a trading holiday (SC10). market_windows: any object with a `holidays` attribute (list[str] of YYYY-MM-DD strings). Satisfied by NseHolidaysConfig from config_loader."; SC10 (`docs/web_claude/01_module_instructions/module_33_startup_checks.txt` L192 to L200) and its test plan (L341 to L344: "Configured holiday -> True"; 2026-04-17); MAIN7 (`module_34_main.txt` L84 to L90: "Market holiday: warning"); fix3 (`docs/web_claude/05_deployment/fixes/fix3_holidays_schema_audit.txt` L56 to L68, 2026-04-18): every consumer doing `if today_iso in cfg.nse_holidays.holidays` (the "OLD (string list)" form) must compare dates instead; `tests/unit/test_startup_checks.py` L896 to L905 (2026-07-17) expects True for a configured holiday, with a list-of-strings fixture.
- **Actual code path:** `utils/startup_checks.py` `check_market_holiday_today` L879 to L904, called by `run_all_startup_checks` at L1583 with `today_date = time_authority.now_ist().date()` (L1548) and the `market_windows` object that `main.py` passes (L2456), a `MarketWindows` whose `holidays` is a `set[date]` (`core/market_windows.py` L67; built by `_load_holidays` L441 to L443). The object the docstring names, `NseHolidaysConfig`, holds `list[HolidayEntry]` (each a `date` and a `name`; `core/config_loader.py` L2395 to L2413).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: `today_str in holidays` (L900) compares an ISO string with `date` objects (or, for `NseHolidaysConfig`, with `HolidayEntry` models); a string never equals either, so the function cannot return True for a configured holiday with the object `main.py` passes or with the object its docstring names; weekends return True (L891 to L896). FACT (re-check of reachability for this AUDIT): the guard decides on the host-local date (L2100), the aggregate on the IST date (L1548). INTERPRETATION: when the two dates are equal, a configured holiday stops the boot at the guard (exit 0) and never reaches the aggregate; when they differ (a host time zone other than IST: inside the START window for a zone west of UTC, or for a START-window bypass such as `--dry-run`, `--resume` or `TS_IGNORE_MARKET_WINDOW=1` in the early IST hours with a UTC host), a configured weekday holiday reaches the aggregate, and the check certainly misses it, so the boot proceeds with no `market_holiday` warning. The entry part's statement that the exposing path is unreachable in `main.py`'s flow holds only for an IST host.
- **Evidence:** code read (L879 to L905, L1508 to L1590, `main.py` L2451 to L2474, the `HolidayEntry` and `NseHolidaysConfig` models); grep: the only non-test caller is L1583; S-47 recorded the same check (merged here).
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE; KNOWN DEFECT (latent). The four conditions of section 1.6 hold: (a) the code has a check for the case, the membership test at L900; (b) by Python's semantics an ISO string never equals a `date` (or a `HolidayEntry`), so the test is false for every configured holiday whenever the check runs with the object `main.py` passes, or with the object its docstring names; (c) the check exists to report a configured holiday, and on an IST-configured host the case arises through the documented START-window bypasses that reach the aggregate (`--dry-run`, `--resume`, `TS_IGNORE_MARKET_WINDOW=1`; L2157 to L2160) in a boot that crosses midnight IST between the guard (L2100) and the aggregate (`utils/startup_checks.py` L1548), and, on a host whose time zone is not IST, in boots during the hours when the host-local date differs from the IST date (C10a); (d) the outcome (configured holidays never reported) contradicts every intent source (the docstring's purpose, SC10 and its test plan, MAIN7, fix3's explicit direction and the unit test), and no source supports it. The KNOWN DEFECT proposed in the entry and modules fragment is kept; the assembly's change to ISSUE / FACT is reversed (Appendix A, notes). Whether it occurred in production: UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Owner:** A01 (`utils/startup_checks.py` and the `main.py` wiring). S-47 (the startup part) recorded the same finding, classified FACT (latent), and is merged here.
- **Rama:** not needed; R9(b) is answered by evidence (section 9).

FACT: an unset `TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN`, `WEBHOOK_SECRET` or `ZERODHA_API_SECRET_<account-ID>` is blocked by the check (exit 3) before L2629; L2629 can raise `KeyError` (exit 2) only if `bot_token_env` names a different, unset variable. No live path gives exit 5 for a missing variable, against docstring L17 (C4). FACT: the Telegram bot-token name exists as one configured key (L2629) and three literals (L230, L269, `alerts/telegram_notifier.py` L288). INTERPRETATION (subprocess semantics): the pre-flight child started at L4089 (`scripts/preflight/startup_hook.py` L67, `Popen` without `env=`) inherits that environment (A20; noted, not investigated).

#### C13 — Workers start before the last guards and the startup reconcile

- **Area and flow:** areas 4, 7 and 9; Phase 0e construction (L2616 to L3844), the Q4(c) and A5 guards (L3436 to L3465), and the Phase 0f reconcile and its kill check (L3880 to L3899).
