SPEC C.1 to C.7 (`docs/v2_design_spec.md` L645 to L734; 2026-04-17): every validation (C.1 to C.5, including the capital invariant "HARD_KILL + exit(1)") precedes C.7, where the background threads start as their modules initialise, the order monitor's before the reconciler's one-shot reconcile (CD-13). The Q4(c) comment (L3428 to L3435: "fail-fast BEFORE any structure-exit / zone infra is built") and the A5 comment (L3444 to L3450: "fail-fast before any capital is reserved"). The BUILD 1 #12 comment (L3869 to L3879): the synchronous reconcile must precede `signal_processor.start()` and the webhook server (stated for the signal pipeline only). The MIS notifier (`alerts/mis_squareoff_notifier.py`; 2026-08-28): a background self-test because "the BOOT PATH MUST NOT WAIT FOR A TRANSPORT" (L237 to L241) and its own poll thread for independence from the MIS orchestrator (L257 to L262; `main.py` L3338 to L3342): independence from the orchestrator, not from the boot's guards. No source found states that the MIS units, the sweeper or the optional workers should start before Q4(c), A5 or the reconcile.
- **Actual code path:** L3355 (MIS boot self-test thread), L3357 (MIS notifier poll thread), L3380 (MIS auto-square-off poll thread), L3407 (the webhook receiver, whose constructor starts the in-flight sweeper, `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L217 to L220), all before L3436 to L3465; the optional workers at L3504, L3536, L3651, L3694, L3748, L3749, L3796, L3797, L3816 and L3832, before L3880 and L3894 to L3899.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the four units above (daemon threads: `alerts/mis_squareoff_notifier.py` L250 to L253, L274 to L278; `orders/mis_autosquareoff.py` L410 to L414) start before Q4(c) and A5; the self-test's docstring says "once per ACTUAL boot ... never suppressed" (L233 to L235; a source), while FACT: the code runs it once per notifier instance (`_boot_test_done`, L243 to L246). FACT: with the file values (`sr_detector.enabled: true` L509, `regime.enabled: false` L548, `allocator_mode: "shadow"` L565, `v3_chain_mode: "shadow"` L574, `watchlist.enabled: true` L620, `wait_for_retest_enabled: false` L529, `structure_exit_enabled: false` L634) five optional thread sets start before the reconcile and its kill check. HISTORICAL (`git log -S`, first commits): S&R detector `dea336a` (27-Jun-2026), allocator `536172c` (12-Jul-2026), PB-01 `5a71fb6` (13-Jul-2026), MIS notifier and auto-square-off `22a143f` and `6f00b4d` (28-Aug-2026). FACT: the guard returns at L3443, L3465 and L3899 call only `store.close()`, which closes the main thread's connection only (C19). INTERPRETATION (CPython): the started threads are daemon threads, not joined at exit and abandoned at interpreter finalization; they keep running, with their own store connections, until the process ends; the MIS self-test sends its messages for a boot that then aborts (message content: A14). INTERPRETATION: the A5 comment's "before any capital is reserved" does not hold literally: capital is initialised (L2761) and rehydrated (L2815) before A5 (CD-12); it probably means before a new trade reserves capital.
- **Evidence:** the cited lines; grep for `threading.Thread(` and `.start()` in the constructed modules; executors created in constructors (for example `orders/smart_tgt_manager.py` L145 and `screening/step_executor.py` L76) start their threads only on the first submit (INTERPRETATION, `concurrent.futures`).
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT (order); sources differ (CD-13, with SPEC C.7 as a second source: MAIN8, MAIN10, MAIN11 and the test name against the code; SPEC C.7 starts threads during initialisation, but only after every validation); OPEN QUESTION (R12).
- **Owner:** A01 (start placement); A12 (the MIS auto-square-off's behaviour), A14 (the notifier), A06 (the sweeper), A08 and A18 (the optional workers). Signal handlers: C14.
- **Rama:** R12 (OPEN QUESTION; can wait).

#### C14 — Late signal handlers and no cleanup on a failed start (W-01 and W-02 merged)

- **Area and flow:** areas 9, 11 and 12; startup order, signals and partial-start cleanup (flows 5 and 6).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** FIX-169 F31 comment L3956 to L3957 (blame 13-Jun-2026): "install signal handlers BEFORE starting threads so a SIGINT during startup triggers clean shutdown instead of default exit". HISTORICAL: `docs/audit/system_audit_14jun2026.md` (2026-06-13) L174 to L176 (F31: "SIGINT during startup bypasses clean shutdown") and L178 to L180 (F32: "Partial startup failure leaks DB connection ... No try/finally around Phase 0e subsystem construction"); commit `c303e04` (13-Jun-2026): "F31: Signal handlers installed before thread start to catch SIGINT during startup" and "F32: Add store.close() at 7 early-return paths". MAIN13 (module_34 L283 to L290; 2026-04-17): handlers at the runtime loop (IC-46). Unit L22 to L23 (2026-08-23): "Send SIGINT on stop so main.py's Ctrl+C handler runs (clean shutdown, SHUTDOWN event, DB flush)"; unit L32: "Exit 1/2 = runtime error/crash -- restart so system can recover mid-day". `docs/RUNBOOK.md` L28 to L34 (2026-08-26): after `systemctl stop`, verify the SHUTDOWN event. HISTORICAL operator procedure of 18-Apr-2026 (IS-4.26, IS-4.27): Ctrl+C is the abort during the start, on a failed pre-flight check (RUNBOOK T5R03, section 2) and when a readiness signal is missing (PLAYBOOK P037, section 3.2); INTERPRETATION: the procedure presumes that a Ctrl+C during the start is a safe abort, which is F31's stated aim; during the checks (L2451, before L3958) it takes this record's `KeyboardInterrupt` path.
- **Actual code path:** `_install_signal_handlers` L1570 to L1579, called once at L3958; the thread starts before it (W-01): L3355 (`f_boot_self_test`), L3357 (`mis_f_prepass_poll`), L3380 (`mis_autosquareoff_poll`), L3407 (`in_flight_sweeper`, constructor), L3504 to L3832 (the optional workers, as in C13), L3922 (`candle-store-timer`), L3947 (`live-feed-consumer`, `live-feed-watchdog` and the third-party ticker thread; live only); `main()`'s `finally` L2187 to L2190; the guard L4286 to L4293; the handled returns after workers start: L3443, L3465 (exit 3) and L3899 (exit 1).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** INTERPRETATION (CPython and POSIX semantics): before L3958, SIGINT raises `KeyboardInterrupt` in the main thread; the trading packages have no bare `except:`, no `except BaseException` and no `except KeyboardInterrupt` (grep), and the guard catches only `Exception`, so the interrupt passes through `main()`'s `finally` (the lock is released) and the interpreter exits; a SIGTERM before L3958 keeps its default action. In both cases `_shutdown` does not run and no SHUTDOWN marker is written. FACT: after L3958 the handler only sets the event; startup runs to the end (the STARTUP event, the session row, "System Active", L4167 to L4203) and `_shutdown` follows; the synchronous broker calls after L3958 catch the `RateLimitAbortedError` that a set event causes (`orders/order_reconciler.py` L755 to L761, L435 to L439, L652 to L654; `orders/smart_tgt_manager.py` L854 to L867), and an uncaught one would give exit 1 (`core/exceptions.py` L284). FACT: no failed start calls `_shutdown`, stops a worker or writes a SHUTDOWN row; the handled returns close the main thread's connection only; any exception (C15) reaches the guard with no close; `main()`'s `finally` always releases the lock. INTERPRETATION (CPython 3.9+; the version is UNKNOWN): interpreter exit runs the executors' queued and in-flight tasks before joining their workers; after L3974 those include signal-processor tasks, so a dying boot can still finish a signal pipeline that reached the queue after L4000; exit also joins the non-daemon flatten thread if a hard kill fired during startup, and abandons the daemon threads. INTERPRETATION (systemd semantics): under the declared unit exits 1 and 2 restart after 10 s, exit 3 does not. HISTORICAL (W-01, `git log -S`): when F31 was fixed, the sweeper, the candle timer and the feed threads already started before the handlers; the optional workers (27-Jun to 13-Jul-2026) and the MIS units (28-Aug-2026) widened the gap: at least seven thread starts precede the handlers in live, twelve with the file-value flags, plus the ticker thread.
- **Evidence:** grep for the three exception forms and for the start calls; `git blame` and `git log -S` of each start; the `c303e04` diff (it moved the call to before `order_monitor.start()`); at `bcf03b5` the call sat just before `_shutdown_event.wait()` (L1162 to L1163). Tests (documents only): `tests/unit/test_main.py` L339 to L341 replaces `_install_signal_handlers` with a mock that sets the event, so no test drives a real signal.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT (the order; the F31 comment does not describe the code; no cleanup on a failed start); INTERPRETATION (the effects of a signal before L3958; exit-time behaviour). Not a KNOWN DEFECT: condition (b) fails, as the effect of a signal depends on timing. W-01 (flow 5) and W-02 (flow 6) are merged here and counted once.
- **Owner:** A01 (A03 for the close semantics; A17 for the unit).
- **Rama:** not needed (F31 and F32 state the intent); R12 covers the early-start intent; treatment after the AUDIT.

#### C15 — Unguarded steps late in the boot

- **Area and flow:** areas 7, 8 and 9; Phase 0e subscriptions to Phase 0h, failure path.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** F32 (C14): a partial startup failure should not leak. The 27-Jul-2026 rule L4022 to L4027: "fail-fast when the failing condition can only arise from a DELIBERATE ACT; degrade-and-alarm when the ENVIRONMENT can cause it". L4221 to L4222 (blame 01-Aug-2026): "live emits CRITICAL and continues (never crash a live boot on a registration gap)".
- **Actual code path:** no try in `main.py` around: L3847 and L3853 to L3858 (subscriptions, the drift handler); L3866 (`eod.post_wire_init`); L3907 to L3916 (probe construction); L3922 to L3977 (starts and rehydrates); L3987 to L4000; L4074 to L4082 (health server); L4094 to L4156 (thread starts and `_parse_hhmm`); L4164 (`validate_all`, warn-only: `core/config_validator.py` L221 to L255); L4167 to L4180 (the STARTUP insert and the session write); L4223 to L4244. `assert_composition` loads its registry without a try (`core/effect_telemetry.py` L165, L129 to L142) and raises `EffectCompositionError` (a `RuntimeError`, L71) in paper only (L204 to L206); in live a mismatch logs CRITICAL and alerts (L207 to L216). `reconcile_once` (L3880) catches every `Exception` itself (`orders/order_reconciler.py` L652 to L654).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: any of these that raises ends the boot with exit 1 (`TradingSystemError`) or 2, without `_shutdown`; the workers already started keep running until interpreter exit (C14). After L4000 the webhook server is listening; after L4191 the operator has had "System Active". INTERPRETATION: a missing or unreadable `config/expected_managers.yaml` in live gives exit 2 after "System Active"; that is a read failure, not the "registration gap" that L4221 to L4222 says must never crash a live boot.
- **Evidence:** the code above; the STUDY's trace rows S90 to S124 (re-checked by the runtime part; they agree).
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT (the steps are unguarded); INTERPRETATION (the consequences). Not a KNOWN DEFECT: whether any of these calls raises depends on the owners and on runtime conditions.
- **Owner:** A01 for the orchestration; which calls can raise is for A12, A04, A05, A06, A07, A11 and A15.
- **Rama:** not needed (F32 states the intent); treatment later.

#### C16 — The boot-order assert

- **Area and flow:** area 8; the Phase 0f flag (L3881) and the Phase 0g assert (L3968 to L3971).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** comments L3869 to L3879 and L3965 to L3967 (blame 24-Jun-2026): the synchronous reconcile "MUST have run before the signal pipeline goes live, so phantom-position capital is corrected before any trade can be sized/placed"; `docs/SYSTEM_MAP.md` L1396 to L1397 (an entry of 24-Jun-2026, BUILD 1 #12; file 2026-09-02): "`assert _startup_reconcile_done` so `reconcile_once()` provably precedes `signal_processor.start()`/webhook". RC13 (`docs/locked_decisions.yaml` L2329 to L2336): `reconcile_once` returns `[]` immediately when another cycle holds its lock.
- **Actual code path:** L3880 (`recon_actions = order_reconciler.reconcile_once()`); L3881 (`_startup_reconcile_done = True`); L3968 to L3971. A12 boundary: `reconcile_once` returns `[]` after logging any `Exception` (`orders/order_reconciler.py` L652 to L654) and returns `[]` without reconciling when its lock is held (L640 to L642).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the flag is set whenever the call returns: after a reconcile, after a logged failure, or after a skipped cycle (the skip cannot happen at L3880, because the poll thread starts only later, at L3961: INTERPRETATION). In this straight-line code the assert cannot fail. INTERPRETATION (Python semantics): if a later edit moved the reconcile below the assert, the local name would be unbound and L3968 would raise, so the assert works as a static tripwire against reordering; under `python -O` it disappears (whether `-O` was used: UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED). FACT: a failed reconcile is not told apart from a successful one, and `main.py` neither stops nor alerts on it (only A12's ERROR log).
- **Evidence:** the cited lines; no test names `_startup_reconcile_done` (grep).
- **Result and classification:** MATCH for the ordering purpose ("provably precedes" holds in code order); ISSUE for the second reading (the flag does not show success). FACT; INTERPRETATION (the tripwire).
- **Owner:** A01; A12 for `reconcile_once`'s error policy.
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; whether a failed startup reconcile should block the boot is a post-AUDIT treatment question (related to R24).

#### C17 — The shutdown event before the flatten drain

- **Area and flow:** area 11; `_shutdown` steps 1 and 2; the flatten worker during an external stop.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** FIX-060, L1628 (blame 15-May-2026), and commit `07979fa` (15-May-2026: rate-limited waits abort on shutdown; "Prevents indefinite blocking during graceful shutdown"): "Set shutdown event FIRST so rate limiter aborts immediately". M-C8, L1632 to L1642 (16-Jul-2026): "drain the HARD_KILL flatten worker FIRST, before tearing anything down ... Draining last would pull the DB out from under a live flatten"; "bounded grace, then a CRITICAL". L1066 to L1075 (16-Jul-2026): a 15 s grace because the unit sets `TimeoutStopSec=30`; "A flatten that outlives the grace gets a CRITICAL: positions may remain open, and that is the operator's call" (HISTORICAL; RS-14); "The INTERNAL eod-self-exit path never hits this". `capital/kill_switch.py` L1266 to L1270 (16-Jul-2026), the `drain_flatten` docstring: "timeout=None waits indefinitely ... that is the INTERNAL eod-self-exit case ... An EXTERNAL SIGTERM passes a bounded grace"; L1189 to L1192: the worker is not a daemon, so "the interpreter itself will not tear down under a running flatten". `docs/SYSTEM_MAP.md` L1069 to L1075 (2026-09-02); `PATHS.md` L121 (2026-09-03); `tests/unit/test_mc8_async_hardkill.py` L433 to L483 (2026-07-16; document only). The two "first" rules conflict in effect (IC-56); the docstring's `timeout=None` internal case is not wired (IC-57).
- **Actual code path:** the event is set at L1629, then `drain_flatten(timeout=flatten_drain_timeout_sec)` (L1643 to L1656) with the default 15.0 (L1624, L1075); the only non-test call (L4252 to L4277) passes no override; the event is set earlier still by the handler (L1573) or the self-exit (L1525). The flatten uses the shared adapter (`kill_switch.set_adapter`, L2653), whose rate limiter waits on the same event (L2348; `broker/rate_limiter.py` L236 to L244); the limiter aborts only a call that has to wait for a token. The flatten loop retries with backoff up to a 2 h deadline (`capital/kill_switch.py` L85, L2015 to L2047).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: on an external stop with a flatten running, the drain waits at most 15 s and then logs CRITICAL "SHUTDOWN WITH FLATTEN STILL RUNNING ..." (L1646 to L1652). INTERPRETATION: during that grace any flatten broker call that must wait for a limiter token aborts; how the flatten counts and retries those aborts is A13's. INTERPRETATION: the same holds for the ENTRY cancellations of step 19 (L1765 to L1772, C21a), which run after the event is set: a cancel that must wait for a token (`broker/zerodha_adapter.py` L1058) aborts and is logged and skipped (`broker/order_monitor.py` L576 to L584), so an ENTRY order can stay open at the broker, against FIX-129 item 45 and CT-078 (CTD L1059: "Cancels ENTRY orders"); this ISSUE part of C21a is held here (IC-56). FACT: after a timeout `_shutdown` continues and "closes" the store; `StateStore.close()` closes only the calling thread's connection (`core/state_store.py` L251 to L264), so the flatten keeps a usable connection. INTERPRETATION: the M-C8 reason ("would pull the DB out from under a live flatten") does not hold literally under the store's per-thread semantics. INTERPRETATION (CPython): after `_shutdown` returns, interpreter exit joins the non-daemon flatten thread; under a systemd stop SIGKILL comes at `TimeoutStopSec`; on a manual Ctrl+C the process stays alive until the flatten ends (up to 2 h), and a second SIGINT only sets the event again. INTERPRETATION: a hard kill that starts between the self-exit's decision and the drain gets the 15 s grace on the internal path too; no caller passes `timeout=None` (the internal path waits at the gate instead, L1272 to L1277).
- **Evidence:** the code above; `broker/zerodha_adapter.py` L1058 (`cancel_order` takes a limiter token); `broker/order_monitor.py` L576 to L584 (a failed cancel is logged and skipped).
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT (the order; the single 15 s call); INTERPRETATION (the effects); OPEN QUESTION (whether the event-first rule was meant to apply to the flatten's own calls during the grace: R15). The external-path design itself (15 s, then CRITICAL) is documented and consistent (a MATCH for that part; C36e for the constant).
- **Owner:** A01 for the order; A13 (the flatten loop) and A04 (the limiter) cross-referenced.
- **Rama:** R15, second half (OPEN; can wait until A13 has established how the flatten loop treats `RateLimitAbortedError`).

#### C18 — Shutdown duration (W-06 merged)

- **Area and flow:** areas 11 and 12; `_shutdown` timing against the declared unit's stop contract (flow 10).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** L1066 to L1072 (16-Jul-2026): "15s leaves the other half of the budget for the rest of _shutdown (WAL checkpoint + close)" (CD-05). HISTORICAL: CTI L893 (2026-06-11): "Graceful shutdown sequence (30s TimeoutStopSec)". Unit L22 to L25 (2026-08-23): `KillSignal=SIGINT`, `TimeoutStopSec=30`; no `KillMode`, which INTERPRETATION reads as systemd's default control-group mode, as `docs/audit/HANDOFF_05-Aug-EVENING.md` L1259 also notes. HISTORICAL: the same handoff L1273 to L1276 (05-Aug-2026): the census is written "within seconds, far inside TimeoutStopSec=30". `docs/RUNBOOK.md` L28 to L34. `signals/signal_processor.py` L327 to L329 (SP4: the stop takes "up to 5s"; CD-18). HISTORICAL operator procedure of 18-Apr-2026 (IS-11.23, IS-11.24; CD-05): after one Ctrl+C the operator waits "up to 30s" for "Shutdown complete" (RUNBOOK T8R07, section 4; PLAYBOOK P060, section 5.2), and after a SIGTERM waits 30 seconds and checks that the process is gone (RUNBOOK T19R01, section 6.4): further sources that a stop ends within 30 s, which leave this record's result unchanged.
- **Actual code path:** `_shutdown` L1599 to L1831, with the owners' stop methods (bounds read by the runtime part in each owner module).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT (fixed maxima from the code; file-value flags): the drain 15 s, only while a flatten is RUNNING or DRAINING (`capital/kill_switch.py` L1282 to L1285, L1299); the signal processor a 10 s join (`signals/signal_processor.py` L336), then two unbounded `shutdown(wait=True)` calls (L339; `screening/step_executor.py` L239); the S&R detector 2 s; the allocator 10 s; the V3 chain 3 s; PB-01 2 s and 3 s (`config/system_config.yaml` L509, L565, L574, L620); the entry gate a 5 s join plus a fixed 3 s sleep (`screening/entry_gate.py` L247, L255); smart TGT 5 s; TGT retry 5 s; the reconciler 5 s; the entry cancels (n rate-limited broker calls); the order monitor 5 s; the clock-skew probe 2 s; the token monitor 5 s; the live feed about 11 s (`data/live_feed.py` L50, L163 to L166); the candle store an event only; "System Stopping" bounded by `alerts.telegram.send_deadline_seconds` (file 8, `config/system_config.yaml` L393; passed at L2640); then the SHUTDOWN insert, the PASSIVE checkpoint and the close (the SQLite busy timeout is 30 s per connection, `core/state_store.py` L236). The STUDY's 71 s is reproduced arithmetically, but it counts the 15 s drain as always run, while the drain waits only while a flatten is running: before the SHUTDOWN marker the maxima are 56 s without a flatten (64 s with the send), 84 s with the file-value optional workers and 99 s with a flatten, plus the unbounded executor waits, the broker cancels and the DB waits. FACT: most joined loops wait on their stop events and return early (`broker/order_monitor.py` L597, `orders/order_reconciler.py` L663, `broker/token_monitor.py` L229, `broker/clock_skew_probe.py` L107, `orders/tgt_retry_manager.py` L138, `screening/entry_gate.py` L405, `data/live_feed.py` L721, `allocation/portfolio_allocator.py` L141, `v3_chain/pb01_entry.py` L136); only the gate's 3 s is unconditional. INTERPRETATION: at the limits, a SIGKILL at 30 s would fall around steps 9 to 11 with no flatten, or steps 6 to 9 with one, before the entry cancels, the SHUTDOWN marker, the checkpoint and the close; the daily self-exit is not subject to `TimeoutStopSec` (systemd is not stopping the unit), so the limit applies to operator or supervisor stops and to host shutdown; the typical stop is short. The real durations are UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (section 6.2).
- **Evidence:** the stop methods read in each owner module (runtime part); the wait sites above.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (the documented budget assumes the steps after the drain fit in about 15 s; their maxima do not) and UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (actual timing; whether any SIGKILL occurred; the systemd version). FACT (the bounds); INTERPRETATION (the SIGKILL consequences). W-06 (flow 10) is merged here.
- **Owner:** A01 (the sequence); A17 (the unit, a declaration only); the owners of each `stop()` (A06 for SP4's docstring against its code).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; R15's first half is answered by evidence; the rest is for the post-AUDIT discussion.

#### C19 — Threads not stopped or joined, and work after the close (W-05 in part and W-21 merged)

- **Area and flow:** area 11; shutdown ownership; activity during and after `_shutdown` (flow 9).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** FIX-057 / FIX-131 comment L1814 (blame 30-May-2026): "WAL checkpoint after all threads joined, before close/exit"; L1816: "PASSIVE: safe with any remaining readers". The store docstring `core/state_store.py` L251 to L258 (17-Apr-2026): "Other threads' connections remain open until they close themselves ... in normal shutdown, each thread should call close() before exiting. For the main thread, call this last during shutdown." (CD-17). SPEC X.3 (`docs/v2_design_spec.md` L877 to L882; 2026-04-17): stop the background threads, `eod_squareoff.stop()` first, then the reconciler, the monitor, the feed and the fund manager; X.5 (L887): "Release process lock + close state_store + exit(0)". HISTORICAL: BLOCKER #2 (`docs/web_claude/03_audit_responses/mega_memory_and_fix_plan.txt` L163 to L167; 2026-04-17): "Add candle_store.stop() in shutdown sequence"; `ops_dashboard/docs/G3_0_CONTROL_PLANE_INVESTIGATION.md` L87 to L89 (2026-07-03): "clean shutdown path (threads joined, DB flushed)" (IC-58). Unit L22 to L23: "DB flush". F32 (C14).
- **Actual code path:** `_shutdown` L1599 to L1831 (L1814 to L1831 for the checkpoint and the close); `core/state_store.py` L190 to L264 (per-thread connections through `threading.local`); `main()`'s `finally` L2187 to L2190.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: three pollers have no stop interface at all: the EOD poller `while True` (`orders/eod_squareoff.py` L300 to L306), the MIS auto-square-off poller (`orders/mis_autosquareoff.py` L401 to L408) and the MIS pre-pass poller (`alerts/mis_squareoff_notifier.py` L266 to L272). Never stopped by `_shutdown`: the waitress webhook server and the health server; the gate-release pool (L865 to L867); the bus async executors (`EventBus.shutdown`, `core/events.py` L347 to L351, is called only in `tests/unit/test_events.py`, against its own docstring, L348: "Call at process exit"; M-10, merged here). Signalled but not joined: the helper threads (they leave within 30 s); the in-flight sweeper (a flag only, `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L1172 to L1173); the candle-store timer (an event only, `data/candle_store.py` L120 to L122); the live-feed consumer (only the watchdog is joined, `data/live_feed.py` L153 to L167). `main.py` joins no thread itself; the only joins are inside some owners' `stop()`. FACT (W-21): `close()` closes the calling thread's connection only (L259 to L264); no worker module calls `store.close()` (grep: only `main.py` and scripts do); so the close at L1828 and those at the guard returns close the main thread's connection only. INTERPRETATION: during and after `_shutdown` the unstopped pollers keep running and, if their time arrives during the stop, the EOD and MIS pollers can place square-off orders through the shared adapter; after the close the other threads keep their own connections and can read and write until interpreter exit, and the PASSIVE checkpoint can run while they write. Still possibly running after the close (W-05): the pollers; both servers (their requests read the store); the sweeper until its next check; the candle timer (one more tick can persist a candle through `_persist_candle`, L3927 to L3944); the feed consumer and the ticker; the entry-gate and smart-TGT executor tasks (`shutdown(wait=False)`, `screening/entry_gate.py` L254, `orders/smart_tgt_manager.py` L219); threads whose joins timed out; the helper threads; the gate-release pool and the bus executors with any queued task; the non-daemon flatten. INTERPRETATION (CPython 3.9+): after `_main_locked` returns, `main()`'s `finally` releases the instance lock before interpreter exit joins the executor workers (after their queued tasks) and the flatten thread: a lock-free tail.
- **Evidence:** The runtime part's AST thread scan (W-07); greps for `.close()` on stores, for `EventBus.shutdown` and for `def stop` in the poller modules.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT (the gaps; the comment at L1814 does not describe the code; the store's per-thread contract is not followed); INTERPRETATION (the effects). Not a KNOWN DEFECT: condition (a) fails for the L1814 text (a comment mismatch) and for the missing stop interfaces (absences, not mechanisms), and condition (b) fails for the effects, which depend on timing. The STUDY's wording "run against a closed store" is corrected (Appendix A). W-05 (flow 9, in part), W-21 and M-10 are merged here.
- **Owner:** A01 (shutdown ownership); A12 (the EOD and MIS pollers have no stop interface); A14 (the MIS notifier); A03 (the per-thread close; the checkpoint with live writers). The lock-free tail touches the instance lock (B-28 and C31, cross-part).
- **Rama:** not needed; treatment later.

#### C20 — Shutdown order against startup order (W-05 in part merged)

- **Area and flow:** area 11; `_shutdown` steps 5 to 24.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** the docstring L1627 (17-Apr-2026): "Reverse-order shutdown (MAIN15)"; MAIN15 (module_34 L299 to L318): "Clean shutdown order (reverse of start)": processor, gate, webhook, smart TGT, reconciler, monitor, feed, "System stopping", SHUTDOWN, close, log; MAIN11's start order (L237 to L252): feed, monitor, reconciler, gate, processor, smart TGT, webhook. BLOCKER #5 (plan L184 to L188; 2026-04-17): start the processor before the gate (nothing on the stop order). H-16 (`docs/web_claude/03_audit_responses/trading_system_v2_audit_report.md` L2491 to L2520; 2026-04-21): stop the webhook receiver before the processor. SPEC X.1 to X.3 (L870 to L882): webhook 503 first, drain signals for 10 s, then the background threads "in reverse init order". BLOCKER #2: stop the candle store. FIX-129 Item 45 (L1765 to L1766): cancel entries before stopping the monitor. L3972 to L3973: the gate "may call continue_from_gate() immediately on release; workers must be ready". The sources conflict on what "reverse" means (IC-55).
- **Actual code path:** L1684 to L1794; L1805 to L1812 (the SHUTDOWN insert).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the core chain follows MAIN15's ordered list (processor, gate, smart TGT, reconciler, monitor, feed, notify, SHUTDOWN, close, log), with each documented amendment in place: H-16 (webhook first), FIX-060 (the event), M-C8 (the drain), FIX-129 (the cancel before the monitor stop), BLOCKER #2 (the candle store after the feed), FIX-057 (the checkpoint). MAIN15's list was the reverse of MAIN11's start order; BLOCKER #5 swapped the start order in the first commit (L3972, blame `bcf03b5`) and left the stop order unchanged, so "reverse" has been untrue for the processor and gate pair since then; later additions (TGT retry; the gate, smart TGT and TGT retry trio) stop in forward order. FACT: the STUDY's hazard (a gate release handed to a stopped processor) is closed in code: `continue_from_gate` aborts the placement once the processor's stop event is set (`signals/signal_processor.py` L2215 to L2222, FIX-165e, 13-Jun-2026; the event is set by `stop()`, L333); and nothing outside the tests calls `EntryGate.add()` (grep), so only `gate_state` rows restored at boot can reach the path (the "DORMANT today" comment at L2236 to L2238 is dated 10-Sep-2026, after the last recorded deployment; the grep is the evidence). INTERPRETATION: for the reconciler-before-cancel and exit-managers-before-monitor pairs no dependency was found (the cancel uses the order monitor's own watch list, `broker/order_monitor.py` L546 to L566). FACT: the SHUTDOWN insert is a separate statement after the stops (L1805 to L1812), not "inside the same transaction as the final state mutations" (SPEC X.4, L884 to L890); which mutations SPEC meant is not established (INTERPRETATION; A03).
- **Evidence:** the code and documents above; `git blame` of L3972 and L1627.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (documentation only). FACT. No functional hazard is established from the code.
- **Owner:** A01; A06 and A07 for the processor and gate behaviour; A03 for SPEC X.4's transaction.
- **Rama:** not needed.

#### C23 — The helper threads (pre-alert, margin re-sync) and the fixed alert texts

- **Area and flow:** area 10; L900 to L1039; the alert texts at L699, L716 to L718 and L747 and the daily-loss text at L767 and L786 to L787 are recorded here and handed to A13 ((e) and (g)).

FIX-164 (L982): "at 09:15 IST (market open)". Against hard-coded times (IC-49): P1 (`docs/locked_decisions.yaml` L222; HISTORICAL, an architecture point attributed to the system owner, RS-05): "All times sourced from core/market_windows.py — single source of truth, no hardcoded times anywhere else. Configurable via config/system_config.yaml"; FRULES 1.4 (`docs/foundation_engineering_rules.md`; 2026-08-06); the configuration's own precedent (`config/system_config.yaml` L105 to L106: the stop time "was a hardcoded 16:00 in main.py, against Foundation Rule 1.4"). Ledger #9 (03-Aug-2026): `main.py` L707 to L714 ("⛔ Do NOT restore 'will close all positions' ... would tell the operator, every afternoon, that positions which deliberately survive are about to be closed") and the header of `tests/unit/test_kill_alerts_delivery_carveout.py` (03-Sep-2026; document only): alerts must not claim that EOD closes delivery (CNC) positions (IC-50). File values: `market_open` "09:15" (L103); `eod_squareoff_time` "15:17" (L49); `mis_squareoff_cutoff` "15:09" with a first check 6 minutes earlier (L59, L90); `circuit_breaker.force_close_time` "15:15" and `max_api_failures` 3 (L740 to L741). `tests/unit/test_fix130_eod_pre_alert.py` L96 to L107 (2026-06-08; document only): no raise with `notifier=None`.

- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** (a) FACT: the literals 14:45 (L943) and 09:15 (L994) do not read the configuration or the special sessions; with the file values the pre-alert comes about 32 minutes before 15:17 and about 18 minutes before the first MIS check, and its texts say "~30 min" (L912, L922). (b) FACT: the pre-alert query (L903 to L907) lists every OPEN trade with fills, including DELIVERY carries, which EOD does not square off (L707 to L713), as "force-squared in ~30 min" (L912), and omits PARTIAL trades; INTERPRETATION: this is the false-claim class that ledger #9 removed from the 15:15 alerts, and the 14:45 alert was not covered. (c) FACT: L926 logs "sent" when `notifier` is None; INTERPRETATION: unreachable in `main.py`'s flow, where the notifier is always constructed (L2628) and passed (L4103); only the tests reach it. (d) FACT: the two wait loops (L962 to L963, L1009 to L1010) do not clamp the sleep, as the self-exit does with `max(1.0, ...)` (L1400, L1560); INTERPRETATION (CPython rejects a negative sleep with `ValueError`): if the wall clock passes the target between the loop test and the argument read, the thread ends unhandled before firing, and nothing notices; that needs the clock to pass `end` between those two reads (a clock step, or a 30 s sleep that ends within microseconds of `end`), so it is rare. (e) FACT (handed off to A13, with A14 for the text and A04 for the order monitor's literal; section 8): the force-close and API-failure texts carry a fixed "15:15", "15:17" and "3 consecutive" (L699, L716 to L718, L747) while the deciding values are configurable; the order monitor's authentication path uses a literal 3 (`broker/order_monitor.py` L648); at the baseline the texts agree with the file values. (f) FACT: both helper threads ignore special sessions. FACT (AST): the pre-alert's local `today` (L948) is unused. (g) FACT (handed off to A13, with A12 for the EOD exit filter and A14 for the text; section 8): the daily-loss callback's Telegram body (L786 to L787, lines 2026-05-30, `0cd1b14`) says "Closing all positions" and "Market-close all positions", and its docstring (L767, the same commit) says that `fire_now` "market-closes all positions", while `eod.fire_now` runs the EOD exit pass, which reads only the open intraday positions (`orders/eod_squareoff.py` L1063: `get_open_intraday_positions()`, trades whose ENTRY leg is MIS or CO, `core/state_store.py` L1207 to L1210), so delivery carries are excluded first, and whose broker filter adds to that by keeping only `EMERGENCY_FLATTEN_PRODUCTS` {MIS, CO} (L1073 to L1087: "DELIVERY (CNC/NRML) positions are never touched"); delivery is on at the file values (`config/system_config.yaml` L142, L149, L155). INTERPRETATION: whenever a CNC carry is open at a daily-loss breach the claim is false, the false-claim class that ledger #9 (L707 to L714; `tests/unit/test_kill_alerts_delivery_carveout.py` L9 to L19, which covers only the two 15:15 alerts) removed elsewhere (IC-50).
- **Evidence:** the code above; the commit message; the configuration at `0cc0009` (`eod_squareoff_time: "15:17"`).
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE, through (a) to (d) and (f). FACT; INTERPRETATION for (b), (c) and (d); sources conflict (IC-50 for the delivery listing; IC-49 for the fixed times); OPEN QUESTION for the special-session and PARTIAL parts (R17). Parts (e) and (g) are texts inside `_make_force_close_cb`, `_make_api_failure_hard_kill_cb` and `_make_daily_loss_cb`, which the approved index assigns to A13 ("the `main.py` failure callbacks"); they are handed off (section 8) and not counted as A01 ISSUE parts, and (g) takes its part of IC-50 with it. Not a KNOWN DEFECT: (a) and (b) rest on sources that conflict over time (condition (d) fails); (c) is unreachable in `main.py`'s flow (condition (c) fails); (d) depends on timing (condition (b) fails); and (f) has no mechanism for special sessions (condition (a) fails).
- **Owner:** A01 (the helpers: (a) to (d) and (f)); A13 (the failure callbacks: (e) and (g), handed off); A14 (alert texts); A12 (EOD timing; the EOD exit filter of (g)); A04 (the order monitor's literal in (e)).
- **Rama:** R17 (partly answered by evidence; the special-session and PARTIAL parts are open and can wait, with R11).

#### C24a — Rollover past midnight

- **Area and flow:** area 10; the self-exit thread, L1329 to L1563.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** L1342 (16-Jul-2026): "after the service-window end, exit cleanly once flat". FIX-189 L1043 to L1051 (19-Jun-2026): "sets the shutdown event once we are past the service-window end ... AND positions are flat ... clean overnight + clean morning start"; `docs/SYSTEM_MAP.md` L2016 to L2031 (the changelog of `d61ece9`, 19-Jun-2026): exit 0, no systemd restart, the token watcher's exit-0-today path skips, a "clean overnight and clean 08:30 start". L1080 to L1086 (25-Aug-2026; HISTORICAL attribution "Option A, Rama 25-Aug-2026"): a unit still `active` at 08:15 means "token_watcher read 'running — nothing to do', NO BOOT happened, the 15:15 SOFT_KILL never auto-cleared, and the next day took NO ENTRIES IN BOTH BOOKS", described as a failure. No source states the intended behaviour of a process that is still up after midnight.
- **Actual code path:** the target is computed on the start date (L1393 to L1397); the due test compares the time of day only (`_eod_self_exit_due` L1270: `if now.time() < window_end: return (False, -1)`).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: from 00:00 until `window_end` (file 17:35) the decision function returns `(False, -1)` without querying. FACT: the day-scoped values (L2255, L2266) are not renewed, and each one-shot helper fires at most once per process (L967, L1005 to L1007); FACT (AST): the `market_windows` parameter (L1334) is never read. INTERPRETATION: a process held up past `window_end` (for example by a conflicting or unresolved position, C26, or by a running flatten) and still up at midnight cannot self-exit before the next `window_end`, even after it becomes flat; at 08:15 the unit is `active`, `deploy/token_watcher.sh` L140 to L142 does nothing for an active unit (A04), and no boot follows: the outcome the 25-Aug comment describes.
- **Evidence:** the cited lines; the runtime part's AST scan of unused parameters.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT (not due from 00:00 until `window_end`); INTERPRETATION (the next-morning consequence, which depends on the token watcher and on a process surviving past midnight). Not a KNOWN DEFECT: condition (d) fails, as no source states the behaviour after midnight, and condition (c) is not met, as the harmful outcome depends on an external starter and on a process surviving past midnight.
- **Owner:** A01 (A04 and A17 for the token watcher and the unit).
- **Rama:** no intent question is needed to record it (no STUDY question covers it); treatment for the post-AUDIT discussion (Appendix A, notes).

#### C25 — No worker supervision and no kill-driven stop (W-04's worker-death result merged)

- **Area and flow:** area 9; the runtime wait (L4249), the kill callbacks and worker liveness (flow 8).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** stay up during kills: MAIN13 (module_34 L283 to L290; 2026-04-17: the main thread waits; "All actual work happens in background threads"); MAIN18 (L336 to L357: the callbacks soft-kill and alert); L4015 to L4020 (27-Jul-2026): setting the event "converts 'no new entries, all protection still running' into 'nothing running at all'"; the M-C8 design (the self-exit waits for the flatten, and the flatten worker is non-daemon so that the process does not tear down under it, `capital/kill_switch.py` L1189 to L1192); `README.md` L52 to L53 (2026-07-16) and `docs/decisions/11_absolute_kill_ladder.md` L58 to L69 (2026-07-28): SOFT blocks entries and allows exits; HARD blocks all orders and dispatches an async flatten that retries for up to 2 h (HISTORICAL: Rama's in-session description, SOFT "stop taking new trades / raise warnings" and HARD "emergency stop", recorded there as accurate; neither says the process ends); HISTORICAL operator procedures of 18-Apr-2026 (IS-9.14): each kill is followed by a `/health` call to read the kill state (RUNBOOK T16R01 and T17R01, sections 6.1 and 6.2), a soft kill keeps managing open positions (PLAYBOOK P080, section 7.1), and the live failure protocol soft-kills and then shuts down as a separate step (PLAN T8R01, section 5.4), which INTERPRETATION reads as a process that keeps running after a kill; the paper hard kill that "just stops everything" (PLAYBOOK P084, section 7.2) does not say whether the process ends; the dashboard's "Full Trading Stop" (`ops_dashboard/frontend/templates/controls.html` L267, A15, line 2026-08-17; IS-9.15): "Halts trading for the rest of the day". Against it (IC-48): SPEC 4.6 (`docs/v2_design_spec.md` L856 to L868; 2026-04-17): a main loop every 5 s that checks the kill switch ("if HARD_KILLED → break loop") and logs a heartbeat; SPEC C.8 (L736 to L740): "Verify all background threads are alive" at readiness; PRULES 11 (`docs/trading_system_project_specific_rules.md` L103 to L109; 2026-04-17): the error-type row "Critical", with the action "Stop system immediately" (L109), which does not mention a kill (INTERPRETATION: it may mean stopping trading; IC-48 (A)). Supervision: E-4, L4056 to L4059 (08-Jul-2026): the liveness of the order monitor, the reconciler and the EOD poller gates `/health`; the 24-Jun post-mortem, L4077 to L4079: TGT retry on `/health` "so a dead/crash-looping worker can't sit unnoticed for days again"; `broker/order_monitor.py` L524 to L527: the self-stop is "visible to external uptime monitors"; L1540 to L1544 (09-Aug-2026): an exception escaping the self-exit thread would mean "the service would then never self-exit at all"; FRULES 1.6 and 4 (`docs/foundation_engineering_rules.md` L44 to L53, L229 to L250; 2026-08-06): "No silent failures"; HISTORICAL: `docs/SYSTEM_MAP.md` L196 (17-Jul-2026: a liveness probe watches the unit's state during [09:00, 16:00) on trading days) and `docs/audit/integrity_audit_2026.md` L3126 (2026-08-01: a hung-but-active process has "NO external detector").
- **Actual code path:** L4249 (a bare wait); `_daemon_liveness` L4060 to L4071, passed at L4081.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: no kill path and no worker failure sets the event or exits (W-04: the setters are L1525, L1573 and L1629 only). `/health` covers three gating workers, the feed connection and the TGT-retry provider. Not covered: the signal-processor dispatcher, the entry-gate poll, the smart-TGT executor, the token monitor, the clock-skew probe, the MIS pollers, the webhook server, the sweeper, the candle timer, the self-exit thread, the two helpers and the optional workers; the death of the webhook server or of the self-exit thread after boot has no surface at all; a hung but alive process shows nothing (no heartbeat, W-10).
- **Evidence:** grep for every setter of the event; the liveness function; the owner modules' self-stop sites (`broker/order_monitor.py` L648 to L653, L677 to L688). FACT (searched at `37f6ba5` and at the baseline): the April documents send their kills to `/kill-switch` and `/hard-kill` on port 5000, and no such route exists in non-test code at either commit (at `37f6ba5` the webhook receiver has only `/health` and `/webhook/<scanner_name>`, `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L111, L123 of that commit); the kill controls and their routes are A13's, A06's and A15's and are not audited here.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE for coverage (against the 24-Jun post-mortem's stated aim, E-4 and FRULES 1.6) and for the stay-up design, which resolves IC-48 one way with no later source that settles it (section 1.6): MAIN13 (`module_34_main.txt` L283 to L290) says only that the signal handlers set the event, the main thread waits and a heartbeat is logged; the later documents (README L52 to L53, decision 11 L58 to L69, the service-window analysis L11 to L13, the April operations documents dated one day after SPEC, and the dashboard's kill text of 17-Aug-2026) describe kill behaviour, or a process that keeps answering after a kill, without stating that a kill must not stop the process and without naming or replacing SPEC 4.6; the nearest statement of intent, L4013 to L4020 (27-Jul-2026), concerns a webhook failure; SPEC 4.6 (L861 to L868) and C.8 say the opposite, and PRULES 11 L109 may (an error-type table row, "Critical" with "Stop system immediately", that does not mention a kill; INTERPRETATION: it may mean stop trading, IC-48 (A)); INTERPRETATION: the later flatten design needs the process to stay up. FACT; OPEN QUESTION (R13, narrowed). W-04's worker-death result is merged here.
- **Owner:** A01 (A15 for `/health`; A04 for the order monitor's self-stop).
- **Rama:** R13 (OPEN QUESTION, narrowed: was a kill meant to stop the process; section 9); whether more workers were meant to be on `/health` is a minor open point. Both can wait.

#### C28 — The webhook self-check

- **Area and flow:** areas 4 and 9; L4002 to L4054.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** HISTORICAL: MAIN11 (module_34 L254 to L259; 2026-04-17): "sleep(2) # let Flask bind ... if not result.reachable: log CRITICAL, exit 1"; P17 (`docs/locked_decisions.yaml` L370 to L376): the startup check includes "own webhook endpoint reachable via local /health curl", "CRITICAL on any failure"; SPEC C.1 (L653 to L659): "Telegram CRITICAL + exit(1)". The 27-Jul-2026 decision (L4007 to L4033; commit `14305e1`, 27-Jul-2026): "DEGRADE, do not block the boot", with an out-of-band alarm ("sentinel -> alert_watcher -> email"), superseding the exit (IC-33). `utils/startup_checks.py` L789 to L809: 2xx or 401 counts as reachable. The sentinel context's own key `"detail"` (L4050) shows an intent to record a detail, read through `getattr(..., None)`.
- **Actual code path:** L4003 (a fixed 2 s sleep), L4004 (a loopback URL on `bind_port`), L4005 (`check_webhook_endpoint`, a 5 s check, `utils/startup_checks.py` L787), L4006 to L4054 (CRITICAL and the sentinel); `WebhookEndpointResult` (`utils/startup_checks.py` L137 to L142).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: if unreachable, CRITICAL and a sentinel, and the boot continues. FACT: the sentinel reads `getattr(wh_result, "detail", None)` (L4050), but the frozen dataclass has only `reachable`, `status_code` and `response_body` and never had a `detail` field (`git log -S`), so the value is always None; the status code is logged by the check (L811 to L814) but is not in the sentinel. FACT: no retry; no liveness of the webhook server after boot (C25); the health port is a literal 8080 (L4075). INTERPRETATION: the server binds `bind_host` (file value: an all-interfaces bind address, `config/system_config.yaml` L328) while the check uses the loopback address; it would misreport only for a specific non-loopback bind host.
- **Evidence:** the cited lines; `git log -S` for `detail:` in `utils/startup_checks.py` (no hit) and for the `getattr` line (added by `14305e1`); tests (documents only): `test_main.py` L916 to L935 (the webhook check is performed).
- **Result and classification:** MATCH for the 2 s settle (MAIN11) and for the degrade (the 27-Jul decision, which supersedes MAIN11's and SPEC C.1's exit and meets P17's CRITICAL); ISSUE (minor) for the `detail` read, which is certain to be None, and for the lack of detection after boot. FACT. Not a KNOWN DEFECT: condition (d) fails: the code's own `getattr` default admits None as an outcome, so the only intent source for the field does not clearly exclude it (Appendix A, notes).
- **Owner:** A01 (A06 for the server; A14 for the sentinel).
- **Rama:** not needed.

#### C29 — Event-bus coverage and semantics

- **Area and flow:** `core/events.py`; every publish and subscribe across the repository; startup wiring (L3846 to L3866).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** `core/events.py` docstring L1 to L43 (2026-05-19); class docstring L242 to L260 (FIX-003 async); `docs/locked_decisions.yaml` G9 L158 to L166 and EV1 to EV6 L533 to L605 (2026-07-22); `capital/drift_handler.py` DH6 L45 to L50 (2026-08-01) and `orders/order_manager.py` L149 to L153 (2026-07-26): subscribers swallow their own errors because the bus raises to the publisher; H-7 comment L3863 to L3865: EOD recovery publishes only after the subscriptions; `tests/unit/test_events.py` header L4 to L11 (2026-05-12); MAIN9 (`module_34_main.txt` L222 to L229: five subscriptions, "if differ, wire the actual handler methods"), superseded with a recorded reason at L3860 to L3861.
- **Actual code path:** `core/events.py` `EventBus` L241 to L351 (`subscribe` L268 to L298, `publish` L300 to L345); `main.py` L3847, L3858; the publish sites in M-07.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** (a) "Events published before the two `main.py` subscriptions, such as kill-switch transitions at L2260, L2265 and L2291, reach no `main.py` handler": FACT, narrowed: `clear_stale_state` (`capital/kill_switch.py` L315 to L364) and `auto_clear_scheduled_kill` (L366 to L429) publish nothing (they persist, audit to `system_events` through `_record_cleared_kill` L443 to L478, and log); `_publish_event` is called only from `soft_kill` (L599), `hard_kill` (L746) and `resume` (L785); so of the three only L2291 publishes before L3847. FACT: a second pre-subscription path exists: a rehydrate invariant failure at L2815 calls `_handle_invariant_violation` (`capital/fund_manager.py` L1887) and so `hard_kill` (L2495) before L3847, then exit 3 (L2822 to L2825). With no subscriber the publish is a no-op (`core/events.py` L318 to L322). INTERPRETATION: the only loss is the duplicate CRITICAL line of `_log_kill_switch_event` (L1586 to L1592); the kill switch's own log lines remain. FACT: no other event type can be published before its subscriptions. (b) "`EventDispatchError` is never caught by name": FACT (AST: no non-test handler; the only `except TradingSystemError` is L4288); eleven of the fourteen non-test publish sites are inside a broad try, and none re-raises (M-07, M-08). (c) "The order monitor publishes some events without a try" (A04's code; handed off, section 8): FACT: `broker/order_monitor.py` L999 (`OrderFilled`) and L1037 (`OrderPartiallyTerminated`); `_poll_cycle` catches and logs (L623 to L630); L1334 is inside a try (consequences in M-08). (d) "The bus has no lock": FACT (L262 to L345); INTERPRETATION (CPython): no consequence is established, because every `subscribe` call runs in the main thread during construction and the last one (L3858) precedes the first publish from any worker thread. (e) "Dispatches on the exact type": FACT (L315); every event class derives directly from `Event` and nothing subscribes to `Event`, so no consequence at the baseline. (f) "`InstrumentsRefreshed` has no subscriber" (a pointer: the finding is counted in M-07; `reload` and `gc_sweep` are A05's, section 8): FACT, widened: its only publisher `InstrumentCache.reload` (`core/instrument_cache.py` L188 to L215) has no non-test caller; its documented consumer `CandleStore.gc_sweep` (`data/candle_store.py` L133 to L168) is neither subscribed nor called; and the event carries only `token_count`, while `gc_sweep` needs the set of valid tokens: the FIX-092 chain is unwired. (g) "The docstring's EV1 and EV6 do not describe the code": FACT, widened (M-05, M-12).
- **Evidence:** `git grep -n '\.publish\(|\.subscribe\('` (non-test), every event-class construction, the kill-switch publish sites, the AST handler scan, the H-7 ordering, MAIN9 and L3846 to L3866 (re-read for this AUDIT), the tests named above as documents.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE: (b) the raise informs no publisher decision, failures are only logged, by the bus and again by the publisher; (g); (c) handed to A04 and (f) counted in M-07; (a) ISSUE, minor, after narrowing (one duplicate log line lost); (d), (e) MATCH (no consequence established). FACT (code); INTERPRETATION (consequences in a, c, d).
- **Owner:** A01 (the bus, the event types, the two `main.py` subscriptions); A04 (order-monitor publish consequences), A05 (`gc_sweep`, `reload`), A09 (drift handler), A13 (kill-switch publishing).
- **Rama:** not needed; the results are established by evidence (bears on R13 and R24).

#### C30 — Clock-skew tiers and the NTP check

- **Area and flow:** time-authority thresholds and callbacks; the startup NTP check (AUDIT direction flow 11).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** G4 (`docs/locked_decisions.yaml` L80 to L89): "<2s normal; 2-5s warn; 5-30s alert + continue trading; >30s halt. Startup NTP check refuses to start if skew >30s"; rejected alternative "Hybrid + log-only on skew (rejected: turns a safety check into a notification)"; configuration comments `config/system_config.yaml` L184 to L187 ("G4 tier 1: log warning", "G4 tier 2: Telegram alert, continue trading", "tier 3: fire on_critical_skew -> soft_kill"; 2026-09-10); `core/time_authority.py` defaults comments L67 to L70 and Inputs L26 ("Callbacks via configure(on_critical_skew=..., ...) (wired by main.py)"); `broker/clock_skew_probe.py` L135 to L137 ("soft_kill for HALT, Telegram for ALERT, log for WARN"; 2026-04-19); `docs/CONFIG_GUIDE.md` L429 to L430 (2026-07-28: "log → Telegram alert → SOFT_KILL. Refuses to start if startup skew > 30 s"); P2 Config Honor (`docs/locked_decisions.yaml` L255 to L261: every configured limit has a runtime check and an alert; no "namesake" values). Silent on WARN and ALERT (not contrary): MAIN21 (`module_34_main.txt` L372 to L375: "Applies configured skew thresholds. Registers critical-skew callback."), the `_init_time_authority` docstring L361 and the time-authority Outputs L29 to L32 (which describe the critical callback). HISTORICAL: PRELIVE BL-21 (`docs/web_claude/03_audit_responses/trading_system_v2_audit_report.md` L3526 to L3529, 2026-04-21): `record_broker_skew` had no caller and runtime drift monitoring was "dormant"; at the baseline the probe calls it. NTP: FIX-129 Item 27 comment `utils/startup_checks.py` L1630 to L1631 ("warn_sec=2, block_sec=5 per spec. Best-effort: NTP unreachable → skipped, not blocking") and L538.
- **Actual code path:** `_init_time_authority` L360 to L378 (called at L2254) passes four thresholds and only `on_critical_skew`; `core/time_authority.py` `configure` L125 to L164, `record_broker_skew` L171 to L256 (no logging in the module); the sole runtime caller `broker/clock_skew_probe.py` L138 to L141 (result discarded; live only, built L3905 to L3918; `clock.probe.enabled: true` in the file, `config/system_config.yaml` L188 to L190); `check_ntp_sync` `utils/startup_checks.py` L506 to L574, called at L1632 to L1641.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the WARN and ALERT callbacks stay `None` (no other non-test caller of `configure`), so an average skew in the WARN or ALERT band produces no log line and no alert (L243 to L248 fire only callbacks that exist); HALT calls `soft_kill` on every qualifying sample (no hysteresis; repeated soft-kill handling is A13's). File values equal the defaults (2.0, 5.0, 30.0, 30.0). `SkewResult.callback_fired` (L255) is set when a tier is crossed, whether or not a callback exists, so it reports True for every WARN or ALERT sample although nothing fired; its only caller discards it. The docstring (L14 to L15, L177 to L178) says the adapter records skew after every broker call; the adapter does not (grep). NTP: one UDP attempt to a hard-coded public pool host, thresholds 2 s and 5 s hard-coded, no round-trip compensation (`time.time()` read after receipt, L547); unreachable gives `passed=True, skipped=True`, a log warning (L536) and no warning entry (L1638 to L1641); `report.ntp` has no reader (grep). The `check_ntp_sync` docstring (L520 to L523) says the warn band gives `passed=False`; the code gives `passed=True` (L549); the aggregate's own warn test (L1640) makes the effective result the intended warning.
- **Evidence:** code read (L360 to L378 and `core/time_authority.py` L24 to L42, L64 to L73, L222 to L256 re-read for this AUDIT); grep for callers of `configure`, `record_broker_skew`, `report.ntp`; `tests/unit/test_time_authority.py` L162 to L193 (2026-07-14) shows the WARN and ALERT callbacks firing when a test wires them; no test covers `main.py`'s wiring of these tiers.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT: the unwired tiers depart from intent established by evidence (R21(a)); `callback_fired`, the adapter claim and the NTP docstring are text and naming differences. Not a KNOWN DEFECT: condition (a) fails: the tiers are not wired, so no mechanism on the path fails; the outcome is a requirement the wiring omits, which this AUDIT classifies ISSUE / FACT (as C10e). The entry part's reason ("sources conflict" with MAIN21) is restated: MAIN21 and the Outputs are silent on WARN and ALERT, not contrary. OPEN QUESTION: the NTP thresholds (G4's refusal above 30 s against FIX-129's block at 5 s "per spec"; R21(b)).
- **Owner:** A01 (time authority, wiring, startup check); A04 for the probe and `get_server_time` (`broker/zerodha_adapter.py` L1709 to L1728: HTTP Date header with a local-clock fallback; the probe's "Honest Caveat" L19 to L26 predates it), A13 for soft-kill semantics, A14 for alert delivery.
- **Rama:** R21(b) (NTP host and thresholds); part (a) is answered by evidence; can wait.

#### C31 — The instance lock: PrivateTmp, fixed port, port squatting, ordering, legacy helpers

- **Area and flow:** the single-instance guard (AUDIT direction flow 12).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** `utils/instance_lock.py` docstring L4 to L39 (2026-07-17): two instances "would double every order"; the guard "must equally never REFUSE a legitimate restart"; X5: layer 1 is a kernel-owned lock, "authoritative" (L133 to L135); FIX-081 layer 2 binds a lock port "(default 5001)"; L141 to L142: the port is "configurable via system_config.yaml or passed explicitly"; L55: "Configurable via acquire_instance_lock(lock_port=...)"; FRULES 1.4 (no hard-coded settings); `docs/audit/batch3_done_17jul2026.md` L191 to L196 and L226 to L227 (2026-07-17: a wrongly refusing lock exits 1, which "3 4" restarts, so "the system silently does not trade"; "a different lock_port does not bypass the guard (layer 1 is authoritative)"); `tests/unit/test_instance_lock.py` L1 to L15 and L207 to L214 (2026-07-27: the file lock must refuse a second start "on its own, before the port is even considered"); `docs/SYSTEM_MAP.md` L905 to L907 (2026-09-02): "the lock is now OS-owned ... as well as the port, so a standalone `main.py` is refused by BOTH layers", and `clear_kill_switch.py` "takes no instance lock"; `deploy/resume.sh` L4 to L7 and `scripts/clear_kill_switch.py` L5 to L9 (2026-06-18: a standalone `main.py --resume` "competes with trading-system.service for the instance lock (port 5001) -- the 18-Jun collision", HISTORICAL); v2 spec Phase 0a (`docs/web_claude/00_source_documents/v2_design_spec.md` L557 to L559, 2026-04-17: lock first; another instance → error, exit 1); FRULES 3.6 (L203 to L214: lock file; "Already running"). PrivateTmp: no source mentions its effect on the lock (grep); the original unit set it as "Security hardening" (STEP3 L115 to L119). HISTORICAL operator procedure of 18-Apr-2026 (IS-3.15): before running `main.py` by hand on the first paper day the operator stops the service and checks that it is inactive (RUNBOOK T4R06, section 1.2; PLAYBOOK T3R08, Gate A), and checks that port 5000, the webhook port, is free (RUNBOOK T4R07; IC-74, B-32). This settles that the April procedure avoided a hand-run copy beside the service by stopping the service; it does not say whether the lock was meant to refuse such a copy, since it predates the instance lock itself (FACT: `utils/instance_lock.py` was added by `ab2c6d2` on 05-May-2026, and `37f6ba5` has no lock), X5 (17-Jul) and the SYSTEM_MAP claim.
- **Actual code path:** L2183 (`acquire_instance_lock()`, no argument); `utils/instance_lock.py` L50 to L51 (the lock file in the system temporary directory on Linux), L129 to L202; unit L46 `PrivateTmp=true`.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the port is always 5001; no configuration key exists (grep of `config/`); layer 1 is the file `trading-system.lock` in the temporary directory; layer 2 binds the loopback address on 5001 and calls `listen(1)`. INTERPRETATION (systemd): under `PrivateTmp` the service sees its own private temporary directory, so a copy started by hand locks a different inode and only the port refuses it (consistent with the 18-Jun collision, HISTORICAL); layer 1, described as authoritative, never sees an out-of-namespace copy, and inside the namespace systemd already allows one main process; SYSTEM_MAP's "refused by BOTH layers" therefore does not hold for a hand-run copy beside the declared service. INTERPRETATION (kernel bind semantics): any process listening on port 5001 (on the loopback address or on all interfaces) makes the lock's bind fail; FACT: a failed bind refuses the start with exit 1 (`utils/instance_lock.py` L188 to L200; L2184 to L2186); INTERPRETATION: under the declared unit that is a 10-second restart loop, the outage the docstring's P2 property names; the X5 tests use dedicated ports and do not cover a squatted 5001. FACT: logging is set up before the lock (L2178 against L2183), so a refused copy writes to the shared log files (C5). FACT: `check_port_available` (`utils/instance_lock.py` L241 to L257) is imported at L95 and never called outside the tests (the L3980 to L3981 comment says it was removed as TOCTOU-prone); `_pid_is_alive` (L68 to L85) is no longer called inside the module and is reused by `scripts/reconstruct_excursions.py` L364 to L365 (2026-09-09) for a PID lock, the method X5 replaced because PIDs are recycled (L17 to L24). FACT (grep, for this AUDIT): `scripts/clear_kill_switch.py` imports no lock, so `docs/audit/service_window_extension_2026-07-24.md` L30 (2026-07-25: the lock is also acquired by that script) is wrong (IC-24). Effective configured port: none configured; 5001 by default. No other configured service uses 5001 (webhook 5000, `config/system_config.yaml` L335; health 8080 literal).
- **Evidence:** module read in full (entry part); grep for `lock_port`, `5001`, `PrivateTmp`, `trading-system.lock`, `check_port_available`, `_pid_is_alive`, and the lock import in `scripts/clear_kill_switch.py`; no reader of the lock file outside the module and the tests; SYSTEM_MAP L895 to L912, the lock docstring L14 to L40 and the batch-3 record read for this AUDIT.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT (code and declarations; the fixed port against the docstring L141 to L142 and FRULES 1.4); INTERPRETATION (PrivateTmp and restart semantics); OPEN QUESTION (R22 part 2, narrowed: the documented design states that layer 1 refuses a standalone copy by itself, and no source considered `PrivateTmp`; the April procedure stopped the service before a hand run, which leaves the question open).
- **Owner:** A01; A17 for the unit; the excursion script's owner for its own lock (cross-reference).
- **Rama:** R22 part 2; part 1 is answered by evidence (configurable intended); can wait.

#### C32 — Boot latency

- **Area and flow:** area 4; Phase 0d, the aggregate's network steps.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** intended behaviour not established: no source sets a boot-time limit. Related statements: SC12, "Does NOT abort on first failure (collect everything)" (module_33 L237 to L239; runner docstring L1543 to L1544); SC7, timeout 10.0 (module_33 L137); FIX-D, a delay for "network stabilization" (L668, L675; `config/system_config.yaml` L753); the MIS notifier docstring: "A slow SMTP at 08:15 would otherwise delay the service's own startup" (L239 to L240); `main.py` comment L1976 ("08:15 token-refresh cron -> 08:30 premarket start"; HISTORICAL schedule); the declared unit's `Type=simple` (L7; INTERPRETATION: systemd does not wait for readiness, so no start timeout applies); HISTORICAL (IS-4.27): the April operations documents expected the readiness signals within about 10 s of the interactive confirmation (RUNBOOK T5R08; PLAYBOOK P031), a description of the expected output of the code of that date, not a limit set on the boot.
- **Actual code path:** `utils/startup_checks.py` L652 to L745 (scanners), L475 to L503 (NTP), L414 to L468 (clock), L1282 to L1284 (strategies); `_http_fetch` L235 to L261.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: all sixteen steps run even after a blocking result. The scanner step sleeps `scanner_check_delay_sec` (file 5.0, L753) and then fetches 16 URLs in turn (`config/scan_webhook_map.yaml`, 16 entries) with a 10.0 s timeout each (L657, L702); the NTP step makes one UDP query with a 5.0 s socket timeout; the clock check makes one Kite call with the client's timeout `timeouts.read_sec` (file 10, `config/broker_limits.yaml` L33); the strategy files are loaded here and again at L3159. INTERPRETATION: if every scanner fetch times out, the scanner step alone takes at least 5 + 16 × 10 = 165 s (urllib's timeout applies per blocking socket operation, and name resolution is not bounded by it, so the real maximum can be higher); because of C6 the scanner step runs on every boot, the first of the day included; its result is a warning only, which `main.py` does not read (S-44); in an outage the clock check fails first, but the boot still waits for the remaining steps before exiting 3.
- **Evidence:** the cited lines. HISTORICAL: `docs/audit/wave7_nine_investigation_22jul2026.md` L40 quotes a boot log with the second detection at 08:15:21.959 and the aggregate's OK line at 08:15:29 (about 7 s with every scanner reachable); a legacy record's statement, not runtime evidence available to this project.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (a design concern; intended behaviour not established). INTERPRETATION (the latency arithmetic); UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (production durations; section 6.2).
- **Owner:** A01; A06 (the scanner map); A04 (the client timeout).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; linked to R6 (the COLD skip).

#### C33 — The session row's content

- **Area and flow:** area 4; the early session row (L2304 to L2314), its Phase 0h rewrite (L4174 to L4180) and the row's consumers.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** MAIN12 (module_34 L261 to L281; 2026-04-17): `store.upsert_session(session_date, mode, last_config_hash=json.dumps(app_config.file_hashes), kill_state=..., ...)`, with no account or trade-type field. MED #10 (L86 to L99): write the row earlier; "Cold start detection still works because kill_state and last_shutdown_ts tell the story" (IC-37). The schema (`core/schema.sql` L478 to L501; 2026-08-23): `account_id TEXT NOT NULL`, `mode -- PAPER | LIVE`, `trade_type -- INTRADAY | POSITIONAL`, `last_config_hash`, `session_start`, `last_updated`; CFG-7 dropped `kill_state`, `kill_reason`, `kill_time` and `kill_type` (L488 to L493). `main.py` comment L2307: "The Phase 0h call below will UPDATE this row with the final config_hash." `_write_session` docstring L621: "INSERT OR REPLACE the single session row (id=1)". Configuration `trade_type: BOTH` (L149) (IC-37). HISTORICAL consumers: `reports/daily_report.py` L136 to L138 (2026-07-25) takes the account from the row; `ops_dashboard/backend/services/summary_bar.py` L35 to L39 (2026-07-25): "prefer the account-roster values ... so a placeholder session row (e.g. account_id="default" off-market) never leaks into the header", while it shows the row's `trade_type` (L42); `ops_dashboard/backend/services/config_view.py` L410 to L411 (2026-09-02) prefers the W0 snapshot's mode and trade type. No source states the literals (IC-37 for MAIN12).
- **Actual code path:** `_write_session` L619 to L633, called at L2308 and L4174.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the row is `(1, session_date, 'default', broker, MODE, 'INTRADAY', config_hash, now, now)`, with broker "zerodha" in live and MODE `mode.upper()`. Both calls pass the same `json.dumps(app_config.file_hashes)` (the configuration is loaded once, L2198), so the Phase 0h write has no different ("final") hash to write. INTERPRETATION (SQLite semantics): INSERT OR REPLACE replaces the whole row, so `session_start` is reset at each write, twice per boot. The daily report's account field reads 'default' (L138, passed on at L194); the dashboard header substitutes the roster's account but shows `trade_type` 'INTRADAY'. INTERPRETATION: the dashboard comment's "off-market" does not match the code, which always writes 'default'. FACT: the MED #10 premise does not hold at the baseline (`kill_state` dropped; no `last_shutdown_ts` column); detection uses the kill switch's own table and SHUTDOWN events.
- **Evidence:** the cited lines; `ops_dashboard/backend/readers/db_reader.py` L122 to L130 (2026-08-18) reads the row's columns.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT; INTERPRETATION (SQLite semantics); HISTORICAL (the consumer workaround); OPEN QUESTION (R20).
- **Owner:** A01 (`_write_session` and its calls; B-14 covers its wiring, cross-part); A03 (schema); A15 (the report and the dashboard).
- **Rama:** R20 (OPEN; can wait).

#### C34 — Logging at exit and inside the signal handler

- **Area and flow:** log delivery at the end of the process.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** `core/logger.py` `shutdown_logging` docstring L462 to L466 (FIX-099, 2026-07-17): "Called during shutdown to ensure all queued log records are written to disk before the process exits"; MAIN15 step 12 (`module_34_main.txt` L317: log "shutdown complete"); comment L112 (`_log` used in the top-level exception handler). HISTORICAL operator procedure of 18-Apr-2026 (IS-11.23): press Ctrl+C once, wait for "Graceful shutdown initiated...", wait up to 30 s for "Shutdown complete", and "Do NOT Ctrl+C twice" (RUNBOOK T8R07, section 4, step 34; PLAYBOOK P059 to P061, section 5.2); the documents give no reason for the last rule.
- **Actual code path:** `setup_logging` (L2178; `core/logger.py` L435 to L445: file handlers behind a `queue.Queue(maxsize=10000)` and a `QueueListener`; L419 to L421: a stdout handler, WARNING and above, synchronous); the guard L4288 to L4293; the signal handler L1571 to L1573.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: no non-test code calls `shutdown_logging` or `logging.shutdown`, or registers an `atexit` hook (grep). INTERPRETATION (standard library): the `logging` module registers its own `atexit` call to `logging.shutdown`, which flushes and closes the registered handlers but does not stop the `QueueListener`; the listener thread is a daemon and is not stopped, so records still queued at exit may never reach the files; WARNING-and-above records, including the guard's final CRITICAL, also go synchronously to stdout (the journal, per unit L38 to L39), so the exposure is to the file sinks and to INFO lines such as "Shutdown complete". The signal handler logs at INFO before setting the event (L1572 to L1573); the Python logging documentation warns that logging may not be usable from signal handlers because its locks are not re-entrant, and the queue's mutex is a plain lock, so a signal arriving while the main thread is inside a logging call could deadlock the handler; the main thread logs during late startup (L3958 to L4249), then only waits, and logs again inside `_shutdown`, so a second SIGINT during the shutdown is the main exposure. W-20 (the runtime part), merged here, adds the mechanism: a Python signal handler runs in the main thread between bytecodes, and `queue.Queue.put` (`core/logger.py` L431 to L452) holds a non-reentrant lock, so a deadlock there would leave the process to a SIGKILL. FACT (for the operations documents): the code logs "Signal %s received -- initiating shutdown" (L1572), "Shutdown initiated" (L1630) and "Shutdown complete" (L1831); no "Graceful shutdown initiated" text exists in non-test code (searched); all three are INFO lines, and the stdout handler takes WARNING and above only (`core/logger.py` L419 to L421; the same at `37f6ba5`, L311 to L312), so INTERPRETATION: an operator watching the terminal of a hand run sees none of them there, only in the log file. FACT: the handler (L1571 to L1573) only logs and sets the event, `_shutdown` has one call site (L4252), and no code restores the default SIGINT handler (grep). INTERPRETATION (Python signal semantics): a second SIGINT after L3958 runs the same handler again, adding one INFO line and setting an event that is already set, so a second Ctrl+C neither repeats nor cuts short the shutdown (C21b); its only exposure is the logging-lock window above, and the documents' "do not press Ctrl+C twice" is consistent with that exposure.
- **Evidence:** code read; grep (entry part); W-20's reading of `core/logger.py` L431 to L452; grep of non-test code for the documents' log texts.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT (no call; the logger's own docstring says it is called during shutdown; the April documents' "Graceful shutdown initiated" line does not exist, HISTORICAL); INTERPRETATION (record loss; the deadlock window, a possible runtime risk; the lines a terminal shows). Not a KNOWN DEFECT: condition (a) fails: the outcome is an omitted call (no mechanism), with an uncertain effect. Whether records were lost: UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Owner:** A01 (the exit path does not call it); A15 owns the logger.
- **Rama:** not needed.

#### C35 — Special sessions (merges C10f)

- **Area and flow:** configured market windows and special sessions (AUDIT direction flow 11).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** FIX-094: `core/market_windows.py` L45 to L47 ("Supports special sessions (e.g. Muhurat trading)") and L96 to L97 ("Special session dates are NOT considered holidays, even if they fall on weekends (e.g. Muhurat trading on Diwali Saturday)"; 2026-06-16); commit `ee91659` (17-May-2026, FIX-094 message: special sessions override hours and EOD; "Special session dates NOT considered holidays (exempts weekend check)"; `next_trading_day` recognises them); `config/system_config.yaml` L114 to L122 ("Uncomment and configure when special session is scheduled"; a commented evening Muhurat example 18:15 to 19:15, EOD 19:12) and L86 to L89 (`special_sessions` moves `eod_squareoff_time` per date but not the MIS cutoff); `docs/CONFIG_GUIDE.md` L206 to L211 (2026-07-28: special sessions); `config/nse_holidays_2026.yaml` L5 to L8 (2026-04-17: Muhurat on 08-Nov-2026, a Sunday; "System treats this as holiday by default. Operator can override if Muhurat session participation is desired"). Against: SU6 and `utils/holiday_guard.py` L11 (a weekend is never a trading day); the FIX-189 START cutoff 18:15 (L2001) and the schema bound `service_window_end < 18:15` (`core/config_loader.py` L83, L179 to L188).
- **Actual code path:** `_load_special_sessions` L446 to L470 (called unguarded at L2318); `MarketWindows` `core/market_windows.py` L75 to L89, L93 to L106, L132 to L150, L177 to L205; the holiday guard L2101 to L2152; the START guard L2157 to L2176.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: `_load_special_sessions` parses `YYYY-MM-DD` keys and three `HH:MM` values with no validation of order or range and promises nothing about malformed entries (docstring L447 to L453; A02 types the block as an optional mapping of strings, `core/config_loader.py` L1992); a malformed key, a missing key or a bad time raises and exits 2 after the lock and inside the START window (INTERPRETATION: a 10-second restart loop until 18:15, then exit 0). `is_market_open` and `is_eod_squareoff_due` follow a special session; `is_entry_allowed` and `is_past_eod_entry_cutoff` do not (regular 10:00 to 15:00 entry and 15:15 cut-off at file values), so no entry is allowed in an evening session. FACT: the holiday guard ignores special sessions, so a start on a weekend special-session date exits 0 at L2150 before `MarketWindows` is built; no CLI option or variable bypasses the holiday guard; the commented evening example lies at and after the START cutoff and after every legal configured stop. INTERPRETATION: the "operator can override" path that the holiday file promises does not exist in the code; an evening session cannot be served by the process (it would self-exit at the configured stop once flat, 17:35 at file values). File value: no session configured (comments only). INTERPRETATION: the commented key is unquoted, which YAML reads as a date rather than a string; whether A02's schema accepts it is A02's.
- **Evidence:** code read (L446 to L470 re-read for this AUDIT); configuration and holiday file read; `tests/unit/test_market_windows.py` L325 to L401 (2026-06-29) cover open, EOD and next-open overrides, not entry windows or a weekend start through `main()`.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT; INTERPRETATION; sources conflict (FIX-094 and the holiday file against SU6 and FIX-189), so not a KNOWN DEFECT (condition (d) fails); the malformed-entry crash is an absence of validation, not a mechanism that fails (condition (a) fails). OPEN QUESTION (R11).
- **Owner:** A01 (window logic, guard, wiring); A02 (schema); A12 for the MIS cutoff; A16 for the cron schedule and A04 for the weekday token refresh (cross-reference).
- **Rama:** R11; can wait.
