#### C41 — A second synchronous reconcile after the kill check (W-03's second-pass result merged)

- **Area and flow:** area 8; Phase 0f to Phase 0g (flow 7).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** MAIN10 (module_34 L231 to L235; 2026-04-17): one synchronous startup reconcile, then "if kill_switch.is_active(): log CRITICAL, exit 1"; MAIN11 (L240): "order_reconciler.start()  # poll thread + event subscription". A second specification of a synchronous reconcile inside `start()`: RC1 and RC14 (`docs/locked_decisions.yaml` L2205 to L2212, L2338 to L2344; file 2026-07-22): `start()` "calls reconcile_once() synchronously before starting the daemon thread", because the "Crash-recovery SL check (G5b) must run before the first trade tick"; `docs/web_claude/01_module_instructions/module_25_order_reconciler.txt` RC13 (L188 to L192; 2026-04-17); M33 L22 to L23 ("Reconciler runs reconcile_once synchronously before poll thread starts (enforced by caller, not here)"); the reconciler's own docstring (`orders/order_reconciler.py` L424 to L426: "RC14: run startup reconciliation, subscribe events, start poll thread") and L441 ("Startup reconcile before trading begins"). The BUILD 1 comment L3869 to L3879 (24-Jun-2026): the synchronous reconcile must precede the processor and the webhook; "The ~15s figure is the BACKGROUND reconciler poll (the 2nd+ reconcile), not this first one". G1 (`docs/locked_decisions.yaml` L34 to L44): unrecoverable discrepancies lead to "halt" (the kill switch: INTERPRETATION). No source says whether the second pass needs a kill check (IC-47).
- **Actual code path:** the first pass at L3880, before `order_monitor.start()` (L3960) and before the rehydrates (L3952, L3955); the kill check L3894 to L3899; L3961 `start()`: L428 the CNC check, L435 to L439 the GTT reconcile (inside its own try), L442 the second `reconcile_once()`, L445 to L451 the poll thread; the processor starts at L3974 and the webhook at L4000.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: no kill check follows the second pass. INTERPRETATION: a kill state set by the second pass, or by the GTT reconcile, does not stop the boot; the process sends "System Active" and runs with entries blocked (soft) or all orders blocked and the flatten dispatched (hard). INTERPRETATION (systemd semantics and the STUDY's detection reading): the same condition in the first pass gives exit 1, a restart after 10 s and HALT (exit 4) on the next boot. INTERPRETATION: the second pass sees different state, because it runs alongside the order monitor's poll thread and after its rehydration (A12 and A04 to assess). FACT: the first pass and its kill check MATCH MAIN10 and BUILD 1 (W-03).
- **Evidence:** the cited lines; `tests/unit/test_main.py` L881 to L892 (kill active after the reconcile gives 1; document only).
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT (the sequence; the missing check); INTERPRETATION (the consequence); OPEN QUESTION (R24); sources conflict (IC-47). W-03's second-pass result is merged here.
- **Owner:** A01 (the sequence); A12 (what the second pass can change); A13 (kill effects).
- **Rama:** R24 (OPEN; can wait until A12 and A13 establish what the second pass can change).

#### B-16 — Interactive helpers and step order against SU7 to SU15 (L1838 to L1946)

Exact phrase required"), SU15 L329 to L338; M41C SU12 L241 to L257 ("Exact phrase only. Anything else: exit 7 or re-prompt"); RDISC L186 to L190 ("Confirm start in LIVE mode? [y/N]", live only); the code docstring L1930 to L1931 (SU12 "Unified confirm screen for both paper and live modes ... Exits 7 on 'n'"); `module_34_main.txt` L496 ("ASCII-only print() strings" for Windows). HISTORICAL operator procedure of 18-Apr-2026 (IS-1.20; IC-76): the prompts in order are the account, the mode, a trade type (intraday or positional) and a y/n confirmation of the three, in paper too (RUNBOOK T5R04 to T5R07, section 2, steps 17 to 20; PLAYBOOK P025 to P028, section 3.1, which lists the first three); no token step, as the token is made on the PC beforehand.

FACT (added at the follow-up): no trade-type prompt exists in `main.py` at the baseline or at `37f6ba5`, whose order was the account, the token check or login, the mode, then the live-only exact phrase (L751 to L764 and L514 to L534 of that commit); the April documents' y/n confirmation in paper agrees with the code's unified confirmation (IC-02).

FACT; sources conflict on SU12 (design documents against the code docstring and the tests) and on the step order (both SU texts against the code comment L2529, and the April operations documents against both, IC-76); HISTORICAL (the trade-type prompt of the April documents, which no version of the code examined has); INTERPRETATION for Ctrl+C and encoding; all latent because of C1.

- **Rama:** not needed now (latent); part of R1.

#### B-28 — Lock released before the process ends (L2187 to L2190)

- **Area and flow:** instance-lock lifetime (AUDIT direction flow 12).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** `utils/instance_lock.py` L57 to L58 ("Held open for the whole process lifetime — closing it releases the lock, which is exactly what must not happen early") and L133 to L138 ("held for the process lifetime"; 2026-07-17); against them, the `release_instance_lock` docstring L222 to L226 ("on clean shutdown ... releases them promptly"); the STUDY's startup-trace row M8 also says "held for the process lifetime" (Appendix A).
- **Actual code path:** `main()` `try/finally` L2187 to L2190; `release_instance_lock` L220 to L238.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: both layers are released when `_main_locked` returns or raises, before `main()` returns to the guard. INTERPRETATION (CPython): interpreter finalisation then joins non-daemon threads (the kill switch's flatten worker, `capital/kill_switch.py` L1193 to L1196, when running) and the executor workers after their queued tasks (the gate-release pool, the bus executors); daemon threads started by a failed boot also run until exit. During that interval the old process can still act while the lock is free. INTERPRETATION (systemd): the unit stays active or deactivating until the main process exits, so systemd itself starts no second copy; a copy started by hand could.
- **Evidence:** reads (L2182 to L2190 re-read for this AUDIT); the STUDY's workers file (flatten worker non-daemon) re-checked at the cited lines by the entry part.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT for the release point against "held for the process lifetime"; INTERPRETATION for the consequences; sources conflict inside the module (lifetime against prompt release).
- **Owner:** A01; A13 (flatten worker), A07 and A06 (gate-release tasks).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; part of R22's design question.

#### B-32 — FIX-081 removed the webhook-port pre-check on the premise that the lock socket covers it

- **Area and flow:** port use (flow 12) at the webhook boundary.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** comment L3980 to L3981 ("Port conflict check removed — acquire_instance_lock() already binds a persistent socket lock. Separate check_port_available() was TOCTOU-prone"); `utils/instance_lock.py` L12 to L13. FIX-081 has no commit subject; it is described only in code comments and `docs/audit/batch3_done_17jul2026.md` (evidence file, section 3.1). HISTORICAL operator procedure of 18-Apr-2026, before any code pre-check (IS-3.15; IC-74): the operator checks by hand that nothing listens on port 5000 before `main.py` starts (RUNBOOK T4R07, section 1.2, step 13), while the playbook's Gate A asks for a `/health` answer on port 5000 before the launch (PLAYBOOK T3R09), which INTERPRETATION needs a process already on the port; the conflict is recorded, not resolved.
- **Actual code path:** HISTORICAL: `ab2c6d2` (05-May-2026) added `check_port_available(wh_cfg.bind_port, wh_cfg.bind_host)`; `39110d8` (16-May-2026, "Phase 17 audit fixes batch") added the lock port and removed that check. At the baseline the lock binds 5001 (L2183) and the webhook binds the configured 5000 on all interfaces (`config/system_config.yaml` L328, L335; L3988 to L3999).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the lock reserves 5001, not the webhook port. INTERPRETATION: a foreign listener on 5000 is no longer detected before the server thread starts; it surfaces only through the post-start webhook self-check (sentinel; the boot continues; C28), and the self-check counts a 2xx or 401 answer as reachable (`utils/startup_checks.py` L807 to L809), so a foreign listener that answers that way passes it; the server thread's bind traceback goes to stderr (the journal under the declared unit).
- **Evidence:** `git log -S` and `git show` (read-only, entry part); reads.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT (ports); INTERPRETATION (consequence); HISTORICAL (commits; the April manual check, IC-74, which does not bear on the code's premise, so the result is unchanged).
- **Owner:** A01 wiring; A06 webhook server; C28 cross-reference.
- **Rama:** not needed.

#### S-11 — A detected configuration change is lost when the boot ends between L2308 and L2656

- **Area and flow:** area 4 (flow 3): L2295 detects the change, L2308 stores the new hashes, L2656 to L2675 record and alert it.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** CL4 (`docs/locked_decisions.yaml` L761 to L770): the hashes are "Used by Phase 0b to detect config changes between restarts and queue a Telegram alert"; MAIN6 (module_34 L78 to L82): "if result.changed: write CONFIG_DIFF system_event. Alert operator via telegram WARN"; the comment L2655. MED #10 places the row write early for a different purpose (cold-start detection) and does not address the hash. FRULES 1.2 (`docs/foundation_engineering_rules.md` L19 to L23): "Re-runs must be safe."
- **Actual code path:** L2295, L2308 to L2314, L2655 to L2675.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT (code order): every exit between L2308 and L2656 (the returns at L2370 and L2374 (6), L2423 (3), L2482 (`--dry-run`, 0 or 3), L2502 (3), L2552 (6) and L2572 (9); the assert at L2506 (2); any uncaught exception, for example C2's (1 or 2)) leaves the row holding the new hashes, with no `CONFIG_DIFF` event and no alert; the next boot's L2295 compares against those hashes and reports no change. INTERPRETATION: a configuration change that coincides with a failed boot, or with an operator's `--dry-run`, is never recorded as `CONFIG_DIFF` and never alerted; the W0 snapshot (L2602; per date and hash, A02) is taken by the next boot that reaches it.
- **Evidence:** the cited lines; no test drives this sequence (the `main.py` tests mock the checks).
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE; KNOWN DEFECT, for the `--dry-run` case and for a failed boot alike. The four conditions of section 1.6 hold: (a) the code has a mechanism for the case, CL4's detection at L2295 followed by the deferred `CONFIG_DIFF` event and WARN alert at L2656 to L2675; (b) by Python's control flow, once L2308 has stored the new hashes, any exit before L2656 (the `--dry-run` return at L2482; the failure exits listed above) makes the event and the alert impossible for certain, and the next boot's comparison finds no change; (c) a configuration change before a `--dry-run` (a documented option: MAIN17, help L202) or before a boot that then fails is an ordinary case on the documented set-up, and CL4 and MAIN6 say the boot handles it (MAIN6 puts the `CONFIG_DIFF` write in phase 0c, inside MAIN17's "Phases 0a-0d"); (d) the outcome (the change is never recorded as `CONFIG_DIFF` and never alerted; only `check_config_hash`'s INFO line, `utils/startup_checks.py` L624 to L627, mentions it) contradicts CL4 and MAIN6, and no source supports it (MED #10 concerns cold-start detection, not the hash). FACT (the order); INTERPRETATION (the consequence). Whether it occurred in production: UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED. A new item (not among C1 to C41).
- **Owner:** A01 (A02 for the hashes).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; post-AUDIT discussion.

#### S-39 — The aggregate runner: steps, classification, skips and error propagation

- **Area and flow:** area 4 (flow 13); `run_all_startup_checks` (`utils/startup_checks.py` L1508 to L1692).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** the runner's docstring (L1528 to L1545: nine checks, collect all, the caller decides); SC12 (module_33 L212 to L250: blocking files, secrets, clock, HALT; warnings for the hash, the holiday and the scanners).
- **Actual code path:** `utils/startup_checks.py` L1508 to L1692.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT (the startup part's AST and the code): sixteen steps: fifteen distinct `check_*` functions, each called once, plus the scenario detection; no try in the runner. Blocking entries (10): `missing_config_files`, `missing_secrets`, `halt_requires_resume`, `clock_skew`, `instrument_cache_too_small`, `invalid_strategy_configs`, `sdk_version_mismatch`, `ntp_clock_skew`, `insufficient_disk_space`, `db_permission_error`. Warnings (6): `config_hash_changed`, `market_holiday`, `scanner_unreachable`, `holiday_calendar_issues`, `ntp_clock_drift_warning`, `temp_config_values(N)`. Stored without an entry: the webhook result; a skipped NTP result. Skips: scanners on COLD (L1589), the webhook without a URL (L1601), a `None` cache (L1608), the disk without `log_dir` (L1650), the DB permissions without `db_path` (L1657). In `main.py`'s flow the webhook step is always skipped, the scanner step runs except across midnight (C6), the cache step is skipped after a failed pre-load (C9), and the disk and DB steps always run: fifteen of the sixteen steps run (fourteen check functions plus the detection), fourteen when the cache is `None`. Error propagation: an exception from any step ends the runner and, through the unguarded L2451, the boot (exit 1 or 2, no `store.close()`, no alert). Steps whose function has no handler at all: `check_config_files_present`, `check_market_holiday_today`, `check_instrument_cache_size` (`count()`, L936) and `check_required_secrets` (the two directly called checks also have none, and `main.py` catches only `StartupCheckFailed` around them, L2768 to L2778, L2884 to L2889); unguarded parts of the others: the store reads in the detection (L214, L310; L237 is the kill switch's in-memory `status()`, read under its own lock, `capital/kill_switch.py` L533 to L541) and in `check_config_hash` (L594); the imports and `exists()` in `check_strategy_configs` (L1271 to L1276); `exists()` in `check_holiday_calendar` (L1389) and in `check_db_permissions`; `UnicodeDecodeError` in `check_temp_config_values` (only `OSError` is caught, L1486). INTERPRETATION (CPython semantics, read locally; the installed version is not established, B-41): `Path.exists()` propagates errors such as a permission error. FACT and INTERPRETATION (the `UnicodeDecodeError` sub-case): a strategy YAML that is not valid UTF-8 is rejected by step 10, whose `except Exception` (L1282 to L1291) records `invalid_strategy_configs` (blocking: exit 3 on its own); step 14 then reads the same file with `read_text(encoding="utf-8")` (L1477, L1481), and the `UnicodeDecodeError` (a `ValueError`, not an `OSError`, L1486) leaves the runner and the unguarded call at L2451, so the guard exits 2 (L4291 to L4293); `load_all` does not read the strategy files (`core/config_loader.py` L25, L36), so nothing stops such a file earlier. FACT: `main.py` consumes only `ok`, `blocking_failures` and (under `--dry-run`) `warnings`; the measured values (skew, drift, free disk and so on) are only logged.
- **Evidence:** The startup part's AST parse (26 top-level functions; 17 `check_*`; 15 called by the runner; no try node in the runner).
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE; KNOWN DEFECT for the `UnicodeDecodeError` sub-case. The four conditions of section 1.6 hold: (a) the code has a mechanism for the case: step 10's validation, which rejects an invalid strategy file as blocking, inside a runner whose docstring says it collects all results (L1543: "does NOT abort on first failure"), and step 14's handler (`except OSError`, L1486); (b) by Python's semantics `read_text(encoding="utf-8")` raises `UnicodeDecodeError` for such a file, which no handler on the path catches, whenever the file is present; (c) a strategy file that is not valid UTF-8 is a malformed input that step 10 exists to reject, on the documented set-up; (d) the outcome, exit 2 (restarted under the declared unit), contradicts the runner's docstring, MAIN7 and the exit list's specific entry "3 startup check failure (blocking)" (L15); under section 1.6's reading of exit-code sources the general "2 unexpected exception" (L14) does not state the intent for a case that the list names. Whether it occurred in production: UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED. MATCH for "collect all" and for each step's classification by its comment; ISSUE for the stale docstring count (nine against sixteen), the lack of exception isolation and the branches that cannot occur in `main.py`'s flow. FACT; INTERPRETATION.
- **Owner:** A01.
- **Rama:** not needed.

#### W-04 — Flow 8: every path that sets the shutdown event, and every kill or worker path that does not

- **Area and flow:** area 9; the event's setters and non-setters.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** MAIN13; MAIN18; L4015 to L4020; E-4; IC-48: (A) SPEC 4.6 (L861 to L868; a HARD_KILL breaks the main loop), SPEC C.8 and PRULES 11 (L109, an error-type table row, "Critical" with "Stop system immediately", that does not mention a kill; INTERPRETATION: it may mean stop trading, as IC-48 (A) notes), all 2026-04-17; (B) `README.md` L52 to L53 (2026-07-16), decision 11 L58 to L69 (2026-07-28) and `docs/audit/service_window_extension_2026-07-24.md` L11 to L13, which describe kill behaviour without stating that a kill must not stop the process, and, added at the follow-up, the April operations documents (IS-9.14; 2026-04-18: a `/health` call follows each kill, and the live failure protocol shuts down as a separate step after the soft kill; RUNBOOK T16R01, T17R01; PLAN T8R01) and the dashboard's kill text (IS-9.15; `ops_dashboard/frontend/templates/controls.html` L267, 2026-08-17: "Halts trading for the rest of the day"), which INTERPRETATION reads as a process that keeps running after a kill; MAIN13 (`module_34_main.txt` L283 to L290) says only that the signal handlers set the event, the main thread waits and a heartbeat is logged; L4013 to L4020 (27-Jul-2026) concerns a webhook failure.
- **Actual code path:** L1525, L1573, L1629; readers L4249, L2348, L946, L962, L1009, L1398, L1489, L1559.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: setters: the self-exit (after `window_end`, flat, no flatten), the handler (after L3958), `_shutdown`; HISTORICAL: the webhook self-check set it until 27-Jul-2026; no `sys.exit`, `os._exit` or other setter in the trading packages (grep; `utils/instance_lock.py` L80 is a liveness probe with signal 0); no kill path (live feed, force close, order monitor, daily loss, orphan, clock-skew HALT, token expiry, drift handler, reconciler, EOD, fund manager, placer; `KillSwitchActivated` only logs) and no worker exception sets it; INTERPRETATION (POSIX): a signal without a handler (for example SIGHUP for a hand-run process) or a SIGKILL ends the process without `_shutdown`.
- **Evidence:** grep (non-test).
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (changed from MATCH under section 1.6's supersession rule): the code resolves IC-48 one way (no kill stops the process), and no later-dated source settles the conflict (the April operations documents and the dashboard text add to side (B) without naming or replacing SPEC 4.6). FACT (the setters and non-setters); INTERPRETATION (POSIX signals; the later flatten design needs the process to stay up); OPEN QUESTION (R13, narrowed). The worker-death result is merged into C25.
- **Owner:** A01; A13 (the kill paths).
- **Rama:** R13 (OPEN QUESTION, narrowed; can wait).

#### M-06 — The fan-out rationale against the actual subscriber counts

- **Area and flow:** the design precondition for using the bus.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** G9 (`docs/locked_decisions.yaml` L160 to L161): the bus is for "genuine fan-out (3+ subscribers reacting to the same event)"; EV6 rationale L599: "Only events with 3+ real subscribers warrant the EventBus"; `docs/v2_design_spec.md` L126 (2026-04-17): "Tiny EventBus for 4 fan-out events ONLY (G9)".
- **Actual code path:** the non-test subscriptions (M-07).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT (non-test code, Live): `OrderFilled` 2 (placer sync, slippage recorder async); `PositionClosed` 2 (shadow tracker, slippage recorder; the adapter's subscription is paper only); `OrderStatusChanged` 2; `KillSwitchActivated` 1 (a log line); `CapitalDriftDetected` 1; `OrderPartiallyTerminated` 1; `EodSquareoffComplete` 1; `InstrumentsRefreshed` 0: no event type has the three or more Live subscribers that G9's rationale expects. FACT: EV6's "affects: orders/order_monitor.py (subscribes to OrderFilled)" (L605) does not describe the code: `broker/order_monitor.py` publishes `OrderFilled`. INTERPRETATION: the bus is used as a general decoupling mechanism, not under the precondition the design states.
- **Evidence:** the grep map in M-07.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. INTERPRETATION (design intent against use); FACT (counts).
- **Owner:** A01.
- **Rama:** not needed (a design observation; no behaviour depends on it).

#### M-08 — Handler errors and the reachability of `EventDispatchError`

- **Area and flow:** dispatch failures, from subscriber to publisher.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** EV4 (`core/events.py` L18 to L20; `docs/locked_decisions.yaml` L570 to L579: failures loud; the publisher is told by one `EventDispatchError`); DH6 (`capital/drift_handler.py` L45 to L50, 2026-08-01) and the `OrderManager` docstring (`orders/order_manager.py` L149 to L153, 2026-07-26): subscribers must swallow, because the bus raises to the publisher; `tests/unit/test_drift_handler.py` L282 to L291 (2026-04-19).
- **Actual code path:** `core/events.py` L322 to L345; sync subscribers that swallow: `OrderManager` (L154 to L171), the drift handler (L117 to L128); sync subscribers without an outer try: `OrderPlacer._on_order_filled`, `_on_order_partially_terminated` (partly), `ShadowTracker._on_position_closed`; publish sites inside a try: 11 of 14 (M-07); not inside one: the order monitor L999 and L1037, and `reload` L209 (no caller).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT (code, A04's file): if a sync `OrderFilled` subscriber raises, `EventDispatchError` leaves `_handle_complete` after the state-machine transition to COMPLETE (`broker/order_monitor.py` L992) and before `untrack` (L1020); on later cycles the state machine refuses COMPLETE to COMPLETE, `_safe_transition` returns False on its "both states terminal" branch (L1252 to L1262), and `_handle_complete` returns at L993 to L994 without untracking. INTERPRETATION: the order would stay watched (polled every cycle) and `OrderFilled` would never be published again for it. If a sync `OrderPartiallyTerminated` subscriber raises, `_handle_terminal` stops before its transition (L1064). Whether the placer's handlers can raise is A10's; the watch consequences are A04's. FACT (AST): `OrderPlacer._on_order_status_changed` (`orders/order_placer.py` L1914) and `ShadowTracker._on_eod_complete` (`orders/shadow_tracker.py` L415) also have no outer try; their publishers publish inside a try (result unchanged). FACT: `EventDispatchError` cannot reach the `main.py` guard: every main-thread publish (`resume` L2291, `post_wire_init` L3866, the reconciles L3880 and L3961) is inside a publisher-side try.
- **Evidence:** reads at the cited lines (modules part).
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT (paths); INTERPRETATION (the stuck watch entry; A04 and A10 to confirm).
- **Owner:** A01 (EV4 semantics); A04, A10 (consequences).
- **Rama:** not needed.

#### M-10 — The async executors are never shut down

- **Area and flow:** event-bus cleanup at process exit.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** `EventBus.shutdown` docstring `core/events.py` L348: "Shut down all async executor threads. Call at process exit."
- **Actual code path:** `core/events.py` L347 to L351; `_shutdown` L1599 to L1831; the `_shutdown` call L4252 to L4277.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: no non-test call (grep; only `tests/unit/test_events.py` L426 to L505); `_shutdown` does not call it and is not passed the bus. INTERPRETATION (standard-library `concurrent.futures`; Python version UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED, B-41): at interpreter exit the executor workers are joined after running their queued tasks, so queued writes can still run after `store.close()` (L1828), on the executor thread's own connection (the store keeps one connection per thread and `close()` closes only the caller's, `core/state_store.py` L190 to L192, L215 to L225, L251 to L264): after the WAL checkpoint, after "Shutdown complete" and after the lock release (B-28); a failure is logged by the handler at WARNING (`orders/slippage_recorder.py` L151 to L152, L191 to L192). The same finding is C19's.
- **Evidence:** grep; reads.
- **Result and classification:** MERGED into C19 (the same finding, counted there; this record is a pointer). FACT (no call, against the method's own docstring); INTERPRETATION (exit behaviour).
- **Owner:** A01.
- **Rama:** not needed.

#### M-11 — `CapitalDriftDetected` carries four different meanings

- **Area and flow:** the drift event's payload contract.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** class docstring `core/events.py` L166: "Broker capital deviates beyond reconciliation tolerance"; EV6 fields `expected`, `actual`, `delta`; drift handler DH1 and DH2 (`capital/drift_handler.py` L15 to L28): rupees for the escalating sources; CHECK5 emits share quantities.
- **Actual code path:** fund manager FM9 (`capital/fund_manager.py` L1489 to L1494); fund-manager bucket overflow (L1512 to L1517); reconciler CHECK5 (`orders/order_reconciler.py` L2568 to L2573), G3 (L3914 to L3919) and `_check7` (L4048 to L4053).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: FM9 carries rupees (broker against local total); the bucket overflow carries `expected=0.0`, `actual` the most negative bucket and `delta=abs(gap)`; CHECK5 carries share quantities (`source_module="order_reconciler"`); G3 carries rupees; `_check7` carries the fund-manager margin against the ledger sum (`source_module="fund_manager_self_check"`); the event docstring covers G3 and FM9 only. Handed to A09 (section 8), not counted as A01's: FACT (A09's file): every bucket-overflow event has `expected=0.0` by construction, so the handler's defensive warning "escalating drift event with ~zero expected; possible publisher bug" (`capital/drift_handler.py` L164 to L175) fires on every such event. FACT (A09's file): DH1's "Today that's {"fund_manager"}" (L17) and its "CHECK2 ORPHAN_ADOPTION" publisher (L19) do not match the code (three escalating sources, L66 to L70; no CHECK2 publish exists); `main.py` L3850 to L3852 repeats "Only fund_manager-sourced events".
- **Evidence:** reads at the publishers and the handler (modules part).
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE for A01's part: one event type carries four meanings (FM9 and G3 in rupees, the bucket overflow with `expected=0.0`, CHECK5 in share quantities, `_check7` against the ledger), while its docstring covers two. FACT. The drift handler's zero-expected warning and DH1's text are A09's and handed off.
- **Owner:** A01 (the event type); A09 (the handler's internals, handed off), A12 (reconciler publishers).
- **Rama:** not needed.

INTERPRETATION: the adapter's "never the tag" comment does not describe the reconciler's recovery path; the 16-against-20 statements conflict, but the code truncates to 16 everywhere, so the conflict has no code effect; what the broker accepts is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED from the code (A04).

#### M-24 — How the hierarchy decides the exit code 1 or 2

- **Area and flow:** the top-level guard's class mapping (process exit).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** module docstring L13 to L14 ("1 TradingSystemError", "2 unexpected exception"); E1's rationale (the root exists for `main.py`'s safety net); EXC-1 and EXC-2 (state-store and startup failures belong in the tree); `deploy/systemd/trading-system.service` L32 (2026-08-23; A17): "Exit 1/2 = runtime error/crash -- restart"; `core/mis_squareoff_timing.py` L37 (2026-08-28): "The boot must FAIL CLOSED on this"; `core/config_loader.py` L113 to L116 (2026-09-10): "the schema and the runtime cannot disagree".
- **Actual code path:** the guard L4285 to L4293 (`except TradingSystemError` gives 1, `except Exception` gives 2; both log CRITICAL with the traceback, "system_error" and "unexpected_error").
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** Live path; FACT for the classes and sites, INTERPRETATION for reachability. Exit 1 (in the tree): `ConfigMissingError` and `ConfigSchemaError` from the live pre-load L2359 (not caught by its `FileNotFoundError` and `KeyError` handlers); `StateStoreError`, `SchemaVersionMismatch`, `MigrationNotPermitted` from L2233; `ConfigValidationError` from `validate_all` L4164 (it raises on drift even with `strict=False`, `core/config_validator.py` L257 to L266); `RateLimitAbortedError` (`broker/rate_limiter.py` L239) once the shutdown event is set; broker errors from unguarded calls such as L2758. Exit 2 (outside the tree): `MigrationError` from L2233 (`core/state_store.py` L429 to L435 does not wrap it), against EXC-1's stated purpose; `MisSquareoffConfigError` from L3329 or the notifier at L3343 (`alerts/mis_squareoff_notifier.py` L164); the schema validates the same timing with a literal `poll_interval_sec=5` (`core/config_loader.py` L126 to L134) while L3334 and L3345 pass the configured `eod_squareoff.poll_interval_sec`; at the file values (5, margin 20, `config/system_config.yaml` L96, L361) both pass, so this path is latent (A02 and A12 lead). Not reaching the guard: `ConfigContradictionError` (wrapped by Pydantic inside `load_all`; exit 5 at L2199 to L2201), `EffectCompositionError` (paper only, `core/effect_telemetry.py` L204 to L206), `_PipelineReject` (signal-processor thread), the script classes (other processes), `StartupCheckFailed` (only at the two guarded calls L2775 and L2886, exit 3), `CapitalStateInconsistent` (L2822, exit 3), `EventDispatchError` (M-08). A short `accounts.csv` row's `AttributeError` also gives 2 (M-33). INTERPRETATION (systemd semantics, A17 boundary): the declared unit restarts 1 and 2 alike, so the split changes only the log label; a configuration error that escapes the load handler (`MisSquareoffConfigError`) would repeat every 10 s, whereas a load-time configuration error gives 5, which the unit does not restart.
- **Evidence:** AST scan of raises and handlers; reads at the cited lines (modules part).
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. FACT; INTERPRETATION. Not a KNOWN DEFECT: condition (d) fails: for `MigrationError` the exit list has no entry that names the case, and its general entry "2 unexpected exception" (L14) agrees with the guard's mapping of a class outside the tree, so only EXC-1's stated purpose is contradicted; for `MisSquareoffConfigError` the boot fails closed, as `core/mis_squareoff_timing.py` L37 requires (that path is also latent at the file values). The classes outside the tree are a design inconsistency (M-19), and the effect under the declared unit is a log label.
- **Owner:** A01 (the guard mapping); A03 (`MigrationError`), A02 and A12 (MIS timing validation), A17 (unit).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; bears on R4 (section 9).

#### M-30 — NRML at the emergency flatten sites

- **Area and flow:** emergency flatten of a product outside the set.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** `capital/kill_switch.py` L1846 to L1851 (2026-09-03): "A KNOWN non-intraday product (NRML) exits under its own intent (H-5 correctness)"; L1978 to L1981: Kite nets per product, so an exit under another product does not offset the position; `main.py` L1154 to L1160: the system never places NRML, an NRML position "can only arrive from outside", and the emergency sites flatten it loudly; `core/constants.py` L39 to L41 (unknown products flattened loudly, campaign rule "G2"); HISTORICAL decision (added for this AUDIT): `docs/audit/q4_hard_kill_delivery_decision_30jul2026.md` L25 to L31 (Rama, 30-Jul-2026, as quoted there): HARD_KILL flattens only MIS/intraday; CNC survives.
- **Actual code path:** `capital/kill_switch.py` L1852, L1984; `broker/zerodha_adapter.py` L578 to L602; `config/system_config.yaml` L160.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: NRML is outside the set, so the emergency sites flatten it with intent `PRODUCT_TO_INTENT["NRML"]`, which is DELIVERY; the adapter resolves DELIVERY to the configured code "CNC" (L587; config L160), or coerces it to INTRADAY (MIS) when `force_intraday_only` is on (L578 to L584), and refuses CNC when delivery is disabled (L593 to L602). INTERPRETATION (from the kill switch's own netting comment): the exit product is CNC or MIS, never NRML, so such an exit would not offset an NRML position. Whether an NRML position ever existed is UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Evidence:** reads at the sites (modules part).
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE for A01's part: the constant maps NRML to DELIVERY while its comment treats NRML as unrecognised at the emergency sites (IC-73). FACT (the resolution path); INTERPRETATION (the effect, handed to A13 and A04).
- **Owner:** A01 (call site; the many-to-one constant and its comment, IC-73); A13 (the flatten behaviour, handed off), A04 (the resolver).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; candidate for the post-AUDIT discussion (how an unrecognised product was meant to be closed).

#### M-32 — AR1 to AR11 against the code

- **Area and flow:** the account registry's own contract.

FACT (added): IC5's share rule differs: the code checks the sum only when more than one row is enabled (L198 to L202) and with a tolerance of 1e-6, so a single enabled row's share is never checked, while IC5 asks for the sum of enabled shares to equal 1.0 within 0.01. FACT: IC5's other rules are not implemented either: its referenced environment variables are to be set, with a warning if not (`modules_38_39_40_final.txt` L92 to L93; compare IC-41 and `required_startup_secrets`), and its `AccountRow` holds resolved `api_key` and `access_token` values (L95 to L98), where AR2 holds variable names; IC5 and SU2 are both dated 2026-04-17, so neither supersedes the other by date (section 1.6).

#### M-33 — `AccountRegistry.load()` raises `AttributeError` for a short or whitespace-only row (and the exit codes that follow)

- **Area and flow:** the account-registry load at boot (the live pre-load and the second load) and the exit codes that follow.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** `load()` docstring `core/account_registry.py` L98 to L102 (lines 2026-04-17, `bcf03b5`): raises `ConfigMissingError` (file absent) or `ConfigSchemaError` (schema problems); the row handler L148 to L153 turns row parse errors into `ConfigSchemaError` with the line number; the existing record SRC-41 (`02_REPOSITORY_AND_FILE_MAP.md`, HISTORICAL, testing copy, 18-Sep-2026) describes the same contract ("bad row -> ConfigSchemaError").
- **Actual code path:** `AccountRegistry.load` L91 to L211: each cell is read as `raw[col].strip()`, beginning with `raw["enabled"]` (L135), the last column of the header (L110 to L114; the `config/accounts.csv` header); the row handler catches `(KeyError, ValueError)` only (L148); the outer handler catches `(OSError, UnicodeDecodeError)` only (L173). Callers: `main.py` L2359 (the live pre-load; handlers L2364 `FileNotFoundError` and L2371 `KeyError`) and L2414 (the second load; `except Exception`, L2420 to L2423).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** INTERPRETATION (standard-library semantics, re-confirmed for this AUDIT on synthetic text with `csv.DictReader`): a data row with fewer fields than the header, including a line holding only spaces, gives `None` for the missing cells (the default `restval`), so `raw["enabled"].strip()` at L135 raises `AttributeError`, which neither handler in `load()` catches; blank lines are skipped. FACT: at the baseline `config/accounts.csv` has five data rows of ten fields (field counts re-checked; no values copied) and no such line, so the defect is latent with the committed file. FACT (exit codes; L2346 to L2424 re-read): in live, the `AttributeError` leaves the pre-load at L2359 (the handlers L2364 and L2371 do not match), the store is not closed, and the guard exits 2; a missing or otherwise invalid file gives `ConfigMissingError` or `ConfigSchemaError` and exit 1 there; in paper the second load's `except Exception` gives exit 3 either way. The `FileNotFoundError` handler at L2364 cannot be reached from `load()` (it checks existence and wraps `OSError`).
- **Evidence:** `load()` read in full; the header order; the synthetic checks; the callers' handlers.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE. KNOWN DEFECT. The four conditions of section 1.6 hold: (a) `load()` has a mechanism for the case, the row handler that turns row parse errors into `ConfigSchemaError` with the line number (L148 to L153); (b) by Python's semantics a short or whitespace-only row leaves `None` in the missing cells, so `.strip()` at L135 raises `AttributeError`, which neither handler in `load()` catches, whenever such a row is read; (c) a malformed row is a malformed input that this validation exists to reject, and nothing in the documented set-up rules it out; (d) the outcome (an untyped `AttributeError` instead of `ConfigSchemaError` with the line number) contradicts the docstring's raise contract and the evident purpose of the row handler; no source supports it. The exit code that follows (2 in live, with the store left open) is recorded with C2 and is not part of this classification (as in M-24, under the declared unit 1 and 2 differ only in the log label). C2's short-row case is this finding, counted once here. Whether it occurred in production: UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED (the VM's `accounts.csv` is unknown).
- **Owner:** A01. The exit-code consequences are recorded with C2 (one finding with C2's short-row case).
- **Rama:** not needed to classify; bears on R2.

- **Actual code path:** `load()` L179 to L211; `primary()` L215 to L222; `main.py` L2545.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: `load()` requires exactly one primary (L179 to L192) but neither that it be enabled nor that any row be enabled; the service path selects `primary()` (L2545); `enabled` gates only AR11, the capital-share check and the interactive selector (L1844, unreachable at the baseline). FACT: the committed primary row is enabled and is the only enabled row (flags re-checked; no values copied). INTERPRETATION: a primary row marked disabled would still be traded by the headless service; under IC5 it could not be the primary, and a file with no enabled row would be refused.
- **Evidence:** `load()` re-read; IC5 and SU2 read for this AUDIT; the test as a document.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE (changed from the modules part's MATCH). OPEN QUESTION: the sources conflict (IC5 against SU2, AR3, the `enabled` comment and the June test), and none records that IC5's two rules were withdrawn; the code follows SU2, AR3 and the test (FACT).
- **Owner:** A01.
- **Rama:** needed before a final classification: was a primary account marked disabled meant to be refused, or traded by the headless service, and was at least one account meant to be enabled? Latent with the committed file; can wait for the post-AUDIT discussion.

#### M-36 — AR11 applies in live, where the value is unused

- **Area and flow:** load-time validation of `paper_capital`.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** AR11 (`core/account_registry.py` L24: "paper_capital > 0 for enabled accounts (validation at load time)"); SU3 (`consolidated_module_41_with_context.txt` L103 to L104: "Validate paper_capital > 0 for enabled accounts", no mode condition; added for this AUDIT); `AccountRow.paper_capital` comment L66 ("Capital (INR) used in paper mode"); SU2 (`paper_capital` "used in paper mode only").
- **Actual code path:** `core/account_registry.py` L162 to L169; `main.py` L2359, L2414, L2591, L2756, L1915.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: L162 to L169 has no mode parameter, so a live boot is refused (exit 1 at L2359; a paper boot exits 3 at L2414) for an enabled row with `paper_capital <= 0`; in the live trading process `paper_capital` is read only on paper branches (L2591, L2756) and in the unreachable interactive banner (L1915). `scripts/system_manager.py` L162 to L165 uses the first enabled row's `paper_capital` as a fallback capital. FACT: the code matches the letter of AR11 and SU3. INTERPRETATION: a live boot can be refused over a value that only paper uses (a design concern).
- **Evidence:** reads (modules part); SU3 read for this AUDIT.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE, minor (a design concern; the letter of AR11 and SU3 is met). FACT; INTERPRETATION.
- **Owner:** A01.
- **Rama:** not needed.

#### M-37 — AR12 `primary_account_tag`: never-raise contract, cache, fixed path, in-process consumers

- **Area and flow:** the alert-label tag helper and its consumers.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** comment `core/account_registry.py` L249 to L271 and docstring L278 to L292 (`42c07f3`, 09-Sep-2026): never raises; a minimal direct read; caches the result; default path "<repo>/config/accounts.csv"; "UNKNOWN" on any failure.
- **Actual code path:** `core/account_registry.py` L273 to L299, L302 to L318.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the cache is filled with whatever the first default-path call returns, including "UNKNOWN" (L294 to L298), for the life of the process; only the unused `reset_primary_account_tag_cache` clears it. The path is fixed relative to the module file (L308 to L310), so it ignores `--config` and the working directory, unlike `AccountRegistry.load(config_dir / "accounts.csv")` (L2359, L2414). `_read_primary_row` returns the first primary row without the exactly-one rule. In-process consumers: `signals/webhook_receiver.py` L129 to L138 (WR4b: strips a leading tag from scan names; treats "unknown" as no tag, L130 to L133); `core/evidence_contract.py` L462 to L465 (the evidence "arm"; "unknown" counts as an absent identity, L210 to L216, so records are FAILED, not mis-attributed); `alerts/cron_alerts.py` L103 (labels). Other consumers: 15 files, of which `scripts/preflight/base.py` L75 and `scripts/preflight/sentinel.py` L58 are statically reachable from `main.py` through the pre-flight hook, and the rest are separate processes (M-44). INTERPRETATION: a transient read failure at the first call in a process is kept for that whole process; both functional consumers handle the fallback safely.
- **Evidence:** L247 to L360 re-read for this AUDIT; consumer grep (modules part).
- **Result and classification:** MATCH (the never-raise contract and the fallback handling); ISSUE (a permanent cached fallback; a path independent of `--config`). FACT; INTERPRETATION.
- **Owner:** A01; A06, A15 (consumers).
- **Rama:** not needed.

#### M-38 — AR12b env-name helpers return the string "None"

- **Area and flow:** the per-account credential-name helpers used by separate scripts.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** comment `core/account_registry.py` L327 to L344 (09-Sep-2026): never raises; "Unresolvable => "" so each caller keeps its existing empty-key error path instead of meeting a new exception type"; docstrings L348 and L353 ("or """).
- **Actual code path:** `core/account_registry.py` L347 to L360; `_read_primary_row` L302 to L318 returns `{str(k): str(v) ...}` (L315) for the first row whose `is_primary` is truthy (L314).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** INTERPRETATION (standard-library semantics, re-confirmed for this AUDIT): for a short primary row that still holds `is_primary` (the fourth column) but lacks `api_key_env` or `api_secret_env`, the missing cells are `None`, `str(None)` turns them into the string "None", the `.get(key, "")` default never applies because the key exists, and `primary_api_key_env()` and `primary_api_secret_env()` return "None", not "". FACT (callers re-read for this AUDIT): `primary_api_key()` then looks up a variable named "None" and returns "" unless one exists, so the value path keeps the empty-key behaviour; the three direct callers of `primary_api_key_env()` (`scripts/fetch_daily_candles.py` L264, `scripts/gemini_data_integrity_check.py` L147, `scripts/reconstruct_excursions.py` L289) still take their empty-key error path, because their `API_KEY` comes from `primary_api_key()`, and use the name only in the message, which then says "None" instead of the fallback text. FACT: `primary_api_secret_env` and `reset_primary_account_tag_cache` have no caller anywhere, tests included.
- **Evidence:** L247 to L360 read; the callers read; the synthetic check.
- **Result and classification:** ISSUE, classified FACT (changed from the modules part's proposed KNOWN DEFECT, minor): the "None" is certain for such a row, and conditions (a) to (c) hold (the `.get(key, "")` default; `str(None)`; a malformed row that `load()` exists to reject), but condition (d) of section 1.6 fails, as the outcome does not contradict every intent source: the AR12b contract's stated purpose (callers keep their existing empty-key error path) is preserved, and only the variable name printed in their messages is wrong, a text difference that the rule excludes from KNOWN DEFECT by itself. Latent with the committed file; production use of these helpers is UNKNOWN (M-41). The two uncalled helpers: dead code (FACT).
- **Owner:** A01.
- **Rama:** not needed.

The selection rules differ: `primary()` (`main.py` L2545, `scripts/eod_broker_reconcile.py` L436, `scripts/auto_refresh_token.py` L340 default), the first enabled row (`scripts/system_manager.py` L163 to L165, IC5's definition), an id among the enabled rows (`scripts/refresh_instruments.py` L320), `get(id)` (three scripts), the first primary row (AR12, AR12b). - **Result and classification:** ISSUE, minor (changed from the entry part's MATCH because of FRULES 1.4, an intent source that part did not use): MATCH with the header; FACT for the hard-coded path.

#### C22 — The 09:15 re-sync and the drift kill ladder

- **Area and flow:** area 10; the margin-sync thread (A01) → the fund manager (A09) → the drift handler (A09) → the kill switch (A13).
- **Intended behaviour and source:** FIX-164 docstring L982 to L990 (12-Jun-2026): "Re-sync capital from broker at 09:15 IST (market open). Fixes the case where the system starts pre-market with stale Rs 0 margins and the user deposits funds after startup but before 09:15"; commit `9536a14` (12-Jun-2026); the re-sync's own alert L1022 to L1034: severity INFO, "Capital Updated at Market Open". BL-2 comment L3849 to L3852 (19-Apr-2026): "Only fund_manager-sourced events trigger kill_switch escalation"; `capital/drift_handler.py` L66 to L70: `fund_manager` is an escalating source ("FM9 sync_from_broker"). HISTORICAL: `docs/decisions/11_absolute_kill_ladder.md` (2026-07-28; it records a decision attributed to Rama, "Option A, LEAVE AS-IS"): L21 to L23 (keep 1,000 soft and 2,500 hard); L85 to L97 ("A single ≥₹1,000 sample soft-kills; a single ≥₹2,500 sample hard-kills"; `sync_from_broker` has one non-test caller, the 09:15 one-shot); it does not mention deposits. The two sources treat the same delta differently (IC-51); direction is addressed by neither (IC-51). `tests/unit/test_fix164_market_open_margin_sync.py` L96 to L244 (2026-06-12; document only): fires when started before 09:15, skipped after, notifies on change, aborts on shutdown, skips holidays, catches a broker failure.
- **Actual code path:** L973 to L1039 (the thread; L1013 to L1016 call `sync_from_broker(new_capital)`); `capital/fund_manager.py` L1411 to L1494 (new total = broker cash + carry; |delta| > 1.0 produces `CapitalDriftDetected(source_module="fund_manager")`, published after the lock); the handler subscribed at L3858; `capital/drift_handler.py` L141 to L193 (tiers by `abs(delta)`) and L233 to L254 (hard or soft kill). File thresholds 250, 1,000 and 2,500 (`config/system_config.yaml` L726 to L730).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the chain as above; the ladder takes the absolute value. INTERPRETATION from the code: with the file values, a deposit or withdrawal of 1,000 or more between a pre-market start and 09:15 soft-kills at 09:15, and 2,500 or more hard-kills (all orders blocked and the flatten dispatched, A13), while `main.py` sends the same delta as an INFO "Capital Updated at Market Open". FACT: A01's wiring (the thread and the subscription) MATCHes FIX-164's stated purpose and its test.
- **Evidence:** the code and documents above.
- **Result and classification:** MATCH for A01's part: the re-sync thread and its subscription (L973 to L1039, L3858) do what FIX-164 states (L982 to L990; commit `9536a14`) and its test pins. The consequence, the drift ladder's single-sample escalation for a deposit (FACT for the chain; INTERPRETATION for the deposit case), lies in A09's and A13's code and is handed off (section 8), not counted as an A01 ISSUE; R16 (OPEN QUESTION) goes with it.
- **Owner:** A01 (call site and wiring); A09 (the ladder and the sync semantics; the seed-against-re-sync adjustment difference in STUDY C22 is A09's) and A13 (the kill effects), handed off.
- **Rama:** R16 (OPEN; needed to classify the consequence; can wait for the post-AUDIT discussion with A09's audit).

#### C27 — Failure handling in the callbacks (A13's code, A01's wiring)

- **Area and flow:** area 7; L657 to L843 and the wiring at L2729 to L2734, L2930 to L2932 and L3005 to L3010.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** MAIN18 (module_34 L336 to L357; 2026-04-17): log CRITICAL, soft-kill, alert, in that order; FIX-128 Fix D docstring L762 to L773 (30-May-2026): alert, then `fire_now`, then SOFT_KILL; the C.1 rule `capital/fund_manager.py` L1474 to L1478 (blame 26-Apr-2026): publish after releasing the capital lock, because otherwise "a subscriber blocks on a downstream resource (kill_switch, rate_limiter, broker call) while another thread holds that resource and waits on the capital lock". `tests/unit/test_main.py` L552 to L602 (callbacks; document only).
- **Actual code path:** `_make_critical_failure_cb` L657 to L685; `_make_force_close_cb` L688 to L724; `_make_api_failure_hard_kill_cb` L727 to L752; `_make_daily_loss_cb` L756 to L817; `_make_orphan_cb` L820 to L843.
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT: the order and contents MATCH MAIN18 and FIX-128 D. FACT: `soft_kill` is unwrapped at L669, L698, L811 and L827, while `hard_kill` is wrapped (L735 to L741); a raising `soft_kill` skips the alert in three callbacks (L669, L698, L827). FACT (AST: `with self._lock:` spans `capital/fund_manager.py` L1261 to L1368; an RLock, L354): the daily-loss callback runs inside the fund manager's lock and performs `eod.fire_now` (broker calls) and `soft_kill` there; INTERPRETATION: that is the cross-thread lock-order pattern the C.1 rule forbids. FACT: the orphan callback receives (internal id, broker order id) (`broker/order_monitor.py` L874, L1126), so its alert's "Reason" shows a broker order id. FACT: the live-feed lambda builds a new closure on every call (L2930 to L2932).
- **Evidence:** the cited lines; the runtime part's AST of the lock scope.
- **Result and classification:** MATCH for A01's part: the wiring at L2729 to L2734, L2930 to L2932 and L3005 to L3010, whose order and contents match MAIN18 and FIX-128 D. The callbacks' own failure handling (an unwrapped `soft_kill` that skips the alert at L669, L698 and L827) and the fund manager's lock scope (`capital/fund_manager.py` L1261 to L1368, against the C.1 rule; CD-26) are A13's and A09's and are handed off (section 8), not counted as A01 ISSUEs. FACT; INTERPRETATION (the lock-order risk, for A09).
- **Owner:** A01 (call site and wiring); A13 (the callbacks) and A09 (the lock), handed off.
- **Rama:** not needed (for A13 and A09).

#### C38b — `scripts/sr_level_export.py` fails before it reaches the `main.py` helper

- **Area and flow:** area 7 (an imported entry path, STUDY 4.6); the script's builder.
- **Intended behaviour and source:** the script's comment L136 (file 2026-07-12): "Reuse main.py's read-only market-data kite + rate-limited fetch closure."
- **Actual code path:** `scripts/sr_level_export.py` L141 (`InstrumentCache(str(path))`), L142 (`instrument_cache.load()`), L143 (`_build_market_data_kite(is_paper=False, kite_client=None)`); `core/instrument_cache.py` L86 to L99 (`__init__(self, rows)` builds a dictionary from `r.symbol` for each element; `load` is a classmethod that requires `path`).
- **Actual behaviour and effective value:** FACT and INTERPRETATION (Python semantics): L141 passes a string as `rows`, and iterating it yields characters without `.symbol`, so `AttributeError` is raised before L143; if L143 were reached it would return `None`, and every fetch would return an empty list (`main.py` L404 to L405, L429 to L430), as the STUDY states. Whether the script was run: UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- **Evidence:** the cited lines.
- **Result and classification:** HANDED OFF, not counted as an A01 result (section 6.3 note; section 8): the script is not an A01 path, so its owner classifies the failure, which is certain whenever the script runs; A01 keeps the STUDY correction (Appendix A) and, in C40, the script's import of `main`. FACT and INTERPRETATION (Python semantics).
- **Owner:** not A01 (the owner is to be confirmed, probably A08); A01 only for the imported helpers.
- **Rama:** not needed.

## 8. Cross-topic ownership and handoffs

Each row names the A01 finding, the owner topic, what A01 records at the boundary, and what the owner topic must examine when it is audited. A01 does not audit the owner's internals and does not count a handed-off finding twice. The short-row case of C2 is A01's own (M-33), and the findings of S-47 and W-20 are held by C10g and C34.

| Finding (ID) | Owner topic | What A01 records | What the owner must examine |
|---|---|---|---|
| C1 | A04 | the interactive branch dies with `NameError` (L2534) before the login flow; in live the client and adapter are built from the pre-interactive mode (latent) | nothing reachable at the baseline; `run_login_flow` and the token lifecycle if the path is ever examined |
| C2 | A04 | the pre-load's handlers cannot catch `load_token`'s `None` result or a decode error, so a token problem ends with exit 2 | the token-file contract (writers, format, `load_token` returning `None` on any error, SU17); the token watcher's fresh-token gate (`deploy/token_watcher.sh` L14) |
| C3 | A13, A04 | the FIX-062 flag cannot be set; the order monitor's authentication failure goes to the hard-kill factory | the callbacks' contents and the kill type for an authentication failure (CTI L838 says SOFT_KILL; the wiring hard-kills); the token lifecycle after an authentication failure |
| C4 | A17; A04 (watcher file), A13 (restart contract) | the code's exit codes and the declared supervisor treatment of each (unit, watcher) | whether the unit and the watcher were installed (B-40); the restart treatment of 1, 2, 6 and 9; the removed start limit; the watcher's failed-only branch |
| C5 | A03, A13, A14 | `--status` and `--dry-run` take the lock, open the store as the migrator, run the kill auto-clears, may write `CRASH_DETECTED`, write the session row and send the holiday message | A03: migration on a diagnostic open; A13: persistence of the auto-clears and of `resume` from a diagnostic; A14: the holiday send |

## 9. The STUDY's questions R1 to R24

Each question was checked against all admitted evidence before any question goes to Rama (Document 04, section 5.5). Dispositions: ANSWERED BY EVIDENCE 4 (R3, R14, R18, R19); retained as OPEN QUESTION 20, of which 13 are narrowed by the evidence to a remaining part (R1, R2, R4, R5, R7, R8, R9, R10, R13, R15, R17, R21, R22) and 7 remain wholly open (R6, R11, R12, R16, R20, R23, R24); NO LONGER MATERIAL 0. Only R24 (and M-35, which has no STUDY R number) bears on an A01 classification; R16 bears on A09's classification of C22's handed-off consequence; both can wait for the post-AUDIT discussion, and the other open answers bear on impact or treatment. The questions are not sent to Rama now: ChatGPT consolidates them after its review.

|---|---|---|---|---|
