The questions retained as OPEN QUESTION, in plain English (narrowed by the evidence where stated):

- **R1 (narrowed):** After May 2026, was the interactive start (`main.py --interactive`) still used, for live trading or for the recovery steps in `docs/disaster_recovery.md`, or was the service start the only route? The documents already show that it was meant to work, and the April 2026 operations documents show that it was the planned way to start the first paper days and to restart after a new token; only its use after May 2026 is open.
- **R2 (narrowed):** When `accounts.csv` is missing or invalid at a live start, the start ends with exit 1, a general "system error" code; in paper the same problem gives exit 3, which is what the code written for it returns. The list of exit codes has its own entry for invalid configuration, exit 5. Was this case meant to give exit 3 or exit 5? (The token case is answered by the documents: exit 6.)
- **R4 (narrowed):** The documents already say that exit 6 (missing or expired token) should stop the service and call you. Was the service also meant to stay down, rather than restart every 10 seconds, after exit 9 (a broker other than Zerodha in `accounts.csv`) and after failures that repeat on every restart: another program holding port 5001, a database migration refused during market hours, a broken holiday file or special-session entry, a missing token file, a short line in `accounts.csv`? And was the April limit of 3 starts in 300 seconds meant to stay after it was removed on 22-Apr?
- **R5 part 3 (narrowed):** Were `--status` and `--dry-run` meant to be usable while the service is running? Today they are refused at the instance lock. That they were meant to be read-only and to stop on holidays is already answered by the documents.
- **R6.** On the first start of a trading day the system writes today's session record before it runs its full set of start-up checks, so those checks always see a "same-day restart" and never a "fresh start". As a result the scanner-website check, which its code skips on a fresh start, runs on every start, and the repeated scenario check and configuration-change check cannot report anything new. Was the scanner check meant to run on the first start of each day (as a June test plan and a July review say) or to be skipped (as the module instruction and the code comment say)? Were the repeated checks meant as real re-checks?
- **R7.** If the system was stopped cleanly earlier in the day, restarted, and then crashed, the next start is treated as a normal restart, not as crash recovery, because any clean-stop record from that day counts. SC4, the docstring and SPEC L606 to L611 state this rule; SPEC L890 can be read as a marker per run; none considers this case. Was such a crash meant to count as a crash?
- **R8.** At start-up the first call to the broker is a clock check, made with the saved login token before the token itself is checked. If the broker rejects the token, the start fails with exit code 3 under the name "clock_skew", and the service is not restarted. That a broker failure stops the start is intended. Was a rejected or expired token meant to be reported as a token problem (exit 6) rather than a clock problem (exit 3), and was the token meant to be checked before the first broker call, as two of the design documents order it?
- **R9(a) (narrowed):** The holiday guard reads the holiday file itself on purpose. When the guard's reading and the configured holiday list disagree, which one was meant to win, and was a missing or broken holiday file meant to stop the boot (as the configuration loader does) or to let it continue (as the guard does)?
- **R10 (narrowed):** The documents say the VM was to run on IST with its clock kept in time by NTP. Was it actually set up that way? (Only the actual state is open.)
- **R11:** How were weekend or evening special sessions (for example Muhurat trading) meant to be traded, given that the holiday guard stops weekend starts, starts are refused from 18:15, and the operator override that the holiday file mentions does not exist in the code?
- **R12.** Several background workers (the MIS square-off checkers and their notice self-test, the webhook clean-up worker and the optional analysis workers) start in the middle of start-up, before the last safety checks and before the start-up reconciliation. If start-up then fails, they keep running until the process ends, without the normal shutdown or its clean-stop record. The original build instruction started every worker after the reconciliation; the design specification started workers as each part was set up, but only after all validation steps. Was the later placement deliberate?
- **R13 (narrowed):** When a kill switch trips, the process keeps running: it blocks new entries and, for a hard kill, closes positions, until the day's self-exit. The original design specification says a hard kill should stop the system, and a line of the project rules ("Critical: stop system immediately", in a table of error types that does not mention the kill switch) may mean the same or may mean only that trading stops; later documents describe the kill behaviour without saying either way, and the April 2026 operating procedures and the dashboard's kill button describe a process that keeps running after a kill without saying that it must. Was a kill meant to stop the process, or to leave it running?
- **R15 (second half).** When the service is stopped from outside while an emergency close-out (a hard-kill flatten) is still running, it first sets the "stopping" signal and then waits up to 15 seconds for the close-out. Setting the signal first makes any close-out broker call that has to wait for its rate-limit slot give up. Was that interaction intended? (The 15-second wait followed by a CRITICAL message is documented as accepted.)
- **R16.** If money is added to the trading account after an early-morning start, the 09:15 capital re-sync sees the difference. With the configured thresholds a difference of 1,000 or more trips a soft kill (no new entries) and 2,500 or more a hard kill (all orders blocked and positions closed), while the same event is announced as an information message, "Capital Updated at Market Open". Was a deposit meant to be able to trip the kill ladder?
- **R17 (remaining part).** The 14:45 end-of-day warning and the 09:15 capital re-sync ignore special sessions such as Muhurat trading, and the warning leaves out partly filled trades. Were they meant to follow special-session times, and were partly filled trades meant to be listed?
- **R20.** The daily session record always stores the account as "default" and the trade type as "INTRADAY", whatever the account and the configured trade type (BOTH). The daily report shows "default"; the dashboard replaces it. Were these fixed values intended?
- **R21(b):** Were the NTP check's server and thresholds meant to be configurable, was "NTP server unreachable" meant to appear as a startup warning, and was the check meant to refuse to start above 5 seconds (FIX-129) or above 30 seconds (G4)?
- **R22 part 2 (narrowed):** Was the lock meant to stop a copy of `main.py` started by hand beside the service, given that the service runs with a private temporary directory (`PrivateTmp`), so that only the lock port, not the lock file, can refuse such a copy?
- **R23.** The check for temporary configuration markers only warns. Its own description says it should block the start when capital is above about Rs 25,000, while the tracking note says "consider promoting". Was it meant to block?
- **R24.** After the start-up reconciliation the system checks whether a kill switch was tripped and stops the start if so. The reconciler then runs a second full reconciliation when its background worker starts, and no check follows that one, so a kill tripped there lets the start finish and the service run with the kill active. Was the kill check meant to cover the second reconciliation too?

## 10. Findings for discussion with Rama

Every finding is for the post-AUDIT discussion with Rama, after ChatGPT's review (Document 04, section 5.5); none is decided here. The two lists are kept apart: (a) holds findings whose comparison with intent is established by evidence (the KNOWN DEFECTs first, then the ISSUEs classified FACT); (b) holds findings that need Rama's intent or decision, each with its question. An item can appear in both lists: its FACT part is established, and the question concerns intent.

(a) Established by evidence (KNOWN DEFECT, then ISSUE classified FACT; an effect that rests on the semantics of Python, the standard library, the kernel, SQLite or systemd is marked INTERPRETATION):

- C1: KNOWN DEFECT: `main.py --interactive` stops with `NameError` and exit 2 at L2534, after the startup checks; the April 2026 operations documents prescribe it for the first paper days and for restarts, and it could run then (the binding broke on 05-May-2026).
- C2: KNOWN DEFECT (parts (i) and (ii)): a missing, unreadable, non-JSON, non-UTF-8 or non-object live token file ends the start with exit 2, not 6; a live `accounts.csv` problem ends it with exit 1, where the code written for it gives 3 and the exit list's entry for configuration gives 5 (which of the two was meant is R2; a short row: M-33).
- C3: KNOWN DEFECT: the FIX-062 token-invalidation guard can never fire in the wired code.
- C9: KNOWN DEFECT: a present but unloadable `instruments.csv` ends the start through an assert with exit 2, not the blocking exit 3.
- C10g: KNOWN DEFECT (latent): the startup holiday check can never report a configured holiday with the object `main.py` passes (or the one its docstring names); the case arises in a boot that crosses midnight IST through a documented START-window bypass and, on a host whose time zone is not IST, in boots during the hours when the host-local date differs from the IST date. S-47 is the same finding.
- M-33: KNOWN DEFECT: `AccountRegistry.load` raises `AttributeError` for a short or whitespace-only row (exit 2 in live, with the store left open; C2's short-row case).
- S-11: KNOWN DEFECT: a configuration change seen by a boot that ends between L2308 and L2656, or by `--dry-run`, is never recorded as `CONFIG_DIFF` or alerted.
- S-39: KNOWN DEFECT (the `UnicodeDecodeError` sub-case): a strategy YAML that is not valid UTF-8 ends the start with exit 2 instead of the blocking exit 3; also, the runner has no exception isolation, and its docstring counts nine of its sixteen steps.
- C4: the declared unit's prevent list omits exit 6 (so the unit restarts it: INTERPRETATION, systemd semantics), which every source that addresses it says should stop and call the operator; exit 9 is undocumented; the docstring misdescribes 0, 1, 5 and 7; "3 4" survives in code comments and documents after the 23-Aug change; the April start limit was removed and never restored.
- C5: `--status` and `--dry-run` are not read-only (lock, migrating store open, kill auto-clears, CRASH event; `--dry-run` writes the session row and makes the next same-day boot a CRASH).
- C6: the second scenario detection and hash check run on state that the early session write has changed; the scanner skip and the hash warning cannot occur in `main.py`'s flow.
- C7a: any SHUTDOWN dated today, or a COMPLETE EOD row, makes a later start WARM, even after a crash that followed an earlier clean stop; the specifications state the rule, and SPEC L890 implies a marker per run (not settled).
- C7b: no session row gives COLD before the kill state is read; a same-day kill without a row ends the start with exit 3, not 4.
- C7c (minor): a session row dated after today is classified CRASH or WARM, not COLD; such a row cannot arise under the documented set-up.
- C7d: removed configuration files are not reported.
- C8: the first broker call (the clock check) precedes the token gate; a rejected token ends the start with exit 3 as `clock_skew`.
- C10a: the holiday decision uses the host-local date while the rest of the boot uses IST.
- C10b: a weekend passes the guard when the next trading day's year file is missing.
- C10c: a malformed holiday file exits 2 before logging and before the START window.
- C10e: the holiday Telegram message ignores the master switch and the configured names (latent at the file value).
- C11: market-open, holiday, time-parsing, token and path derivations are repeated with different rules and literals.
- C12: the required-secret list is built by convention while consumers read configured names; a missing credential gives 6, 3 or 2, never 5; the API secret is required but unread in live.
- C13: the MIS units and the sweeper start before Q4(c) and A5, and the optional workers before the reconcile; a failed start leaves them running.
- C14: the signal handlers are installed after seven to twelve threads have started; no failed start runs `_shutdown`; the April operations documents use Ctrl+C as the abort during the start.
- C15: late boot steps are unguarded; a failure there leaves the workers and the webhook server running.
- C16: the boot-order flag is set even after a failed reconcile.
- C17: the shutdown event is set before the flatten drain; throttled flatten calls can abort during the grace; the ENTRY cancellations of step 19 can abort the same way (C21a).
- C18: the fixed maximum shutdown waits (84 s, or 99 s with a flatten, at the file values) exceed the declared 30 s stop timeout, which the April operations documents also expect a stop to meet.
- C19 (with M-10): three pollers have no stop interface; the servers, the pool and the bus executors are never stopped; `main.py` joins no thread; the close covers the main thread only; the "threads joined" comment is untrue.
- C20: "reverse-order shutdown" has been untrue since the first commit; no functional hazard is established.
- C21c: two `_shutdown` steps sit outside any guard.
- C23: fixed 14:45 and 09:15; the pre-alert lists DELIVERY carries as "force-squared" and omits PARTIAL trades; unclamped sleeps (the failure callbacks' fixed texts, (e), and the daily-loss alert's claim, (g), are handed to A13, section 8).
- C24a: a process still up after midnight cannot self-exit before the next `window_end`.
- C25: worker liveness covers three workers, the feed and TGT retry; the death of many other workers has no surface; the stay-up design resolves IC-48 one way (the April operations documents and the dashboard's kill text add to the stay-up side without settling it).
- C28: the self-check's sentinel `detail` is always None; nothing detects a server failure after boot.
- C29: `EventDispatchError` informs no publisher; one duplicate kill log line is lost before the subscriptions; the docstring's EV1 and EV6 do not describe the code ((c) is handed to A04; (f) is counted in M-07).
- C30: the WARN and ALERT skew tiers are unwired; `callback_fired` misreports; the NTP check is hard-coded and its docstring misstates the warn band.
- C31: the lock port is fixed at 5001 against its docstring; logging precedes the lock; a squatted port refuses every start (INTERPRETATION, kernel bind semantics); unused and reused legacy helpers; in April, before any lock existed, the operator stopped the service before a hand run.
- C33: the session row always records 'default' and 'INTRADAY'; each write is an INSERT OR REPLACE, so `session_start` resets at each write (INTERPRETATION, SQLite semantics); the "final hash" comment is untrue.
- C34: `shutdown_logging` is never called at exit, although its docstring says it is called during shutdown; the signal handler logs through a non-reentrant queue lock (W-20); the "Graceful shutdown initiated" line that the April operations documents tell the operator to wait for does not exist, and the shutdown lines are INFO, which the terminal does not show (INTERPRETATION); a second Ctrl+C only re-runs the handler (INTERPRETATION, Python signal semantics).
- C35: special sessions are unvalidated (a malformed entry exits 2) and cannot be traded on weekends or in the evening.
- C36a: `smart_tgt.enabled` is overridden by a literal True and honoured by no component.
- C36b: `timeouts.connect_sec` is never read; the connect bound is the read value.
- C36d: private members are used across owners.
- C37: an optional-feature failure can leave partial wiring (PB-01, retest, structure exit).
- C40: import-time `load_dotenv`; a script imports `main`; unused code; stale text; `main.py` far above MAIN24's size.
- C41: no kill check follows the second synchronous reconcile inside `order_reconciler.start()`.
- B-01: the docstring's "Locked Decisions" point to register sections that do not exist and to two differing SU definitions.
- B-05: the `--resume` help text omits the START-window bypass and the unarmed self-exit.
- B-15 (with S-09): `--status` omits the open positions, daily P&L, last STARTUP and SHUTDOWN events and subsystem versions that MAIN16 lists.
- B-16: the interactive helpers differ from SU8, SU11, SU12 and the SU step order (latent); the April operations documents add a third order and a trade-type prompt that no version of the code examined has (IC-76).
- B-19: the token watcher's "Mirrors the main.py guard" has been untrue since 25-Jul.
- B-33: the guard's `next_trading_day` has no cap, unlike `MarketWindows` and P1_market_windows_api; an empty file silently gives an empty calendar; the April procedure's manual holiday check calls `is_trading_day()` without its two required arguments (HISTORICAL).
- B-34: the single-time-source claim is not met (30 `datetime.now(` lines in 23 Python files and 9 `date.today(` lines in 7, code lines only, outside the time authority).
- B-35: time-authority docstrings differ from the code (`>` against `>=`; the adapter claim; the stdlib-only claim; unused functions).
- B-36: `MarketWindows` caps at 30 days against P1_market_windows_api's 10; unused helpers (the unvalidated IST contract is the caller's by design: a MATCH).
- B-37: the market-windows module functions call `now_ist()` against the header's "no internal clock".
- B-28: the lock is released when `_main_locked` returns, before interpreter finalisation, against "held for the process lifetime".
- B-32: FIX-081 removed the webhook-port pre-check on the premise that the lock port covers it; it does not; in April the port was to be checked by hand, and the playbook reads that check the opposite way (IC-74).
- B-07: the scanner fetch uses GET, not P17's HEAD.
- B-26: logging starts after the holiday guard and the START window and before the lock, against MAIN4's and L8's "first" and the SPEC's lock-first order (IC-03).
- S-07: COLD, WARM and CRASH run one procedure, while G5a, SPEC and CT-085 specify different ones.
- S-17: `--dry-run` prints less than the full report, and constructs and calls the broker stack.
- S-38: the startup-checks module has side effects that its docstring denies.
- S-40: the crash-test list of startup checks names checks that do not exist; the April commands runbook's "14 pre-flight checks" matches neither its own date's eight steps nor the baseline's sixteen (HISTORICAL).
- S-42: the NTP check's docstring and comment are stale; it blocks at 5 s against G4's 30 s.
- S-44: a scanner outage at boot produces only log lines (the intended severity awaits a decision, (b)).
- S-45: `check_webhook_endpoint`'s comment describes an older `main.py`.
- S-46: `check_config_files_present`'s ordering claims are untrue in `main.py`'s order.
- S-51: `check_db_permissions` runs after the store open it was meant to precede.
- S-52: `check_disk_space` runs after logging and gives exit 3, against its docstring (before logging; exit 1).
- S-56: the TEMP-marker check only warns (the intent awaits R23, (b)).
- B-03: the invalidation helper's intended exit 6 is not what the next start gives (exit 2 through C2); its existence test is unguarded.
- S-22: the broker pin's comment lists an accepted value as bad; the pin runs after the Zerodha client was used.
- W-04: no kill path and no worker failure sets the shutdown event; the stay-up behaviour resolves IC-48 one way (not settled by the April operations documents or the dashboard text).
- W-10: no runtime heartbeat, against MAIN13, SPEC 4.6 and FRULES 1.6.
- W-13: the health server's start is unchecked; its failure leaves no liveness surface.
- S-25: two early returns lack `store.close()`, against F32.
- M-04: `Event.payload` is never set or read.
- M-05: the events module docstring (EV1, EV6, the "does not" bullets) does not describe the code.
- M-06: no event type has the three or more Live subscribers that G9's rationale expects.
- M-07: `InstrumentsRefreshed` is never published or subscribed in non-test code.
- M-08: a raising `OrderFilled` subscriber would leave the order watched and never re-published (A04 to confirm).
- M-11: `CapitalDriftDetected` carries four meanings (the handler's warning on every bucket-overflow event is A09's, handed off).
- M-12: EV8, EV9 and the kill-switch event docstring do not match the publishing code.
- M-13: the legacy `OrderFilled` fields are never set; the signal processor holds an unused bus.
- M-15: the ID5 validators are not used at insert time.
- M-16: conflicting broker-tag-limit statements; tag-based recovery relies on a 16-character tag.
- M-19: thirteen custom exception classes are defined outside `core/exceptions.py`, eight of them outside the tree.
- M-20: three concrete exception classes and the whole `SignalError` subtree are never raised.
- M-21: `SEVERITY` is read only by the logger; no alert routing uses it.
- M-23: several "Raised by" docstrings are stale; `ClockSkewTooLarge` is never raised at runtime.
- M-24: `MigrationError` and `MisSquareoffConfigError` reach the guard as exit 2, against EXC-1's purpose.
- M-28: unknown products fall back to "INTRADAY", "" or None, by site.
- M-29: the emergency-flatten membership is copied as SQL and local literals elsewhere.
- M-30: the constant maps NRML to DELIVERY while its comment treats NRML as unrecognised; the exit behaviour is A13's (handed off).
- M-31: stale claims about the constants' consumers; an unused import.
- M-32: three IC5 rules are not implemented: the capital-share rule (tolerance; the single-enabled-row case), the warning for an unset referenced environment variable, and resolved `api_key` and `access_token` values in `AccountRow` (AR2 holds variable names).
- M-34: duplicate account ids are not refused, against SU2's "unique, PK".
- M-35: `load()` requires exactly one primary row, but neither that it be enabled nor that any row be enabled (IC5 against SU2 and AR3).
- M-36: AR11 refuses a live boot over the paper-only value (a design concern; the letter of AR11 and SU3 is met).
- M-37: the AR12 tag caches a failed first read for the life of the process and ignores `--config`.
- M-38: the AR12b name helpers return "None" for a short primary row (messages only).
- M-39: account readers and selection rules differ across processes (latent).
- M-40: the registry docstring's "used by ZerodhaAdapter" is stale.
- B-39: `ist_now.sh` hard-codes an absolute VM interpreter path against FRULES 1.4.

(b) Needing Rama's intent or decision (OPEN FOR RAMA; the questions are worded for Rama in section 9):

- C1 (R1, narrowed; bears on impact, not on the classification): after May 2026, was `main.py --interactive` still used, for live trading or for the recovery steps in `docs/disaster_recovery.md`? (Its planned use for the first paper days of April 2026 is answered by the operations documents.)
- C2, the L2374 exit (IC-40; with R4): was a missing credential variable at a live start meant to give exit 5 (docstring, M41C) or 6 (the code, FIX-163)?
- C2 part (ii) (R2, narrowed): was a missing or invalid `accounts.csv` at a live start meant to give exit 3 (the code written for it; IC13) or exit 5 (the exit list's entry for invalid configuration)?
- C4: Besides exit 6, were exit 9 and the failures that repeat on every restart meant to be restarted automatically, and was the April start limit meant to remain (R4, narrowed)?
- C5: Were `--status` and `--dry-run` meant to be usable while the service is running (R5 part 3)?
- C6 (R6): was the scanner check meant to run on the first start of the day, and were the repeated detection and hash checks meant as re-checks?
- C7a (R7): was a crash after an earlier same-day clean stop meant to count as a crash?
- C8 (R8): was a rejected token meant to be reported as a token problem with exit 6, and checked before the first broker call?
- C10c, C11 (R9(a), narrowed): when the holiday guard's own reading of the holiday file and the configured holiday list disagree, which was meant to win, and was a missing or broken holiday file meant to stop the boot or to let it continue?
- C13 (R12): was starting the MIS units and the optional workers before the last guards and the reconcile, and leaving them running after a failed start, deliberate?
- C17 (R15, second half): was the event-first rule meant to apply to the flatten's own broker calls during the grace?
- C22 (R16, with the handoff to A09): was a deposit after a pre-market start meant to be able to trip the drift kill ladder at 09:15?
- C23 (R17, remaining part): were the helper threads meant to follow special sessions, and was the pre-alert meant to list PARTIAL trades?
- C25, W-04 (R13, narrowed): was a kill meant to stop the process, or to leave it running until the self-exit?
- C30: Were the NTP server and thresholds meant to be configurable, was "unreachable" meant to be a startup warning, and was the check meant to refuse above 5 seconds or above 30 seconds (R21(b))?
- C31: Was the lock meant to stop a hand-run copy beside the service despite `PrivateTmp` (R22 part 2)?
- C33 (R20): were the session-row literals 'default' and 'INTRADAY' intended?
- C35: How were weekend or evening special sessions meant to be traded (R11)?
- C41 (R24): was the kill check meant to cover the second synchronous reconcile?
- B-41, C10a (R10, narrowed): was the VM actually set to IST and kept in time by NTP?
- B-26 (no STUDY R number): was the first step at process start meant to be the logger (MAIN4), the holiday guard (SU6) or the lock (SPEC)?
- S-44 (no STUDY R number): was a scanner outage at boot meant to be a warning (SC12) or CRITICAL (P17, SPEC C.1), with an alarm (the 27-Jul rule)?
- S-56 (R23): was the TEMP-marker check meant to block above about Rs 25,000 of capital?
- M-35: Was a primary account marked disabled meant to be refused, or traded by the headless service, and was at least one account meant to be enabled (IC5 against SU2, AR3 and the June test)?

ISSUEs classified INTERPRETATION only, and so in neither list: C32 (a design concern; its step timeouts are FACT, the total is INTERPRETATION). C34 holds W-20's INTERPRETATION-only point.

## 12. Coverage gaps

What this AUDIT could not establish, and why: either the runtime evidence does not survive (Document 06, section 4.2), or the point needs Rama's intent (section 10(b)), or it belongs to another topic.

- **Runtime, UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED** (no production VM, log, journal, database or crontab survives): which exit codes occurred and whether restart loops followed after 18-Jun-2026; whether the unit and the watcher were installed, and the systemd version (B-40); the VM's time zone, NTP state and Python version (B-41); whether `--interactive`, `--status`, `--dry-run` or `--resume` were run after 05-May-2026; whether WARN- or ALERT-band skews occurred; whether the Telegram switch was ever off; whether log records were lost at exit; whether a second copy ran while a finishing process had released the lock; whether any event handler raised or an order stayed watched (M-08); whether an NRML position ever existed (M-30); the VM's `accounts.csv`, environment and configuration in force (C12, M-33 to M-39); whether production ran `42c07f3` and the consumer commits after `7d4970a` (M-41); whether `check_tz.sh`, `ist_now.sh` or `deploy_preflight.py` were ever run.
- **Not traced** (out of scope or owned elsewhere): the paper-only `_make_paper_quote_provider` (L473 to L610) and the paper branch of `_build_market_data_kite` (L406 to L418); the Windows lock branch beyond its documentation (development PC only); the internals of the kill-switch clears, migration on open, the login flow, alert delivery, the order monitor and the drift handler (A13, A03, A04, A14, A09), read at the boundary only; the third-party `waitress` and `kiteconnect` libraries (pinned, not in the repository).
- **Documents not read:** of the binary documents under `docs/`, 31 of the 34 PNG dashboard captures, which were checked by name and through their three index documents and show no A01 startup, shutdown or approval procedure (the three opened state none either; evidence file, section 6.3); the three `.docx` operations documents are no longer a gap: they were read in full on 05-Nov-2026 (section 3.2; evidence file, sections 1.1 and 6.3); `docs/archive/` beyond search hits; the full bodies of the long registers (`docs/SYSTEM_MAP.md`, `docs/MASTER_PENDING_01-Aug-2026.md`, `docs/audit/integrity_audit_2026.md`, `UNPUSHED_LEDGER.md`, `docs/campaign_practices.md`) beyond the passages cited (evidence file, section 6.3).
- **Not found in the repository:** the `main_module` and `startup_module` register sections that the build instructions name (B-01); the deploy record that commit `2f67bb8` cites; a commit subject introducing FIX-081.
- **Tests:** read as documents only; none was run. The `main.py` test harness patches the lock, the store, the kill switch, the time authority and the holiday guard, so no test exercises the diagnostic side effects (C5), the late lock release (B-28), the interactive `NameError` (C1) or the holiday check with the object `main.py` passes (C10g); the X5 lock tests use dedicated ports, not a squatted 5001 (C31).
- **Broker behaviour that the code cannot show** (A04): the broker's real tag-length limit (M-16); whether the broker rejects a stale token at the first call (C4 row 6; C8).
- Runtime facts cannot be established, because the production VMs, their databases, logs, journals and crontab are gone: the token file's state at any boot; whether `-O` or `PYTHONOPTIMIZE` was used; the Python, systemd and library versions; whether the declared unit was installed; the boot and shutdown durations; whether any restart loop, SIGKILL, lost `CONFIG_DIFF` alert, failed reconcile, mid-boot MIS action, deposit-driven kill, worker death or broker authentication failure occurred; the configuration values in force.
- Third-party behaviour (waitress, kiteconnect, requests) is outside the repository: the server and ticker threads (W-24), the client's timeout handling (C36b) and concurrent use of one client (C38a).
- Owner internals were not audited, by scope: A02 (schemas, the hashed set, the W0 snapshot), A03 (the per-thread close, the checkpoint, SPEC X.4's transaction), A04 (the token lifecycle, the Date-header hook, the limiter, the order monitor's self-stop), A05 (the instrument cache's exceptions, the feed), A06 (the sweeper, the webhook server, SP4), A08 and A18 (the optional workers), A09 (the drift ladder), A10 (the placer's `enabled` check), A11, A12 (the MIS and EOD pollers, what the second reconcile can change), A13 (the flatten loop under `RateLimitAbortedError`, the kill type for an authentication failure, the failure callbacks' texts of C23 (e) and (g)), A14 (alert texts and delivery), A15 (`/health`, the logger), A17 (the unit), A20 (the pre-flight hook and script).
- The runtime part did not check whether the S&R detector, V3 runner, PB-01 capture, zone warmer and retest monitor loops wait on a stop event (only their join bounds were read).
- The webhook server's `threads=8` and `connection_limit=100` (L3994 to L3995), which neither part covered, were checked at assembly (C36e: MATCH with the 24-Apr fix direction); whether the values suited the production load, and the waitress library's own behaviour, are UNKNOWN / NOT ESTABLISHED.
- The existing records describe the testing copy at a commit not in the clone; their claims are HISTORICAL.
- Rama's intent: the questions retained as OPEN QUESTION (section 9) and the items of section 10(b) cannot be settled from the documents.

### Notes

Changes made when the two fragments were assembled, when the AUDIT was corrected after four independent checks, after a second-pass check of those corrections, at the follow-up on the operations documents (note 13) and at the start-limit check (note 14), with the reasons. Nothing here changes the STUDY.

1. The KNOWN DEFECT rule of section 1.6 (four conditions, applied as written, with condition (c) read as section 1.6 states it) replaced the two fragments' slightly different readings and was applied to every proposed or considered KNOWN DEFECT:
   - KNOWN DEFECT, with the conditions stated in each record: C1 part (a); C2 parts (i) and (ii) (part (ii) since the second-pass check, note 12); C3; C9; C10g (latent); M-33 (on the raise contract alone); S-11; S-39 (the `UnicodeDecodeError` sub-case).
   - ISSUE, with the failing condition stated in the record: C7c ((c): no documented option writes a later date); M-38 ((d): the contract's stated purpose is preserved); C10e ((a): no mechanism for the switch).
   - Considered and kept as ISSUE: C4 ((b): the outcome depends on an installed supervisor); C5 and C10b ((d): the code's own sources support the outcome); C6 ((b) and (d): no failure follows, and the sources on the skip conflict); C7b ((d): the sources conflict); C14 ((b): timing); C19 ((a) and (b): absences and a comment mismatch; timing); C10c and C35's malformed entry ((a): no handler exists; for C35's sessions also (d): the sources conflict); C30 ((a): the tiers are unwired); C34 ((a): the call is missing); C28's `detail` read ((d): the code's `getattr` default admits None); C36a ((d): BL-7b supports the A01 line); C36b ((a): no reader exists); C7d ((c): reachability depends on A02); C24a ((c) and (d): no source states the behaviour after midnight); C23 parts (a) and (b) ((d)), (c) ((c): unreachable in `main.py`'s flow), (d) ((b): timing) and (f) ((a)), with parts (e) and (g) handed off to A13; S-25 ((b): no failure follows); M-24 ((d): the guard maps classes as its exit list states, and the configuration case fails closed); B-03 (it records the wiring of C2 and C3, which hold the defects).
   - Not classified by A01: C38b (HANDED OFF: certain whenever the script runs, but the script is not an A01 path).
   - Duplicates treated as one finding with one classification: C2's short-row case and M-33 (counted under M-33); S-47 and C10g (counted under C10g).
2. 5. The token watcher: the corrected statement is used throughout. `deploy/token_watcher.sh` does nothing while the unit is active or activating and acts only on a failed or inactive unit (L140 to L144, L144 to L188). Under the declared unit it therefore retries, within the same day, exit 5 only (through its catch-all branch, up to 3 starts an hour with a fresh token); exits 3 and 4 are not restarted the same day; exits 1, 2, 6 and 9 are restarted by systemd itself, so the watcher never sees them. A worksheet statement that the watcher retries 5, 6 and 9 was corrected.

The startup fragment's own conflict numbers and the intent part's numbers were mapped through the evidence file; four conflicts or second sources with no IC entry, and one new intent source, are listed in section 3.4 as additions. Where a fragment cited the intent part's IS-5.17, the evidence file's number is IS-5.20.
